# Wyzesales Rebuild — Decisions & Scope

A running record of what's been decided for the Flutter + Supabase + Netlify rebuild of Wyzesales, consolidated from the design discussion so far. Companion to `WyzesalesDailyLoad_Analysis.md`, `Wyzesales_Screens_and_Recommendations.md`, and `Wyzesales_Forecast_Redesign.md`.

---

## 1. Architecture

- **Stack**: Flutter (mobile, tablet, and desktop from one codebase) + Supabase (Postgres, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Cron) + Netlify (hosting the Flutter web build). Same stack as the existing SeaWyze app.
- **WyzeSalesExtract becomes a plain data extract.** It pulls raw facts and reference data from the WCSA IQRetail database via ODBC and loads them into Supabase — no aggregation, no business rules, no forecasting inside the .exe. This removes the need to involve WCSA's IT/security team for every future logic change.
- **All aggregation and business logic moves to Supabase**, split between:
  - **SQL views / materialized views** for stateless, single-pass logic: fiscal-year date math, the `ConsolidatedSales`-style rollups, the profit-percentage calculation, and the location-code remap (as a small reference table, not hardcoded IF/ELSEIF).
  - **Supabase Edge Functions** for stateful or multi-branch logic: the seasonal forecast (see Section 4) and any validation/alerting logic.
- **Business-rule configuration** (excluded customer accounts, the sales-rep override list, the supplier suppression list) moves into editable Supabase tables rather than a config file on the Windows server — changeable by Craig directly, no redeploy.
- **Multi-tenant from day one.** Even though only WCSA exists today, the schema carries a client/tenant identifier throughout, so adding another Wyzelink client later doesn't require a schema rework.
- **Data migration**: not needed, resolved 2026-08-21. WCSA's live database has more than 3 years of history, but it was a deliberate business decision early in Wyzesales' development to only ever look at the last 3 years — older data was agreed to be irrelevant and distorting. Since the daily-load pipeline (both the text-file extract and the Xojo database it feeds) has therefore never held more than that same rolling 3-year window at any point, there's no older data anywhere to recover. The first run of the rebuilt extractor against Supabase naturally reconstructs the same 3-year window the current app already shows — that first run *is* the migration, not a separate project. Budget figures (the one thing the daily extract never touches, so it couldn't have regenerated anyway) also need no migration: confirmed those are all still zero, since WCSA hasn't started using that feature yet.
- **Cutover plan**: run the new app in parallel with the current Wyzesales for a period, comparing numbers, before WCSA fully switches over — consistent with how carefully everything else in this project has been validated.

---

## 2. Bugs confirmed as fixes, not preserved quirks

- **Stale branch/location on Quotes and Sales Orders.** The current daily-load script never looks up the correct branch for these two files — every row silently inherits whatever branch was left over from the last Sales Analysis row processed. The rebuild resolves branch correctly and consistently for every dataset (Transaction, Sales Analysis, Quotes, Sales Orders alike), directly from each transaction line's own warehouse code (corrected 2026-08-21 — an earlier draft tried to derive it from the selling rep's "assigned branch" instead, which turned out not to exist anywhere in WCSA's actual data; see `Wyzesales_Schema_DesignNotes.md` Section 7).
- **Seasonal-forecast averaging bug.** The old forecast always checked one specific month (February) to decide whether to average against a prior year or not, regardless of which month was actually being forecast. Superseded entirely by the Holt-Winters redesign (Section 4), which doesn't have this failure mode.
- **Year 1/Year 2 profit swap on Sales Analysis and Sales by [Dimension]** (found while building the rollup views — see `Wyzesales_Rollups_DesignNotes.md` Section 2, not yet sanity-checked with you). `SalesAnalysisBuilder.cs` deliberately preserves a copy/paste bug from the original script: the profit figures shown for "Year 1" and "Year 2" belong to each other's periods (Year 3 and all value/quantity figures are unaffected). Treated the same as the two bugs above — fixed, not preserved, in `v_dimension_monthly_sales`. Worth comparing last year's/the year-before's profit numbers between old and new once you can, since they'll now differ from what you're used to seeing.

---

## 3. Sales rep attribution

WCSA's database already has two distinct facts for every sale: the rep recorded on the invoice itself, and the rep assigned to that customer in the debtor master. Today's system (already ported into WyzeSalesExtract) defaults to the invoice's rep, except for a hardcoded list of ~11 "override" accounts where the customer's assigned rep takes precedence instead.

**Decision**: preserve this behavior exactly, but generalize the mechanism —

- Every transaction stores **both** the invoice's rep and the customer's assigned rep as separate fields, not just for the override accounts.
- The override list becomes a flag on the customer record ("attribute this customer's sales to their assigned rep") — editable directly by Craig or WCSA staff, not a hardcoded array requiring a code change.
- **Row-level security (who can see a row)** uses a union rule: a user can see a transaction if their rep code matches *either* the invoice's rep or the customer's assigned rep — so nobody's own legitimate work is ever hidden from them.
- **Reporting/attribution (whose number it counts toward)** uses the single resolved value from the override rule above, feeding Dashboard, Performance, and Budget numbers.

---

## 4. Forecast redesign

Full detail in `Wyzesales_Forecast_Redesign.md`. Summary: replaces the old two-year seasonal-ratio comparison with **Holt-Winters triple exponential smoothing** — tracks level, trend, and seasonality separately, uses all available history with more weight on recent months. Three-tier confidence fallback based on how much history an entity has (24+ months = full method, 12–23 = partial, under 12 = simple trend projection, each tagged with a confidence level). Smoothing parameters and thresholds live in a Supabase settings table, tunable without a redeploy. Budget figures and forecast figures live in separate tables from the start, eliminating the old backup/restore dance entirely. To be validated via backtesting against real historical data before cutover.

---

## 5. User roles and access

Four tiers, confirmed from the current User Management screen:

| Role | Access |
|---|---|
| **User** | Own transactions only (per the union rule in Section 3) |
| **RegUser** | All transactions for their assigned branch/region |
| **AdminUser** | Full access to all WCSA data |
| **SuperUser** | Full access, plus exclusive access to the User Management screen — Craig only |

User accounts are created directly (Craig sets up the account and hands over login credentials out of band), and the user can change their own password after logging in — no self-service email invite flow needed.

**Budgets screens — Admin/SuperUser only (2026-08-21).** Craig asked that User/RegUser accounts not see the Budgets screens at all, not just be blocked from editing them. Implemented at the Flutter app layer, in two places: `app_shell.dart` hides the "Budgets" nav section entirely unless `profile.canEditBudgets` (adminuser/superuser); `budgets_screen.dart` itself now shows a plain access-denied message instead of the entity picker/grid for anyone else, which is the actual enforcement point — the nav gate alone wouldn't stop a direct/bookmarked URL to `/budgets/:dimension`, since the router has no role-based route guard. Deliberately left `budget_figures`' own RLS SELECT policy (schema/001, readable by any profile for the client) untouched, per Craig's explicit answer that the Performance screen's "R Target"/"% Target" figures — sourced from the same table via `v_dimension_performance` — should keep showing to everyone. That means this is a UI-layer restriction only: a User/RegUser can't reach Budgets through the app, but the database itself doesn't block them from querying `budget_figures` directly (e.g. via the Supabase REST API with their own token) the way it would if the RLS policy were tightened. Worth knowing, not currently seen as a problem since it matches what was asked.

Each user record carries: name, email, contact number, level (one of the four above), rep code, and branch code — matching the existing Add/Edit/Delete Users screen.

**Role model changed under multi-tenancy (2026-08-25) — see Section 9.** SuperUser as a distinct top tier is retired: schema/008's role migration moves Craig's own account (the only row that had it) to AdminUser, and every AdminUser can now add/edit/delete users, not just a single top-tier account — Craig's own decision, "essentially super user should fall away and be replaced with administer-platform... all adminuser's should be able to add, delete and edit users." Cross-tenant reach (seeing/managing every client, not just your own) is a separate flag entirely, `is_platform_admin`, covered in Section 9 — it's not a fifth role level, and it's orthogonal to AdminUser/RegUser/User. `superuser` stays in the `user_level` Postgres enum as an unused legacy value (can't be cleanly dropped without recreating the type) but nothing is assigned it going forward. The table above is otherwise unchanged — User/RegUser/AdminUser access rules are exactly as documented.

## 6. UI / UX

- **Visual language matches SeaWyze**: dark sidebar navigation, light content area, card-based KPI tiles, colour-tinted quick-action tiles, progress-ring style charts, light/dark mode toggle. SeaWyze is also built in Flutter — a shared theme/component package between the two apps is possible once there's access to the SeaWyze project (pending: repo access or an export).
- **Responsive across mobile, tablet, and desktop.** The sidebar-heavy desktop layout will need an adapted navigation pattern on mobile (e.g. bottom tab bar or drawer) rather than a literal shrink of the desktop layout — same colours and components, different structure.
- **Light and dark mode**, matching SeaWyze.
- **New WyzeSales logo/wordmark**, in the same family as SeaWyze's branding — to be designed later, not blocking the current phase.
- **Mobile**: always-online assumption, no offline caching required.

**Visual redesign to match SeaWyze, first pass (2026-08-21).** Craig sent real screenshots of SeaWyze's Login and Dashboard screens asking WyzeSales to look as polished. Agreed rollout: shell/theme + Login + Dashboard first (this pass), the other 12 screens once Craig's confirmed the look. Changes:
  - `AppShell` rebuilt around a permanent dark sidebar (was a slide-out hamburger drawer) — logo top, nav list middle with active-route highlighting, user profile + sign-out moved to the bottom (was in the top app bar). Collapses back to a drawer under 900px width for mobile, same sidebar content either way. The top strip above the content area is light (matching the screenshot — only the sidebar itself is dark), showing the screen title, today's date, and the theme toggle.
  - Nav active-highlighting takes an optional `currentRoute` param on `AppShell` rather than reading it back out of go_router's context — only `DashboardScreen` passes it so far; the other screens still compile and work, they just won't highlight their own sidebar entry until updated in the next pass.
  - New `AppLogo` widget (circle-outlined chart icon + "Wyze"/"Sales" two-tone wordmark, mirroring SeaWyze's own "Sea"/"Wyze" split) used on both the sidebar and Login screen — still a placeholder, a real logo is separately flagged as not-yet-designed (see above).
  - Login screen background changed from navy to the same light gray-blue as the rest of the app, matching Craig's reference; labels moved above each field (was Material's floating label style); added a password show/hide toggle; footer version/copyright line, all matching the screenshot's layout.
  - Dashboard gained a 5-tile KPI row (Sales MTD/YTD, Gross Profit MTD/YTD — sourced from `v_consolidated_sales`, the same whole-company rollup Sales Analysis' Graph tab uses; colour flips red if a figure is ever negative — plus Last Updated) and a quick-actions row (shortcuts to Sales Analysis / YTD Comparative / Quote Analysis / Sales Order Analysis / Budgets-if-permitted). Deliberately did NOT copy SeaWyze's work-order-specific tiles (Overdue/Due soon/Open work orders, the Planned Maintenance Compliance ring, "Attention required") since those concepts have no WyzeSales equivalent — inventing fake metrics to match the screenshot literally would be worse than a close-but-honest visual match. The existing top-5-by-dimension bar chart section is unchanged data, just restyled.
  - New shared widgets: `StatCard` (KPI tile), `QuickActionTile` (colour-tinted shortcut), `AppLogo` — all under `lib/shared/widgets/`, reusable when the other screens get this treatment.
  - Verified via the same tree-sitter syntax check as every other change in this project (see `WyzeSales_Flutter_App_DesignNotes.md`); a couple of icon names were deliberately chosen to match ones already proven to compile elsewhere in this codebase rather than guessed, since there's no real Dart compiler here to check an unfamiliar icon actually exists.

**Second polish pass (2026-08-21), from Craig's annotated side-by-side screenshots:**
  - Shrunk the `TextTheme` again — headlineSmall/titleLarge/titleMedium (page titles, section headers, card titles) came down further, matched against a real SeaWyze form label Craig screenshotted. The Dashboard's KPI numbers deliberately did NOT shrink with them (`StatCard` now reads `headlineMedium` instead of `headlineSmall`) — those are meant to stay bold/prominent, same as SeaWyze's own.
  - `SegmentedButtonThemeData` and `SwitchThemeData` added, both pinned to teal for the selected state — Material3's default (a muted secondaryContainer tint) wasn't reading as the app's actual brand teal.
  - `AppShell`'s top bar background changed from the scaffold's gray-blue to white (`colorScheme.surface`) — only the sidebar is meant to be dark, matching SeaWyze.
  - Top bar's date and icon buttons (menu, theme toggle) now sit inside rounded gray "chip" backgrounds, matching SeaWyze's pill-style icon buttons — previously plain unstyled icons/text.
  - New `BoxedDropdown` shared widget (`lib/shared/widgets/boxed_dropdown.dart`): every dimension/sort/year/month switcher on Sales By, Budgets, and Performance was a bare `DropdownButton` with no border or background — Craig circled this directly, comparing against SeaWyze's bordered white dropdown boxes. Swapped all 6 usages across those 3 screens for `BoxedDropdown`, which wraps `DropdownButtonFormField` so it picks up the same `InputDecorationTheme` box every text field already uses.
  - `Sales By`, `Budgets`, and `Performance` now pass `currentRoute` into `AppShell` so their own sidebar entry highlights correctly — previously only the Dashboard did, so navigating to any other screen showed no active highlight at all, a visible gap next to SeaWyze's clearly-highlighted active item.
  - This pass touched Sales By/Budgets/Performance's dropdown+route-highlight only, not their full layout — the broader sidebar/top-bar/card treatment is still pending confirmation before extending to all 12 screens per the original rollout agreement.

**Third polish pass (2026-08-21), sidebar footer + quick actions:**
  - Sidebar's `_ProfileFooter` gained a version/copyright line beneath the profile row — "WyzeSales v0.1 · © 2026 WyzeSales" (matching the Login screen's existing footer wording verbatim), small and muted, centred. SeaWyze's own sidebar carries the same pattern under its profile row; WyzeSales' didn't have one at all until now.
  - Sidebar nav item text (`_NavTile`) dropped from a hardcoded 14px to 13px. This was the one piece of app text that had never been touched by either of the two earlier font-shrink passes — it was a hardcoded literal `TextStyle`, never tied to the app's `textTheme` — so it's the most likely explanation for Craig continuing to see a font-size mismatch against SeaWyze in these latest side-by-side sidebar screenshots specifically, even after two rounds of theme-wide shrinking elsewhere.

The icon also moved into its own smaller rounded colour badge (38×38, more saturated tint) rather than sitting bare on the tile, and the tile's own background tint was lightened for a more uniform pastel look — both matching SeaWyze's presentation.
  - Verified via the tree-sitter syntax check as usual; pushed to Craig's Mac via the direct heredoc-write pattern and confirmed byte-identical via `device_stage_files` + `diff`.
  - Flagged back to Craig as a best-effort guess on the font-size point rather than a confirmed fix — his last two messages both raised "font sizes still look different" without pinpointing which text, so if the sidebar text wasn't what he meant, this needs another annotated screenshot to pin down.

Craig confirmed the sidebar nav item text was indeed what he meant by the font-size complaint, resolving that thread. Same message raised two structural nav changes:

**Sidebar nav restructure — collapsed dimension tiles, Budgets moved under Settings (2026-08-22).** Craig pointed out that listing 5 nav tiles under each of "Sales By"/"Budgets"/"Performance" (one per dimension: Sales Person, Category, Customer, Item, Branch) was pointless, since all 5 open the same parameterized screen and that screen already has its own dimension-switcher dropdown (`BoxedDropdown` on each of `sales_by_screen.dart`/`budgets_screen.dart`/`performance_screen.dart`). Changes in `app_shell.dart`:
  - "Sales By" and "Performance" collapsed from 5 tiles + section header down to a single tile each (opening on the first dimension, Sales Person), with no section header — same bare-tile pattern the Dashboard entry already used.

Chosen as a general-purpose home for admin-only screens, not just Budgets, since the still-unbuilt User Management screen (Section 7) would also belong there once it exists.
  - `_NavTile` gained an optional `activePrefix` param: since a collapsed tile's `route` only points at the first dimension, active-highlighting now matches on a route *prefix* (`/sales-by/`, `/performance/`, `/budgets/`) so the tile still highlights correctly while viewing any of the other 4 dimensions, not just the default one. The plain per-screen tiles (Dashboard, Sales Analysis, etc.) keep exact-match highlighting, unaffected.
  - Verified via tree-sitter, pushed via the same heredoc pattern, confirmed byte-identical via `device_stage_files` + `diff`.

**Sales Analysis tab divider + filter field labels (2026-08-22).** Craig circled two more things:
  - The black line under Sales Analysis' Graph/Table tab bar was Material3's default full-width `TabBar` divider (separate from the thin teal underline beneath the selected tab, which was already correct) — the only `TabBar` in the app, so fixed directly at that one call site with `TabBar(dividerColor: Colors.transparent, ...)` rather than a global theme change.
  - The Year/Category/Item/Sales Person/Branch/Customer filter fields on Sales Analysis' Table tab, Quote Analysis, and Sales Order Analysis were still using `DropdownButtonFormField`'s built-in floating `labelText` — the same small/thin Material floating-label look Craig flagged and moved away from on the Login screen in the very first redesign pass, just never carried over to these filter rows. `BoxedDropdown` gained an optional `label` parameter (Text at `labelLarge` + 6px gap above the box, matching the Login field-label pattern exactly) and both `filter_bar.dart`'s `_FilterDropdown` and the inline Year dropdown in `document_analysis_view.dart` now use it. Left `label` unset on the Sales By/Budgets/Performance dimension switchers, which don't need a persistent label.
  - Verified via tree-sitter, pushed via heredoc, confirmed byte-identical.

**Platform Admin screen — structural rebuild to match SeaWyze exactly (2026-08-25).** Craig sent two screenshots of SeaWyze's actual live Platform Admin screen and said (for at least the second time) that WyzeSales' UI was still far off, asking for it to look and feel exactly the same. Unlike the earlier passes above (theme tokens, font sizes, dropdown boxes), this one was structural: `platform_admin_screen.dart`'s `TabBar`/`TabBarView` shell and plain `DataTable` rows didn't exist in SeaWyze's version at all — SeaWyze uses a nested left-nav *inside* the screen body plus hand-rolled card/table widgets. Rebuilt `platform_admin_screen.dart` from scratch, porting SeaWyze's actual widget structure (read in full from the SeaWyze reference project) rather than approximating from the screenshots alone:
  - Stateful nested left-nav (`_selectedNav`/`_navItem`/`_navSection`) replaces `DefaultTabController`/`TabBar` — grouped OVERVIEW (Clients, Licenses) and CONFIGURATION (Pricing) section labels, teal active-item pill highlighting, and a small "Platform admin only" identity pill at the top (SeaWyze's own pill reads "SeaWyze only"). Collapses to a horizontal scrollable nav strip under 600px width, same as SeaWyze's mobile fallback.
  - New `_Card` (bordered container, uppercase title + optional top-right action button), `_StatCard` (big bold value + small gray label), `_HeaderCell`, `_LicenseBadge`, `_IconBtn`, `_PrimaryBtn`, `_PriceField`, and dialog helpers (`_dialogHeader`/`_dialogFooter`/`_tf`) — all ported directly from SeaWyze's own private widget classes in the same file, using WyzeSales' existing `AppColors` (SeaWyze's `overdue`/`dueSoon`/`normal` semantic names map to WyzeSales' `negative`/`caution`/`positive`).
  - Clients tab gained a 4-tile stat row (Total clients, Active licenses, Expiring in 30 days, Annual recurring — in Rand via the existing `formatRand`, not SeaWyze's €) above a `_Card`-wrapped client table with a teal "+ Add client" button and custom `Container`-built rows (name + code, plan, users, a coloured license-status badge) in place of `DataTable`.
  - Licenses tab: same `_Card`/custom-row treatment, columns unchanged (Client, Users, Discount %, Annual price, Expiry, Status, edit icon) — only the presentation changed, not what's editable.
  - Pricing tab: restyled from a bare `ListView`/`ListTile` list to a `_Card` with a teal "+ Add plan" button and custom rows; an inactive plan now shows a small "Inactive" badge, matching the badge-driven look used elsewhere on this screen.
  - Add client / Edit license / Edit plan dialogs rebuilt on SeaWyze's own dialog shell (`_dialogHeader`/`_tf`/`_PriceField`/`_dialogFooter` inside a plain `Dialog`, not `AlertDialog`) for visual consistency with the rest of the screen — same fields, same validation, same repository calls as before; this was a presentational change only, not a functional one.
  - New shared util `lib/shared/utils/responsive.dart` (`dialogMaxHeight`/`dialogInsetPadding`), ported from SeaWyze's identical helper — keeps these new dialogs well-behaved on short/mobile viewports the same way SeaWyze's do.
  - Deliberately did not add SeaWyze features WyzeSales has no backing for: no Notifications tab (platform-wide banner — a separate SeaWyze feature, not part of this ask), no per-client "dormant" toggle (no such column/concept exists in WyzeSales' schema), no vessel-shaped fields anywhere (a WyzeSales client is users only, per the original multi-tenancy design).
  - Verified via the tree-sitter syntax check (0 issues across all 47 lib files), pushed to Craig's Mac via the zip → `SendUserFile` → `device_commit_files` → `unzip` → `cp` pattern, confirmed byte-identical via `md5sum` on both the device and sandbox copies.
  - Settings screen (`settings_screen.dart`) still uses the older plain-Material look and is the logical next target for the same treatment, flagged here rather than started in this pass so this rebuild could be confirmed against SeaWyze first.

Craig confirmed "No issues" on `flutter analyze` for the Platform Admin rebuild and asked to go ahead with Settings too ("Yes please").

**Settings screen — same structural rebuild, same pass (2026-08-25).** Read SeaWyze's actual `settings_screen.dart` in full (its Company/Users/License tabs — skipped the Vessels tab and its dialogs entirely, no WyzeSales equivalent) and rebuilt WyzeSales' `settings_screen.dart` on the same pattern as Platform Admin above:
  - Nested left-nav inside the screen body replaces `DefaultTabController`/`TabBar`, under a single "Account" section label (SeaWyze also has a "Fleet" section for its Vessels tab — dropped, no equivalent). Same mobile collapse-to-horizontal-strip behaviour under 600px as Platform Admin.
  - New shared helpers ported from SeaWyze's file — `_card` (title/subtitle/badge/action header), `_twoCol` (responsive info-grid), `_infoRow` (boxed label+value), `_warningBanner`, `_primaryBtn`/`_outlineBtn`, and the same `_dialogHeader`/`_dialogFooterWithLoading`/`_tf` dialog shell used on Platform Admin — all local to this file (Dart privacy is per-file, so having the same private helper names in both screen files is not a conflict).
  - Company tab: client name/code shown in boxed `_infoRow`s inside a `_card`, "Rename" as a teal `_primaryBtn` in the card header (was a plain `OutlinedButton`); the rename dialog itself moved off `AlertDialog` onto the shared dialog shell. WyzeSales' `Client` model only has code/name (no address/contact/documents fields like SeaWyze's `CompanyModel`), so the card stayed to just those two fields — no invented fields.
  - Users tab: card header now shows a live "X of Y used" seat-count subtitle and disables "Add user" (plus shows a caution banner) at the seat limit — both read-only additions using the license data this tab already had reason to fetch, no new write behaviour. Each user row restyled to SeaWyze's look: initials avatar (teal-tinted for Admin), name/email column, a level badge, a "Support"/"Inactive" badge where relevant, a small active-status dot, and the existing pause/resume + delete actions restyled as icon buttons — no new actions added (WyzeSales has no "Edit user" dialog in SeaWyze's sense; kept it that way rather than inventing one).
  - License tab: three stat tiles (Users used, Days remaining, Annual price — WyzeSales has no vessels, so SeaWyze's 4-tile row with a vessel tile became 3), a detail-row block (start/expiry/status/discount) plus a pricing-breakdown box (base plan line, additional-users line, monthly + annual totals — both kept, since WyzeSales' `License.discountedMonthly` and stored `annualPrice` are both meaningful figures the old tab already surfaced), and the same teal-tinted "Request upgrade" box design, wired to the existing `requestUpgrade()` call.
  - Before sending: changed several `if (mounted) Navigator.of(context)...`/`ScaffoldMessenger.of(context)...` guards to `context.mounted`, believing that was the fix the earlier `flutter analyze` round had established. Also applied the same change to 3 spots in the just-delivered `platform_admin_screen.dart` that had been missed in that pass. **This was wrong** — see the correction directly below.
  - Verified via tree-sitter (0 issues across all 47 lib files), pushed to Craig's Mac via the same zip → `SendUserFile` → `device_commit_files` → `unzip` → `cp` pattern, confirmed byte-identical via `md5sum`.

**Correction — `mounted` vs `context.mounted` is not one-size-fits-all (2026-08-25).** Craig's next `flutter analyze` came back with 9 issues: the same `use_build_context_synchronously` lint, but now firing on the very lines just "fixed" to `context.mounted` (3 in `platform_admin_screen.dart`, 5 in `settings_screen.dart`), plus a new `unnecessary_non_null_assertion` warning. The lint's own message spells out the actual rule, which is more precise than "always use `context.mounted`": *"Guard a `State.context` use with a `mounted` check on the State, and other BuildContext use with a `mounted` check on the BuildContext."* In other words it depends on what kind of `context` is being used at that call site:
  - Inside a `State` subclass's own method, where `context` resolves via the inherited `State.context` getter with no local `context` parameter shadowing it (e.g. a dialog's plain `_save()` method) — guard with the bare `mounted` (the State's own getter). This is what all the "fixed" spots above actually were, and why changing them to `context.mounted` introduced new findings instead of clearing them.
  - Where `context` is a distinct local variable or parameter — a nested builder callback's `context`, or a `BuildContext` passed explicitly into a plain (non-State) function/method, like `_UserRow._confirmDelete(BuildContext context, WidgetRef ref)` in `settings_screen.dart` — guard with `context.mounted` instead, since that `context` isn't tied to any State's own `mounted`.
  This also explains the original session's earlier fix correctly: those spots were nested `builder:` callbacks shadowing the outer `context`, which really did need `context.mounted`. The mistake this time was applying that same fix to a syntactically different case (a plain State method with no shadowing) without checking which case actually applied. Reverted all 8 flagged spots back to bare `mounted`, left the one genuinely-parameter `context` (`_confirmDelete`) on `context.mounted`, and removed the stray `license!` non-null assertion the warning also caught (Dart's flow analysis already treats `license` as promoted at that point once `atLimit` — itself gated on `license != null` — is true, confirmed by the analyzer directly). Verified via tree-sitter, pushed the corrected files, confirmed byte-identical via `md5sum`. Not yet re-confirmed by Craig's own `flutter analyze` — needed before this can be called done.

---

## 7. Screen inventory (confirmed)

From `Wyzesales_Screens_and_Recommendations.md`, plus the newly added User Management screen:

Dashboard · Budgets (one template × 5 dimensions: Sales Person, Category, Customer, Item, Branch) · Sales Analysis (Table + Graph tabs) · YTD Comparative · Quote Analysis · Sales Order Analysis · Sales by [Dimension] (×5) · Performance (×5) · **User Management** (SuperUser only — add/edit/delete users, fields: name, email, contact number, level, rep code, branch code)

Recommended simplification carried forward: Budgets, Sales by, and Performance collapse from 15 near-duplicate screens into 3 reusable, dimension-parameterized components.

## 8. Still open

- Craig to run a real `dotnet build`/`dotnet restore` and `flutter pub get`/`flutter analyze` on machines with real network access — the one thing this sandbox's verification couldn't replace for either the extractor or the Flutter app. See each project's README.
- Apply `004` through `007` to Production Supabase (already done on Staging), after 001–003, in order — none of `005`/`006`/`007` are optional. `007` (grants) is the one to watch: without it, every screen fails with "permission denied," not an RLS/empty-data issue — see `007`'s own header comments for why RLS policies alone weren't enough.
- Build out real charts (Dashboard top-5, Sales Analysis Graph tab) and PDF/CSV export, once a real Flutter build is running and a charting/PDF package can be verified — both are placeholder-but-visible in the current app, not silently missing.
- Decide whether Sales Analysis' Graph tab and YTD Comparative need true multi-filter (category + branch + ... together) trend lines, or whole-company totals (current behaviour) are good enough — see `WyzeSales_Flutter_App_DesignNotes.md` Section 3.
- Whether `budget_figures` should eventually carry a fiscal-year column (to keep historical targets, not just this year's live number) — optional, additive, not blocking.
- Confirm the Year 1/Year 2 profit swap fix (Section 2) against real numbers once there's something to compare against.
- Backtesting the forecast against real historical figures — no longer blocked (Section 1), can happen once real data is flowing.
- Whether the `company` dimension (a single company-wide rollup) is actually wanted on a screen, or can be dropped.
- Confirm quote/sales-order rep attribution (flagged in `WyzeSalesExtract_Rewrite_DesignNotes.md` Section 3) — not blocking.
- Logo design — deferred, not urgent.
- **`send-upgrade-request` Edge Function / Resend setup — deliberately deferred until `wyzesales.com` is live (2026-08-25).** The other three multi-tenancy Edge Functions (`create-client`, `create-user`, `delete-user`) don't touch Resend and can be deployed and used right away. This one needs `support@wyzesales.com` to exist and be verified as a sending domain in Resend before it'll work — Craig hasn't bought the domain yet, so there's nothing to configure yet. Once the domain's live: buy it, add `wyzesales.com` (not `seawyze.co.za` — a stale/expired domain-claim attempt for that one surfaced in Resend while checking on this, unrelated to WyzeSales) in Resend's "Add domain" screen, add the DNS records it gives you at the registrar, set `RESEND_API_KEY` as an Edge Function secret, then deploy `send-upgrade-request`. Flagged here specifically so it doesn't get lost.

**Done**: Supabase schema foundation (`001`), rollup views (`002`), Holt-Winters forecast Edge Function (`003`), budget UPDATE policy fix (`004`), the profiles RLS recursion fix (`005`), RLS enabled + read policies on the 12 tables `001` left unprotected (`006`), and explicit table/view grants to `authenticated` (`007`) — `004`/`005` found while building the Flutter app, `006` caught by Supabase's own linter, `007` caught by the first real run of the Flutter app against Staging (`permission denied for table items`). None hypothetical — each verified against a real seeded Postgres instance before being sent, `007` specifically re-tested with a role that had no ambient extra grants, which is what exposed it. Historical data migration and budget migration both confirmed unnecessary (Section 1). WyzeSalesExtract rebuilt as a plain raw-facts extractor writing directly to Supabase, replacing the text-file/SFTP pipeline. WyzeSales Flutter app scaffolded as an independent project (own `lib/`/`pubspec.yaml`, SeaWyze's theme values copied not shared) covering all 8 screen types from Section 7 below, with some pieces intentionally left as visible placeholders (see `WyzeSales_Flutter_App_DesignNotes.md` Section 3) rather than built blind.

## 9. Multi-tenancy — licensing, platform admin, per-client support logins (2026-08-25)

Built per `Wyzesales_MultiTenancy_Design_Proposal.md`, reviewed and confirmed with Craig before any code was written (all 8 original questions plus one AskUserQuestion follow-up on platform-admin scope). Same shape as SeaWyze's Admin/Settings tier system, simplified throughout to WyzeSales' actual unit — **a client is users only, never vessels** — so every vessel-shaped field SeaWyze carries (`max_vessels`, `base_vessels`, `price_per_additional_vessel`, `helm_tier`) was dropped entirely rather than carried along unused.

**Schema — `schema/008_wyzesales_multitenancy.sql`:**
- New `pricing_plan` table (name, description, `base_price`, `price_per_additional_user`, `base_users`, currency, `is_active`) and `license` table (one per client — `max_users`, `base_users`, dates, status, `annual_price`, `discount_percent`).
- **`discount_percent` instead of a price-override flag** — Craig's own preference over my original `is_custom_price` suggestion: a manually negotiated rate is expressed as a percentage off the plan-derived total, so it stays correct automatically if the base plan's rates change later, rather than freezing a stale absolute number the way SeaWyze's own plan-recalculation silently does today (it overwrites every license's `annual_price` on a rate change, including hand-negotiated ones — see the design doc Section 1's write-up of that as a real gap, not copied here).
- `profiles` gains `is_active` (mirrors SeaWyze's deactivate-without-delete) and `is_platform_admin` (WyzeSales staff — Craig's account, plus each client's `support+<code>@wyzesales.com` login).
- Three `SECURITY DEFINER` helpers (`get_my_client_id()`, `is_adminuser()`, `is_platform_admin()`) — same recursion-safe pattern `005` established for `is_superuser()`, needed because RLS on `license`/`pricing_plan`/`clients` now has to query `profiles`, same as the original recursion trap.
- RLS on the two new tables is **tighter than SeaWyze's own equivalent**, deliberately: SeaWyze's `platform_admin_update_pricing`/`platform_admin_insert_license` policies are `USING(true)` — any authenticated request can write them, the `is_platform_admin` check exists only in the Flutter screen, not the policy. Here every write is gated by `is_platform_admin()` in the policy itself.
- **Seat limit enforced at the DB level**, not just in the app (Craig's decision 4) — a trigger on `profiles` insert/update checks the client's `license.max_users` against its active, non-platform-admin user count, and raises rather than silently allowing an over-limit signup. SeaWyze only checks this in its Flutter "Add user" flow, so a direct API call bypasses it there.
- **Role migration**: every row with `level = 'superuser'` (today, just Craig's own account) moves to `adminuser` + `is_platform_admin = true`. `profiles_superuser_all` (schema/005), which had no client scoping at all — a real cross-tenant hole once more than one client exists — is replaced with two narrower policies: an AdminUser can only manage their own client's users, and cross-tenant reach requires `is_platform_admin()`. `budget_figures_write`/`_update` narrowed from `level in ('adminuser','superuser')` to `adminuser` only.
- **WCSA backfill**: a `Standard` pricing_plan row and a license row for the existing WCSA client, so the app finds real data under the new model instead of nulls. Figures are explicit placeholders (`base_price` 2500, `price_per_additional_user` 350, `base_users` 5, `max_users` 20, all ZAR/month) — not a real quoted price, adjust via the new Platform Admin → Pricing/Licenses tabs immediately after deploying.
- **Not done by this migration, needs a manual step after it runs**: creating the `support+wcsa@wyzesales.com` login itself. Supabase Auth users can't be created from plain SQL (inserting into `auth.users` directly bypasses identity/credential setup and isn't supported) — create it via the new `create-user` Edge Function once deployed, or the Supabase dashboard, with `is_platform_admin = true` and `client_id` = WCSA's.

**Edge Functions** (new `wyzesales/supabase/functions/` directory — didn't exist before this):
- `create-client` — platform-admin-only. Creates a client + license + first adminuser + a `support+<code>@wyzesales.com` platform-admin login in one call, best-effort sequential rollback on failure (same limitation as SeaWyze's `create-company` — several separate Admin-API/table calls can't share one Postgres transaction).
- `create-user` — callable by any AdminUser (not a single top-tier role, per Craig's decision 8), forces the new user onto the caller's own `client_id` regardless of what's in the request body, and surfaces the seat-limit trigger's own error message as-is if that's what blocks it.
- `delete-user` — same defensive checks as SeaWyze's own: no self-delete, no cross-client delete, no deleting a platform-admin account through this path.
- `send-upgrade-request` — emails `support@wyzesales.com` (Craig's decision 6 — "Everything we do going forward must be support@wyzesales.com"; the domain isn't live yet, per Craig's own caveat, so sending will fail until it exists and is verified with Resend).
- All four need `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (or the new `sb_secret_...` key) as an Edge Function secret, and `send-upgrade-request` additionally needs `RESEND_API_KEY` — same secrets SeaWyze's equivalents use. **Not yet deployed** — Craig needs to run `supabase functions deploy` for all four once he's reviewed them.

**Flutter app:**
- New models: `Client`, `PricingPlan`, `License` (the latter with a `discountedMonthly` getter that recomputes live from the joined plan's current rates + `discount_percent`, rather than trusting the stored `annual_price` column, which is only as fresh as the last time the Pricing tab recalculated it — this is what makes the discount self-correct on a rate change instead of going stale).
- `Profile` gains `isActive`/`isPlatformAdmin`; `canManageUsers`/`canEditBudgets` both simplify to `level == adminuser` (superuser dropped from both, per the role migration above).
- New `SettingsRepository` (own-client scoped: client info, license read, users CRUD, upgrade request) and `PlatformAdminRepository` (cross-tenant: clients-with-license listing, license/pricing/client writes, client creation).
- New **Settings** screen (Company / Users / License tabs, AdminUser-gated) and **Platform Admin** screen (Clients / Licenses / Pricing tabs, `is_platform_admin`-gated) — both follow this project's existing plain-Material style (`DataTable`/`AlertDialog`/`AsyncSection`), not SeaWyze's hand-rolled card components. No Notifications tab on Platform Admin (a separate platform-wide-banner feature, not part of this ask) and no Vessels tab on Settings (no WyzeSales equivalent).
- `SessionNotifier` now signs a user out the first time their profile loads with `is_active = false` — Supabase Auth itself has no knowledge of that column, so this is the actual enforcement point for deactivation, same as SeaWyze needs at the app layer for `is_dormant`/`is_active`.
- Sidebar (`app_shell.dart`): "Settings" nav tile (Company/Users/License) now sits alongside Budgets under the existing "Settings" section, gated on `canManageUsers`; a new "Platform" section with a "Platform Admin" tile is gated on `isPlatformAdmin`.
- Verified via tree-sitter (46 files, 0 issues) after every edit, same verification convention this whole project has used throughout.

**Deployment status (updated 2026-08-25):** `008` and `009` (Section 10) are both confirmed run against `wyzesales-staging` — verified directly via `information_schema` queries, not just assumed. The `support+wcsa@wyzesales.com` platform-admin login is created and confirmed linked to WCSA's `client_id` with `is_platform_admin = true`. All four Edge Functions (`create-client`, `create-user`, `delete-user`, `send-upgrade-request`) are deployed to `wyzesales-staging` — confirmed via clean CLI output, no errors, project ref `uxyqthscnlznrjpyogwg`. None needed a manually-set `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`: newer Supabase projects auto-inject `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`/`SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` into every Edge Function already (the dashboard's Secrets page actually blocks a custom secret from using the `SUPABASE_` prefix at all, which is what surfaced this — the corrected guidance is in this doc now for next time). `flutter pub get` and `flutter analyze` both run clean against a real Flutter SDK (2026-08-25) — the first real compiler/analyzer check any of this multi-tenancy code has had, catching what tree-sitter-only verification structurally can't. `flutter analyze` first flagged 4 real issues (1 `prefer_const_constructors`, 3 `use_build_context_synchronously` — nested builder params were shadowing the outer `context`, so the `mounted` check guarding a post-await `ScaffoldMessenger`/`Navigator` call wasn't provably tied to the same `BuildContext`); fixed by un-shadowing the builder params and switching those specific guards to `context.mounted` rather than the bare `mounted` (State-level) check used everywhere else in these files. Second pass caught one delivery mistake on this end — a zip got committed straight onto a `.dart` path without extracting it first, silently truncating that file to 5KB of zip bytes — caught by the usual checksum-verification step before being reported as done, not by Craig. Third `flutter analyze` run: 0 issues.

Remaining: (1) `send-upgrade-request` deliberately deferred until `wyzesales.com` is live and verified in Resend — see Section 8's dedicated bullet so it isn't lost, (2) fix the placeholder pricing numbers via the new Pricing/Licenses tabs, (3) an actual `flutter run` to click through the new Platform Admin / Settings screens end to end — `pub get`/`analyze` passing confirms the code is sound, not that the UI behaves correctly. Production still doesn't exist (Section 10) — whenever Craig creates it, it needs `001` (already corrected) through `008` only, no `009`.

Docs location: `wyzesales/docs/` in the project folder didn't exist before this round, so schema/008 and this file were placed there. Craig's actual canonical copy of the project's docs turned out to live in a separate, disconnected `Desktop/Wyzesales/` folder (flat, no `schema/` subdirectory) — once he named it, `008` and this file were pushed there too, matched against that folder's existing `001`-`007` first (see Section 10 for what that comparison found).

## 10. `sales_document_facts` primary key — Staging still on the pre-fix schema (2026-08-25)

While syncing docs into Craig's canonical `Desktop/Wyzesales/` folder, its copy of `001_wyzesales_foundation.sql` turned out to differ from the one in this project's own docs — missing a fix made to that file while WyzeSalesExtract was being built (see `001`'s own current comment on `sales_document_facts`): the primary key was originally the natural key `(client_id, document_kind, document, item_code)`, corrected to a plain generated `id` once it was clear a real document can legitimately carry the same item on two separate lines, which the natural key would silently collapse into one.

That fix went into the `001` file itself, but never went out as its own migration — so any environment that had already run the old `001` before the fix was made would still be running the natural key underneath, with no record of that anywhere in this doc until now. Confirmed by direct query against `wyzesales-staging` (2026-08-25): its `sales_document_facts_pkey` is still the four-column natural key. Production doesn't exist yet (Craig hasn't created it), so this is a Staging-only problem — a fresh Production just runs the already-corrected `001` and never sees the issue.

**Fix: `009_wyzesales_sales_document_facts_pk_fix.sql`** — adds a generated `id` column (Postgres backfills it for any existing rows automatically), drops the old natural-key primary key, adds the new one on `id`, and re-adds the `(client_id, document_kind, document)` index the corrected `001` expects. Written defensively (`IF EXISTS`/`IF NOT EXISTS`, plus a existence check before the final `ADD PRIMARY KEY`) so it's a no-op if run twice, or run against an environment that's already on the corrected schema. Craig still needs to run this against `wyzesales-staging` himself — not run yet as of this entry.

Worth noting for next time: `002`-`008` were confirmed byte-identical between this project's own copy and Craig's `Desktop/Wyzesales/` folder — `001` was the only one that had drifted, and only because of this one pre-existing, undocumented fix. Nothing else to reconcile.

## 11. Synthetic test data for Staging (2026-08-26)

Craig asked (2026-08-26): "How do we get test data into this app now?" — with the multi-tenancy backend and the restyled Platform Admin/Settings screens in place, there was nothing to click through and look at yet. Two real options exist: run **WyzeSalesExtract** (the actual production ETL, connects directly to WCSA's IQRetail SQL Server database — real data, but real IQRetail access is a prerequisite and it's not a demo/test tool) or write a **synthetic seed script**. Asked Craig directly which he wanted; he chose synthetic seed data.

**Verification approach, given the stakes of running something this complex against a real Supabase project**: rather than write the script and hand it straight to Craig, stood up a throwaway local Postgres 16 database in this working environment, applied all nine real migrations (`001`-`009`) against it end to end, then wrote and ran the seed script against that local copy first — checking the app's actual views (`v_dimension_monthly_sales`, `v_dimension_performance`, `v_sales_documents`, `forecast_input_series`) return sane numbers before this ever touches `wyzesales-staging`. All nine migrations applied cleanly with no changes needed (two small Postgres-only stubs were added purely so the migrations' `auth.uid()`/`profiles` references have something to resolve against locally: an `auth.users` table and an `auth.uid()` function returning null — neither is part of what runs against Staging).

**`010_wyzesales_seed_test_data.sql`** — explicitly labelled as test data, not a schema migration. Scoped entirely to the `WCSA` client (creates the row if it's missing, so it also works as a from-scratch bootstrap). Populates:
- Reference data — 3 branches, 6 sales reps (`R01`-`R05` plus `44`, the walk-in/counter-sale bucket), 6 categories, 5 suppliers, 20 customers (a mix of `attribute_to_assigned_rep = true/false`, matching the real attribution rule in `resolved_rep_code()`), 40 items with realistic water-industry names/pricing — all inserted `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, safe to re-run without clobbering anything edited by hand afterwards in the app.
- ~30 months of `sales_document_facts` (March 2024 through the current month), covering `invoice` (mild growth trend + a Dec/Jan Southern-hemisphere slowdown, so Sales Analysis/YTD Comparative aren't flat lines), `credit_note` (negative-valued partial returns, ~4% of invoice lines, so net sales actually move), `quote` and `sales_order` (positive-valued "pending" documents that reach `v_sales_documents`/the Quote & Sales Order Analysis screens but are excluded from the sales rollup views, matching the real schema's scoping).
- `budget_figures` and `sales_forecast` for every dimension/entity combination that has real history, derived from each entity's own historical average for that fiscal-month label (not invented for entities/months with no data) — seeded directly with `confidence = 'full'` rather than depending on the `compute-forecast` Edge Function, which is built but not yet deployed.
- Fact/budget/forecast tables are wiped and reinserted for WCSA on every run (`delete` then insert) — the same "fully derived, safe to replace wholesale" pattern the real extract already uses for these tables — and the whole script runs inside one transaction. `setseed(0.42)` makes re-runs produce byte-identical numbers, not just "some" numbers, which is what let idempotency actually be checked (ran it twice locally; second run produced the exact same row counts, not double).

**Locally verified results** (throwaway DB, discarded after this check): 1,856 invoices + 89 credit notes nets to ~R61.05M across the ~30 months; every one of the 20 customers, 40 items, 6 categories, 3 branches, and 6 reps shows up in the rollup views (no dead reference rows); `v_dimension_performance` for `company` shows 30 real fiscal-year/month rows (FY2025 full year through FY2027 partial) with actual/target/forecast all populated and varying sensibly month to month; spot-checked `resolved_rep_code` on a `true`-flagged customer (`CUS001`) — every invoice on that account resolves to its assigned rep `R01` regardless of which rep code the sale itself carried, exactly matching the real attribution rule.

**Delivered**: `010_wyzesales_seed_test_data.sql` pushed to `wyzesales/docs/schema/` in the project folder and to `Desktop/Wyzesales/`. **Craig still needs to run it himself, once, against `wyzesales-staging` only** (Supabase SQL editor, paste-and-run) — not run there yet as of this entry. Safe to re-run if he wants a fresh batch of numbers later (it will regenerate, not double, the fact/budget/forecast data), but reference data he edits by hand afterwards (e.g. renaming a customer in the app) will survive a re-run untouched.
