## 12. Sales Analysis graph, dashboard bar colour, table layout (2026-08-26)

Craig, same day as the seed script above: "Can we build the Sales analysis graph. Can we change the black on the bar line in the dashboard to a more appeasing colour. Then on all of the screens please can we fit the views into the screens. It is bundled into top corner or does not fit into the screen." Three separate fixes:

**Sales Analysis Graph tab.** Its own placeholder comment said "a visual trend line is planned for a later pass (see design notes) — shown as a table for now" — that pass is this one. New `lib/shared/widgets/trend_line_chart.dart`: a small `CustomPainter`-based multi-line chart, no third-party charting package — same reasoning as `SimpleBarChart`'s own comment (this sandbox has no way to verify a package like fl_chart actually compiles; a real Flutter SDK only exists on Craig's machine). Plots the same trailing-3-fiscal-years/monthly data the old table did, now with an R Value/R Gross Profit toggle (matching the other analysis screens), one line per fiscal year (oldest = faded neutral, current FY = full brand teal, so "this year" reads as the important line), and a hover/tap interaction: moving the pointer (or tapping on touch) highlights the nearest month with a vertical guide line and a detail row above the chart listing every series' exact value at that point — deliberately a fixed detail row rather than a floating tooltip, since overlay positioning isn't something this sandbox can visually check.

**Dashboard bar colour.** Root cause: `SimpleBarChart`'s track (the bar's background) read `Theme.of(context).dividerColor` — which is a *different* property from the `dividerTheme` this app actually configures in `app_theme.dart`. `ThemeData.dividerColor` is a separate top-level field that falls back to Flutter's own Material default (a flat black at low opacity) whenever nothing sets it directly, which nothing here ever did. That default black-at-low-opacity track was the "black on the bar line" Craig flagged. Fixed by switching to the same theme-derived neutral tint `app_shell.dart`'s top-bar icon chips already use (`colorScheme.onSurface` at 8% alpha) — reads as a soft neutral in both light and dark mode instead of literal black, no new colour introduced.

**Tables not filling the screen.** Root cause, present on every analysis screen built around Flutter's `DataTable` (Sales Analysis' Table tab, Quote Analysis, Sales Order Analysis, Sales By, Performance, Budgets, YTD Comparative — six files in total): each one wrapped its `DataTable` directly in a horizontal `SingleChildScrollView`, which sizes the table to its own intrinsic content width rather than the viewport's. A table with few columns (e.g. YTD Comparative) then rendered small and pinned to the top-left with nothing but plain scaffold background around it — "bundled into the top corner"; a table with many columns (Sales By, Performance) had to be found by scrolling sideways with no visual cue there was more. New shared `lib/shared/widgets/responsive_data_table.dart` wraps every one of these in a `Card` (so there's always a themed panel, matching every other section of the app) plus a `LayoutBuilder`-driven `ConstrainedBox(minWidth: viewport width)` — the standard fix for making a `DataTable` fill the screen while a genuinely wide table still scrolls sideways instead of overflowing. Applied at all six call sites (`document_analysis_view.dart` — shared by three screens, `sales_by_screen.dart`, `performance_screen.dart`, `budgets_screen.dart`'s month table, `ytd_comparative_screen.dart`).

**Deliberately not touched in this pass**: Platform Admin and Settings' own tables use fixed pixel widths (560px/660px `SizedBox`s with fixed per-column widths) inside a similar horizontal scroll — but that's a literal port of SeaWyze's own layout, done on Craig's earlier explicit instruction to make those two screens "exactly the same as SeaWyze." Milder version of the same "narrow table, wide screen" look is possible there on a big monitor, but changing it risks drifting from that pixel-parity instruction on a screen Craig already confirmed clean (`flutter analyze` "No issues"). Flagged here rather than silently left alone or silently changed — worth a decision from Craig either way, not assumed.

**Verification**: no real Flutter SDK exists in this working sandbox (confirmed again this session — no `dart`/`flutter` binary), so as with every other Dart change in this project, these were checked with a tree-sitter parse (a real Dart grammar, not the earlier ad-hoc setup — `tree-sitter==0.25.2` + `tree-sitter-dart==0.1.0`, the combination that actually loads) confirming no syntax errors across all 9 changed/new files, plus a manual read-through for the kind of thing tree-sitter can't catch — in particular, deliberately avoiding a second `.clamp()` call on an already-rounded `int` in the new chart's hit-testing code, since `num.clamp()` returns `num` even when called on an int, and this project has no live analyzer here to double-check an implicit `num`→`int` downcast resolves the way intended (this exact class of "num.clamp() surprises people" is a well-known, well-documented Dart gotcha). Files pushed to both `projects/wyzesales/` and confirmed byte-identical via checksum. **Not yet confirmed by a real `flutter analyze`/run on Craig's machine** — that's the real check, same as every other UI change in this project.

## 13. SAMTRA-derived colour rebrand and header-row reflow (2026-08-26)

Craig uploaded `SAMTRA_design_concept.html` — a marketing mockup for an unrelated maritime training academy, not the WyzeSales app — and said: "I'm struggling with the colour scheme. Can we change the colour scheme as per the attached. Also if the screen is not big enough to render the view correctly can we insert a scroll function instead of truncating the data." First read wrongly assumed the mockup itself needed a new palette; Craig corrected this ("It is not on the website I just sent you a mock up... Stop") and then confirmed via a clarifying question that the ask was the opposite direction: apply the mockup's own navy/amber palette *to* WyzeSales.

**Colour mapping.** `AppColors` in `app_theme.dart` now sources every brand value straight from the mockup's `:root` CSS: `navyDeep #0A1620`, `navyMid #0E2230`, `navySurface #132A38`, dark-mode text `#EDF1F3`. The app's teal accent is now the mockup's amber (`#FFB23E`, dim variant `#9C7022`), and a new `AppColors.onAccent` (`#1A1206`, the mockup's own `.btn-primary` text colour) replaces plain white wherever text/icons sit on a solid accent fill — amber is much lighter than the old cyan-leaning teal, so white-on-teal that used to read fine is now low-contrast. Fixed at every solid-fill usage found by grep: `colorScheme.onPrimary`, the elevated-button and segmented-button foreground colours in both light and dark themes (`app_theme.dart`), the profile-menu avatar initials (`app_shell.dart`), and the two custom pill-button widgets that share this pattern (`settings_screen.dart`'s `_primaryBtn`, `platform_admin_screen.dart`'s `_PrimaryBtn`) — each now computes its foreground colour from whether the button is enabled/filled, rather than hard-coding white.

**Deliberately kept the field names `teal`/`tealDark`** despite them now holding amber values, rather than renaming across the ~8 files that reference them — a purely cosmetic mismatch between name and value, and a mechanical rename across that many call sites carries more risk than benefit for a naming nit. Documented directly in a code comment on the `AppColors` class so it isn't a silent trap for whoever reads that code next.

**Flag for Craig's attention**: the existing `AppColors.caution` (status colour, amber-ish `#F59E0B`) is now close in hue to the new brand accent (`#FFB23E`). Left both as-is rather than guessing which should move, since caution/status colours carry semantic meaning (e.g. "target at risk") that's separate from brand identity — but a badge and a button can now look like they're using "the same colour" for two different reasons on the same screen. Worth a decision from Craig: leave them near-identical, or shift `caution` toward orange/red to separate it from the brand amber.

**Light theme intentionally untouched** — `lightBackground`, `lightSurface`, `lightText`, `lightTextSecondary` keep their previous values, since the SAMTRA mockup is dark-only and has no light-mode equivalent to derive from. Only the dark theme's background/surface tones and the shared accent/onAccent values changed.

**Header/filter-row overflow (second half of the request).** "Insert a scroll function instead of truncating the data" was read as being about the actual data — which the Section 12 `ResponsiveDataTable` work already scrolls safely — but several screens' *filter/header rows* had the same class of bug the tables had: a `Row(mainAxisAlignment: spaceBetween)` whose children were sized to overflow sideways off narrow windows rather than reflow, because a `Wrap` placed as a plain (non-`Expanded`) child of a `Row` receives unbounded width from that slot and never actually triggers its own wrapping — confirmed by contrast with `document_analysis_view.dart`, which already used the correct `Expanded(child: Wrap(...))` pattern. Rather than adding a literal horizontal scrollbar to these rows (awkward with `spaceBetween`, and `Expanded` doesn't work inside a horizontally-scrolling `Row`), every affected header now uses `Wrap` with `alignment: WrapAlignment.spaceBetween` — visually identical to the old `Row` when there's room, and drops content cleanly to a second line instead of clipping or requiring a scroll gesture when there isn't. Fixed in `performance_screen.dart` (3 filter dropdowns + export buttons), `sales_by_screen.dart` (dimension switcher + sort dropdown vs. toggle + export buttons), `ytd_comparative_screen.dart`, `sales_analysis_screen.dart`, and `dashboard_screen.dart`'s "Top 5 breakdown" row. Checked the rest of the app for the same `spaceBetween` pattern: `settings_screen.dart`'s occurrences are plain label/value rows already protected by `Flexible`+ellipsis (no filter controls to reflow), `budgets_screen.dart`'s `Row` is a fixed-width sidebar plus `Expanded` content pane (a different, already-safe layout), and `filter_bar.dart` already used `Wrap`.

**Verification**: all 9 changed files (`app_theme.dart`, `app_shell.dart`, `settings_screen.dart`, `platform_admin_screen.dart`, `performance_screen.dart`, `sales_by_screen.dart`, `ytd_comparative_screen.dart`, `sales_analysis_screen.dart`, `dashboard_screen.dart`) parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check. Pushed to `projects/wyzesales/` and confirmed byte-identical via checksum. **Not yet confirmed by a real `flutter analyze`/run on Craig's machine** — same caveat as every other UI change this session, since no Flutter SDK exists in this sandbox.

Craig ran `flutter analyze` on this batch afterward — the first real compiler check any of this session's work has had. Result: 2 `info`-level `prefer_const_declarations` hints in `trend_line_chart.dart` (two `final` locals that could be `const`), nothing else. Fixed and re-pushed the same day. Everything else in this pass — the SAMTRA rebrand, all the table/chart/reflow fixes — compiled clean on the first try despite having no local compiler to check against.

## 14. Dashboard rebuild — search, pie-chart breakdown, quick actions (2026-08-26)

Craig, same day: "Can we insert a search function as per seawyze in the top bar. Remove last update tile and insert last update text in the top bar. Make the quick action tiles resemble seawyze and space them correctly across the screen. I don't see why the whole screen needs to disappear / refresh when flipping between R Value and Gross Profit. Can we replace the top 5 bar chart with 2 pie charts, MTD and YTD... Select, Top 5, bottom 5, top 5 diminishing, top 5 growth." Six changes, all landing on the Dashboard and a few shared pieces. Before building, read SeaWyze's actual source (`~/projects/seawyze` — the same reference app Section 6's UI restyle and Section 12's "exactly like SeaWyze" tables already point to) rather than guessing at "as per seawyze," and clarified the ambiguous parts with Craig up front rather than assuming: 5 dimensions including Branch (not just the 4 the old grid had), one interactive dimension/rank-mode picker rather than 8 fixed pies, MTD trend = month-over-month/YTD trend = year-over-year, drill-down pins the clicked entity to the top of the Sales By table rather than hard-filtering everything else out, and search covers the 5 reference-data dimensions with each result labelled by which dimension it's from.

**The actual "why does the screen disappear" bug.** Root cause, confirmed by reading `AsyncSection` (`shared/widgets/async_section.dart`): it's a thin `FutureBuilder` wrapper that replaces its *entire* subtree with a lone spinner on `ConnectionState.waiting`. The old Dashboard wrapped its whole body — KPI tiles, quick actions, everything — in one `AsyncSection`, and the R Value/Gross Profit toggle's `onChanged` reassigned that one shared `Future`, which re-ran every query on screen (KPI totals, all 4 dimensions × 2 periods, name lookups) just to re-sort by a field already sitting in the fetched rows. Fixed by splitting the screen into two independent pieces of state: `_kpiFuture` (loaded once, still behind `AsyncSection`, since KPI totals genuinely don't change on any control here) and `_dimensionData`/`_dimensionLoading` (a plain nullable field + bool, not a `Future` at all) for the breakdown section — switching R Value/Gross Profit or the Top 5/Bottom 5/Diminishing 5/Growth 5 selector is now a pure re-sort of rows already in memory, no network call, no spinner. Only actually switching *dimension* refetches, and even then the previous dimension's charts stay on screen (a small 14px spinner appears next to the heading) rather than the section going blank.

**Top-bar search.** New `shared/widgets/top_bar_search.dart`, ported from SeaWyze's `GlobalSearchBar` — same shape (a `LayerLink`+`OverlayEntry` typeahead dropdown, a full-screen dismiss layer behind a `CompositedTransformFollower` results panel, 300ms debounce), pointed at WyzeSales' own reference data instead of SeaWyze's vessels/tasks/permits. New `ReferenceDataRepository.searchAllDimensions()` queries all 5 dimension tables (added a `search` param to `salesReps`/`categories`/`branches`, which didn't have one — `customers`/`items` already did) and tags each match with its dimension, so a name matching more than one dimension shows as e.g. "Acme Corp — Customer" rather than being ambiguous (Craig: "search on the dimensions and then display the context for the user to choose"). Picking a result navigates to `/sales-by/<dimension>?highlight=<code>` — the same destination the pie-chart drill-down uses (see below). Inserted into `AppShell`'s top bar in the same slot SeaWyze gives its own search field (`Expanded`, between the screen title and the date/actions cluster); the title itself is no longer `Expanded` since the search field needs the stretchy slot instead, and the date chip / new "last updated" chip are now hidden on narrow (drawer-breakpoint) widths to keep that row from overflowing once a long screen title and a narrow window compete for the same space.

**Last-updated moved into the top bar.** New `lastDataUpdateProvider` in `core/app_providers.dart` (a plain, non-autoDispose `FutureProvider<DateTime?>` reading `sales_document_facts.extracted_at`) replaces the Dashboard's own inline query — both the Dashboard and a new `_LastDataUpdateChip` in `AppShell`'s top bar now read the same cached value, instead of the Dashboard fetching it privately. The KPI row's "Last Updated" `StatCard` is gone; the same reading now shows as a small chip in the top bar on every screen, not just the Dashboard, matching Craig's exact request.

**Quick action tiles**, restyled to match SeaWyze's `_QuickActionBtn` exactly rather than approximately: added the coloured border that was the actual visible mismatch (the previous restyle, Section 6, had the tinted fill and icon badge but no border), and a dark/light-aware fill opacity (SeaWyze uses a stronger tint in dark mode). "Space them correctly across the screen" was a layout bug, not just a styling one: the old `Wrap` of fixed-150px tiles left them clumped at the left edge instead of spread across the row. Replaced with SeaWyze's own `LayoutBuilder` pattern — `Expanded` tiles filling a `Row` on a wide screen, a 3-column `GridView` below 500px.

**The pie-chart breakdown**, replacing the old 4-dimension × MTD/YTD grid of 8 bar charts. New `shared/widgets/simple_pie_chart.dart` — a dependency-free donut chart (`CustomPainter`, no fl_chart, same reasoning as `SimpleBarChart`/`TrendLineChart`: no way to verify a third-party charting package compiles in this sandbox). The Dashboard now has one dimension picker (all 5 — Customer/Item/Sales Person/Category/Branch, fixing the old grid's omission of Branch), one Top 5/Bottom 5/Diminishing 5/Growth 5 picker, the existing R Value/Gross Profit toggle, and two donuts (MTD, YTD) for whatever's currently selected. Switching dimension is the only control that refetches (a fresh `fetchDimensionMonthlySales` call for the current and prior fiscal year); everything else re-derives client-side from those same rows — including "Diminishing 5"/"Growth 5", which need a comparison baseline: MTD compares this calendar month against last calendar month, YTD compares this fiscal-year-to-date against the *same elapsed fiscal months* last year (not the whole prior year, so a 5-month-old fiscal year isn't compared against a full 12 months of the one before it) — matching Craig's "MTD Chart must look at the monthly trend and YTD looks at the yearly trend" exactly. The ranking mode picks *which* 5 entities are shown (including one that dropped to zero activity entirely, for Diminishing 5 — reachable only by treating the entity-code set as the union of both periods, not just the current one); the pie itself always shows each selected entity's current-period value, so what's being measured stays consistent across all four modes and only the selection criterion changes.

**Negative-value pie slices** — a real simplification, flagged rather than hidden: a wedge's size is its *magnitude* share of the total magnitude shown, not its signed value, because a pie's areas only mean something as parts of a positive whole, and Gross Profit's Bottom 5/Diminishing 5 can genuinely include a loss-making customer that has no sane "part of a whole" interpretation summed with its sign intact. A negative slice is still sized honestly by how big the loss is, and is coloured red (`AppColors.negative`) regardless of its position in the palette, so it reads as "this one is a loss" rather than blending in as an ordinary contributor.

**Hover and drill-down.** Hovering a wedge (or its legend entry) shows detail in a fixed row above the chart — "Customer: R45,230 (32% of shown)" — deliberately not a floating tooltip, same reasoning as `TrendLineChart`'s own fixed detail row (overlay positioning isn't something this sandbox can visually verify). Clicking a wedge calls back to the Dashboard, which navigates to `/sales-by/<dimension>?highlight=<code>`. Rather than hard-filtering that screen down to one row (Craig, when asked to clarify: "I'm not sure what you mean. I think 1" — picked "filtered to that entity" without being fully sure what that meant in practice), `sales_by_screen.dart` now *pins* the matching row to the top of the table regardless of the active sort, tints its background, and shows a small dismissible banner naming it — chosen over a literal filter so the rest of the dimension stays visible for comparison, which seemed like the more useful reading of "drill down to inspect it." Worth flagging back to Craig in case a literal single-row filter was actually what he had in mind. The router's `/sales-by/:dimension` route now reads an optional `?highlight=` query parameter to drive this, shared by both the pie-chart drill-down and the new top-bar search.

**Files changed**: `core/app_providers.dart` (new `lastDataUpdateProvider`), `core/router/app_router.dart` (`?highlight=` parsing), `data/models/reference_data.dart` (new `DimensionSearchResult`), `data/repositories/reference_data_repository.dart` (`search` param on 3 more methods, new `searchAllDimensions`), `shared/widgets/app_shell.dart` (search + last-updated chip in the top bar), `shared/widgets/top_bar_search.dart` (new), `shared/widgets/quick_action_tile.dart` (restyled), `shared/widgets/simple_pie_chart.dart` (new), `features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart` (rebuilt), `features/dimension_templates/screens/sales_by_screen.dart` (highlight/pin support).

**A Flutter-specific bug caught and fixed before delivery, not by `flutter analyze`**: the first draft called `setState()` transitively from `initState()` (the loading-flag flip at the top of `_loadDimension()`, invoked directly from `initState`) — Flutter forbids any `setState()` call during the synchronous window in which a widget is first mounted and built, including the synchronous prefix of an `async` method before its first `await`. This throws a real runtime assertion ("setState() or markNeedsBuild() called during build") the moment the Dashboard opens; nothing in this sandbox's tree-sitter-based syntax check can catch it, since it's a framework contract violation, not a parse error. Caught on a manual re-read and fixed by seeding `_dimensionLoading = true` as a direct field write in `initState` instead of through `setState`, leaving every actual `setState` call in event handlers and post-`await` continuations where it belongs. Mentioned here specifically because it's exactly the kind of bug that could have slipped through everything this sandbox can check and only shown up when Craig opened the screen.

**Verification**: all 10 files parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check; manually re-read for the class of bug tree-sitter can't catch (the `setState`-during-`initState` issue above; also double-checked the `switch` statement in `_pickSlices` has explicit `break`s, since Dart's plain `switch` *statement* — unlike the newer switch *expression* — still requires them and silently falling through is a compile error the syntax checker wouldn't flag either). Pushed to `projects/wyzesales/` and confirmed byte-identical via checksum. **Not yet confirmed by a real `flutter analyze`/run on Craig's machine.**

## 15. Drill-down sort mapping + KPI/filter row spacing (2026-08-26)

Craig, after Section 14 landed: "When we drill down. e.g. Top 5 Customers must show Sales by Customer formatted top 5 in descending order. Bottom 5 would show in ascending order etc. Does this make sense? Also can we format the 4 tiles to spread across the available screen space as well as chart filter options." Two independent fixes.

**Drill-down sort mapping — yes, this makes sense, and it's now what happens.** The Dashboard's `_drillDown` (in `dashboard_screen.dart`) now carries the clicked pie's rank mode, period, and measure through the URL: `/sales-by/<dimension>?highlight=<code>&rank=<top5|bottom5|diminishing5|growth5>&period=<mtd|ytd>&measure=<rValue|grossProfit>`. `app_router.dart` parses all three and passes them to `SalesByScreen` as new `initialRank`/`initialPeriod`/`initialMeasure` constructor params. `sales_by_screen.dart` maps them onto its own sort state via a new `_applyInitialRank()`, run once in `initState` (and again in `didUpdateWidget` if a second drill-down lands on an already-open screen):

- **Top 5** → sort by the matching column (latest month for MTD, current FY for YTD), descending.
- **Bottom 5** → same column, ascending.
- **Diminishing 5** → sort by month-over-month or year-over-year *change*, ascending (biggest decline — the most negative number — first).
- **Growth 5** → same change column, descending (biggest growth first).

MTD maps to the latest-month column and YTD to the current-FY column because those are literally the two columns the Dashboard's own MTD/YTD pies are built from — clicking a wedge in one of those charts now opens the table sorted by the same number that put that wedge in the top/bottom 5. A sort-direction toggle button (arrow icon, next to the sort dropdown) lets a normal, non-drill-down visit flip between highest-first and lowest-first freely; two new dropdown entries ("Sort: FY change", "Sort: month change") make the change-based sort available outside a drill-down too, not just as an internal-only mode.

**Trend definitions replicated exactly, not approximated.** `_SortBy` gained `yearOverYearChange`/`monthOverMonthChange`, backed by two new per-entity maps (`momDelta`, `yoyDelta`) computed in `_load()` using the *same* two definitions Section 14 established for the Dashboard's own Diminishing 5/Growth 5 ranking: month-over-month compares the latest month present in the fetched rows against the one before it; year-over-year compares the current fiscal year's total against the *same set of elapsed fiscal months* last fiscal year (not the whole prior year) — re-deriving `elapsedFiscalMonths` from `sales_by_screen.dart`'s own already-fetched rows rather than trusting a value passed through the URL, since the two screens' data windows aren't identical (`sales_by_screen.dart` pulls 3 fiscal years back, the Dashboard only 2) and the set of "elapsed" months could in principle differ by the time someone lands on this screen.

**A pre-existing bug fixed as a side effect.** `didUpdateWidget`'s dimension-switch branch used to reassign `_future = _load()` directly, without a `setState()` wrapper — meaning a dimension change via the in-page switcher dropdown wasn't guaranteed to immediately repaint `AsyncSection` into its loading state (Flutter has no obligation to schedule a rebuild off a plain field mutation). The full `didUpdateWidget` rewrite needed for the three new constructor params folds this into one `setState()` call that also picks up highlight/measure/rank changes, fixing the missing-setState bug along the way rather than leaving it to coexist with the new logic.

**Highlight banner now names what it's sorted by.** When arriving via a drill-down, the pinned-row banner above the table now reads "Showing Acme Corp pinned to the top — sorted to match Top 5 · MTD." instead of the bare "pinned to the top" text from Section 14 — makes the sort visibly traceable back to the chart/mode that was clicked, not just a silent reordering.

**KPI tiles and filter/options row now spread across the screen.** Both were previously left-aligned `Wrap`s that clumped toward the left edge of a wide window instead of using the available width — same underlying "spread across the screen" ask Craig has made before (Section 13's header-row reflow), but this time about spacing rather than overflow. Fixed the same way as every other "spread across the screen" request in this app: `WrapAlignment.spaceBetween` (growing gaps between fixed-size children when they fit on one line, same behaviour as `Row`'s `MainAxisAlignment.spaceBetween`, while still safely reflowing to a second line on a narrow window) rather than switching to `Expanded`/stretched tiles — chosen because stretching the KPI cards or filter dropdowns to fill all available width would distort their fixed, deliberately-sized layout (200px KPI cards, fixed-width dropdowns) into something wider and less proportioned than Craig's actual request called for. Applied to the Dashboard's KPI `Wrap` (`dashboard_screen.dart`) and, separately, its dimension/rank-mode/measure filter row — previously two nested `Wrap`s (an inner one grouping the three controls, an outer one wrapping that against the section heading), now flattened into a single flat `Wrap(alignment: spaceBetween)` with the heading and all three controls as direct siblings, so the spacing is genuinely even across the whole row rather than only between two clumps.

**Files changed**: `core/router/app_router.dart` (`?rank=`/`?period=`/`?measure=` parsing), `features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart` (`_drillDown` now carries rank/period/measure; KPI row and filter row both `spaceBetween`), `features/dimension_templates/screens/sales_by_screen.dart` (`initialRank`/`initialPeriod`/`initialMeasure` params, `_applyInitialRank()`, `momDelta`/`yoyDelta` computation, expanded `_SortBy` + direction toggle, banner detail text, `didUpdateWidget` setState fix).

**Verification**: all 3 files parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check. Pushed to `projects/wyzesales/` and confirmed byte-identical via checksum. **Not yet confirmed by a real `flutter analyze`/run on Craig's machine** — same caveat as every prior entry; in particular this pass added a `switch` *statement* (`_applyInitialRank`) and extended another (`_buildTable`'s sort switch) with explicit `break`s throughout, matched against the same real Dart requirement flagged in Sections 13/14, but only a real compiler can confirm nothing was missed.

Craig ran `flutter analyze` on this batch: one `duplicate_definition` error (`_load()` in `sales_by_screen.dart` had accidentally declared `currentFy` twice — once at the top of the method, again lower down for the new `yoyDelta` computation, where it should have just reused the existing one) and one `prefer_const_constructors` info in `top_bar_search.dart` (a `TextStyle` built from `AppColors.teal`, a compile-time constant, that hadn't been marked `const`). Both fixed and re-pushed same day.

**Then Craig sent a screenshot** showing the actual running Dashboard: the 4 KPI tiles and the filter/chart-options row were still visibly bunched on the left with a wide blank gap on the right, despite both being wrapped in `WrapAlignment.spaceBetween`. Root cause, missed in the original fix: `Wrap` does not behave like `Row` here. A `Row` defaults to `MainAxisSize.max` — it claims all available width from its parent even when unconstrained, which is *why* `Row(mainAxisAlignment: spaceBetween)` reliably spreads content elsewhere in this app. `Wrap` has no equivalent property — laid out directly inside a `Column` (which gives its children only *loose* width constraints), it sizes its own bounding box to just fit whatever its children need on that line, then that box is left-aligned within the column. With 4 fixed 200px KPI cards totalling well under the row's actual available width, there was never any leftover space inside the Wrap's own (self-shrunk) box for `spaceBetween` to distribute — the alignment property was doing nothing, silently, and had been doing nothing since Section 15 first shipped.

**Fix**: wrap every such `Wrap` in `SizedBox(width: double.infinity, child: Wrap(...))`. `SizedBox`'s `double.infinity` width gets clamped down to the parent's actual (finite) max width during layout — it does not blow out unbounded — so this forces the `Wrap` to claim the *full* row width before laying out its children, giving `spaceBetween` real space to spread the gaps across. Confirmed this does not change the existing safe-reflow behaviour: the wrap-to-a-second-line threshold was always based on the incoming max-width constraint from the parent, never on the `Wrap`'s own previously-shrunk reported size, so content still drops cleanly to a new line on a narrow window exactly as before.

Applied everywhere this same pattern had been used for a "spread across the full width" effect, not just the two rows Craig's screenshot showed — the same latent bug was sitting in every header row from Section 13's reflow fix, just less visible there because two roughly-similar-sized clusters happen to leave a smaller gap than four small fixed-width cards did. Fixed in `dashboard_screen.dart` (KPI row, filter/options row), `sales_by_screen.dart` (dimension/sort header row), `ytd_comparative_screen.dart`, and `sales_analysis_screen.dart` (both single-row headers). Not touched: `performance_screen.dart`'s header `Wrap`, which was already correctly wrapped in `Expanded` inside a `Row` (a `Row`'s `Expanded` child *does* get a tight width constraint, so that one never had this bug) — flagged as the working reference pattern in Section 13 and left alone.

**Why this got past this session's own checks**: tree-sitter's syntax check has no concept of layout or rendering, so it was never going to catch this — this is a genuine Flutter-layout-semantics bug, the kind that only a running app (or a real widget test with actual constraints) can surface, exactly like the `setState`-during-`initState` bug in Section 14. Worth remembering going forward: `Wrap`'s alignment properties (`spaceBetween`, `spaceAround`, `spaceEvenly`, `center`) only have a visible effect once the `Wrap` itself is stretched to claim more width than its children need — on its own, inside a `Column` or similar loose-width parent, a `Wrap` always shrink-wraps to content first.

**Verification**: all 4 files parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check. Pushed to `projects/wyzesales/` and confirmed byte-identical via checksum. **Not yet confirmed by a real `flutter analyze`/run or a visual check on Craig's machine** — this fix specifically needs the visual check, not just the compiler, since the bug it addresses was a rendering/layout issue invisible to any static check available in this sandbox.

## 16. Sales By's recent-month table columns were mislabelled (2026-08-26)

Craig asked what criteria the pie charts' Top 5/Bottom 5/Diminishing 5/Growth 5 use, mentioning it looked like "Top 5 Customer are calculated on R Value for June 2026" — surprising, since today is 26 August. Answered the ranking criteria directly (current-period value for Top 5/Bottom 5, period-over-period change for Diminishing 5/Growth 5; MTD = the real calendar current/previous month via `DateTime.now()`, YTD = current fiscal year vs the same elapsed fiscal months last year), but flagged that MTD being clock-driven rather than data-driven was worth double-checking against his numbers.

**Craig then sent screenshots making the mismatch concrete**: the Dashboard's Customer — MTD pie showed Johannesburg City Utilities at R319,818, but Sales By's own table had R319,818 sitting under a column headed "Jun 26", while the column headed "Aug 26" showed R263,060 instead. Tracked this down by reproducing the arithmetic: the pie's full 5-customer MTD total (R1,070,605) matches the sum of the values sitting under the table's "Jun 26" header exactly, to the rand, across all 5 customers — not a coincidence.

**Root cause, found in `sales_by_screen.dart`'s `_buildTable`, and it's not the Dashboard's fault**: the three recent-month table *headers* were built by looping over `data.recentMonths` directly — sorted most-recent-first (Aug, Jul, Jun) — while the *row cells* underneath were built by looping over `data.recentMonths.reversed` — oldest-first (Jun, Jul, Aug) — a leftover from a comment ("recentMonths is sorted most-recent-first; walk it oldest-to-newest so 'vs prior month' reads left-to-right chronologically") that correctly fixed the *cells'* ordering at some point but never got applied to the *header* loop sitting a few lines above it. For exactly 3 months this silently swaps the first and third columns' data under each other's headers while the middle column (Jul, position 1 in both a list and its reverse) coincidentally lines up — which is exactly the pattern in the screenshot: "Aug 26" showing June's real number, "Jun 26" showing August's real number, "Jul 26" correct throughout. The Dashboard's MTD pie was never wrong; it computes independently from `DateTime.now()` and the raw rows, with no header/cell indirection to get out of sync. It was quietly displaying the correct August total the whole time — Sales By's table was just mislabelling which column that number belonged to.

**Fix**: hoisted the `chronological` (oldest-to-newest) ordering out of the row-building closure into one `final chronological = data.recentMonths.reversed.toList();` computed once in `_buildTable`, and pointed the header loop at the *same* `chronological` list instead of `data.recentMonths` — headers and cells now iterate in lockstep, matching the FY columns' own left-to-right-oldest-to-newest convention just above them in the same table. Nothing about `recentMonths`' own most-recent-first ordering changed elsewhere (it's still what `_sortBy.currentMonth`/`_applyInitialRank`'s "latest month" sort and the Dashboard's own MTD period rely on, and those were never affected by this bug — they read `recentMonths[0]`/`.first` directly, not through the table's header/cell split).

**Worth Craig's attention**: this table has been mislabelling its two outer month columns since Section 12 first introduced the 3-recent-months layout — every screen reading that table (not the pie charts) has had its "latest month" and "third-most-recent month" figures swapped this whole time, on every dimension. Worth a quick sanity check against a number you already know once this lands, just to confirrm the fix reads right on your data, not just mine.

**Verification**: `sales_by_screen.dart` parses clean with the tree-sitter Dart check. Pushed to `projects/wyzesales/` and confirmed byte-identical via checksum. **Not yet confirmed visually on Craig's machine** — same as Section 15's follow-up, this is a data-correctness bug only a real look at real numbers can close out, not something any check in this sandbox can verify end-to-end.

## 17. Column-click sorting, horizontal scrollbar, even column spacing — every table (2026-08-26)

Craig: "On every table of data. 1. Can we sort the data ascending or descending order by clicking on a column header. 2. Build in a horizontal scroll bar when the data does not fit into the screen horizontally. Space the data evenly eliminating white space." Applies to every screen built on the shared `ResponsiveDataTable` widget: Sales Analysis' Table tab, Quote Analysis, and Sales Order Analysis (all three share `document_analysis_view.dart`), Sales By, Performance, Budgets, and YTD Comparative — 6 files in total, touched together since the changes are structural to the shared wrapper.

**Column-click sorting.** Every table now uses Flutter's built-in `DataColumn.onSort`/`DataTable.sortColumnIndex`/`sortAscending` — clicking a header sorts by that column immediately; clicking the same header again flips the direction (Flutter's own built-in behaviour, not something built by hand); the arrow icon in the header shows which column and direction is active. Each screen owns its own sort state (`_sortColumnIndex`/`_sortAscending`) and comparator, since only the screen that built the rows knows how to compare its own data — `ResponsiveDataTable` itself stays a dumb display wrapper, same separation of concerns as the app's other shared widgets.

Two deliberate exceptions, both flagged rather than silently applied: Budgets' month-editing table keeps its fixed fiscal-month order (Mar → Feb) with no click-to-sort — it's an editable grid with a live `TextEditingController` per row, and letting rows reorder out from under someone mid-edit seemed like the wrong trade for a sort feature nobody's likely to want on a 12-row form; and `sales_by_screen.dart`'s old bespoke sort dropdown + direction-toggle button (added in Section 15, mapping Top 5/Bottom 5/etc. onto specific columns) is now gone entirely — native column-click sorting subsumes everything that dropdown did (its "current FY"/"latest month"/"FY change"/"month change" options are just clicking those literal columns now) while also making every other column sortable too, which the dropdown never covered (FY2025 or FY2026 specifically, for instance).

**A real trade-off in the drill-down mapping, worth Craig's attention.** The Dashboard's Diminishing 5/Growth 5 pies rank entities by raw Rand change (current period minus previous), not percentage — established explicitly in Section 15 to match the Dashboard's own definition. Sales By's "vs FY"/"vs prior month" columns display a *percentage*, and column-click sorting now sorts by exactly what's printed in the column it's clicking — anything else would look broken the moment someone compared the sorted order against the visible percentages. So a Diminishing 5/Growth 5 drill-down now opens Sales By sorted by the closest matching column (the percentage variance), not a re-derivation of the pie's own raw-Rand ranking — the two can genuinely disagree (a large account's modest percentage swing can outrank a small account's dramatic one, or vice versa), so the 5 rows pinned toward the top of a Diminishing 5/Growth 5 drill-down won't always be the literal same 5 wedges that were in the chart. Top 5/Bottom 5 drill-downs are unaffected — those were always plain-value comparisons with no percentage-vs-Rand ambiguity. As a side effect, `sales_by_screen.dart`'s precomputed `momDelta`/`yoyDelta` maps (the raw-Rand deltas Section 15 added specifically for the old dropdown) are no longer used anywhere and were removed.

**Horizontal scrollbar.** `ResponsiveDataTable` now wraps its horizontal (and, where applicable, vertical) `SingleChildScrollView`s in a `Scrollbar(thumbVisibility: true, ...)` each with its own explicit `ScrollController` — a real, documented Flutter gotcha here: a `Scrollbar` with no controller attaches to whichever `Scrollable` notification reaches it first, which is ambiguous/wrong the moment there's more than one scrollable in the same subtree (as there now is, horizontal + vertical nested). Giving each `Scrollbar` its own controller, matched to its own `SingleChildScrollView`, is the standard fix and keeps the two independent.

**Even spacing, eliminating white space.** Root cause: Flutter's `DataTable`, like `Wrap` (Section 15's follow-up), always sizes itself to its own intrinsic column widths — the "fill the width" fix from Section 12 (`ConstrainedBox(minWidth: viewport width)`) made the *panel* full width but never stretched the *columns* into that space, leaving a blank gutter after the last column exactly like the KPI tiles did before their own fix. `DataTable` has no flex-column-width API to reach for instead, so `ResponsiveDataTable` now grows `columnSpacing` (the fixed gap `DataTable` puts between every pair of columns) until the table's own rendered width exactly matches the viewport — spreading the leftover space out as gaps rather than stretching any column's content, the same philosophy as every other "spread evenly" fix this session. Because Flutter can only report a `DataTable`'s natural width *after* laying it out once, this is a genuine two-pass widget: it lays out with default spacing, measures the real rendered width via a `GlobalKey`, and — if narrower than the viewport — immediately re-lays-out with the computed spacing. That means a one-frame "pop" from packed to spread is possible on load or on a window resize; there's no single-pass way around that for a widget with no built-in flex-width support. It only re-measures when the viewport width actually changes, not on every rebuild, so it settles immediately rather than oscillating.

**Verification**: all 6 files parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check. Pushed to `projects/wyzesales/` and confirmed byte-identical via checksum. **Not yet confirmed visually on Craig's machine** — this entire section is layout/rendering behaviour (the two-pass column-spacing measurement, the dual-Scrollbar wiring, the click-to-sort arrows) that nothing in this sandbox can render or click-test; a real look at a real table, ideally one wide enough to need the horizontal scrollbar, is the actual check here.

## 18. Global cross-dimension filters, persistent across every screen (2026-08-26)

Craig: "Selection filters need to be iterative throughout the application. i.e. If I select a sales person on any screen and I navigate to another screen that filtered salesperson must stay filtered and the data accordingly. Then on each screen we need to be able to somehow see what filters are applied. Multiple filters can be applied at once." Asked three clarifying questions before starting, given the size of the change: Craig's answers — all 5 dimensions plus Year and Month "where applicable"; build the new Supabase migration now rather than shipping line-level-only ("filter by Customer by Item etc. needs to work for these views too"); filters persist for the session only, not remembered across logins.

**The architectural problem this had to solve.** `v_sales_documents` (the line-level view behind Sales Analysis' Table tab, Quote Analysis, and Sales Order Analysis) already carries every dimension column on every row, so those three screens could already be filtered by more than one dimension at once — they just didn't share a persistent, app-wide copy of the filter values. The other five screens (Dashboard, Sales by [Dimension], Performance, Budgets, YTD Comparative, plus Sales Analysis' own Graph tab) are built on `v_dimension_monthly_sales`/`v_consolidated_sales`/`v_dimension_performance` — each pre-aggregated by at most one dimension, with no other dimension's code exposed as a column on the result. There was no way to ask any of them for "item sales, filtered to one customer" — the customer code simply isn't a column on that view's 'item' rows. Fully honouring Craig's ask meant two separate pieces of work: a new Supabase migration that closes that gap, and an app-wide place to hold "which filters are active right now" that survives navigating between screens.

**schema/011_wyzesales_global_filters.sql — the new migration.** Adds `v_sales_cube_monthly`, the same underlying per-line rollup `v_dimension_monthly_sales` already computes internally (schema/002's `base` CTE) but exposed as its own view with all 5 dimension codes attached to every row instead of being flattened into a `UNION ALL` of 5 separately-grouped queries. Three new SQL functions — `fn_dimension_monthly_sales_filtered`, `fn_consolidated_sales_filtered`, `fn_dimension_performance_filtered` — query that cube with whatever combination of the 5 dimension codes, fiscal year(s), and fiscal month a caller supplies, and group down to whichever single dimension that screen displays (or no dimension at all, for the whole-company case). Functions rather than more views, because a view's `GROUP BY` is fixed at creation time and there's no way to parameterize "group by whichever dimension the caller asks for" otherwise. None of them use `security definer`, so they run as the calling role (same as this project's existing `security_invoker` views) — RLS on `sales_document_facts` still applies per-caller through the exact same chain it always has, and no existing table's grants or policies needed to change. **This SQL file has not been run against Supabase yet — Craig needs to run it himself against wyzesales-staging, the same as every prior schema/NNN migration.**

**`SalesRepository`'s three rollup-fetch methods** (`fetchDimensionMonthlySales`, `fetchConsolidatedSales`, `fetchDimensionPerformance`) each gained an optional `filters` (`GlobalFilters`) parameter. When nothing in it needs the new machinery, the method goes straight to the same plain view query as before — no behaviour change, no extra round trip, for every call site that hasn't been touched. The moment any cross-dimension filter (or, for the two monthly-rollup methods, the global Month filter) is active, the query routes to the matching new RPC function instead. Both paths return the exact same row shape, so `DimensionMonthlySales`/`ConsolidatedSales`/`DimensionPerformance`'s existing `fromMap` needed no changes at all. `fetchSalesDocuments` (the line-level method, unchanged in shape) gained a `fiscalMonth` parameter that translates to a calendar `doc_date` range via a new `calendarMonthStartFor()` helper in `fiscal.dart`, since `v_sales_documents` has no `fiscal_month` label column of its own to filter on directly.

**`GlobalFilters` (new `core/filters/global_filters.dart`) is the app-wide state.** A plain immutable class holding one optional `FilterSelection` (code + display label, so a chip never needs a second network call just to show a name) per dimension, plus an optional fiscal year and fiscal month. Held in a `StateNotifierProvider` — the same Riverpod shape `SessionNotifier`/`ThemeModeNotifier` already use in this app — rather than any screen's local `State`, so the identical instance survives `go_router` navigation between screens. `GlobalFiltersNotifier` listens to `AuthRepository.onAuthStateChange` and resets to empty on sign-out, exactly mirroring `SessionNotifier`'s own pattern: filters live purely in memory for the length of the signed-in session, are never written to local storage or Supabase, and the next login always starts clean, matching Craig's "session only" decision directly.

**The filter bar (new `shared/widgets/global_filter_bar.dart`) is how "what's applied" stays visible.** Mounted once in `AppShell`, below the top bar, so the exact same strip shows on every screen — a chip per active filter (dimension name + entity label, or "Year: FY2026"/"Month: Jun"), each with its own delete (×), a "+ Add filter" menu that opens a searchable entity picker (reusing `ReferenceDataRepository.entitiesFor`, the same source `BudgetsScreen`'s own entity list already reads) for the 5 dimensions or a simple list picker for Year/Month, and a "Clear all" once more than one filter is active. Hidden via a new `AppShell.showGlobalFilters` parameter (default `true`) on the two screens that aren't sales-data screens at all — Settings and Platform Admin.

**How each screen actually applies the filters — this is the part most worth Craig double-checking, since it involved real judgment calls, not just plumbing:**

Sales Analysis' Table tab, Quote Analysis, and Sales Order Analysis (all three share `document_analysis_view.dart`) needed the least new machinery, since `v_sales_documents` already carried every dimension. Their existing Category/Item/Sales Person/Branch/Customer dropdowns and Year dropdown now read from and write to `globalFiltersProvider` directly instead of local `State` — pick a customer here, it's the same "Customer" chip that shows up on the Dashboard. A new Month dropdown was added (no prior equivalent on this screen), defaulting to "Any."

The Dashboard, Sales by [Dimension], Performance, and Sales Analysis' Graph tab / YTD Comparative all apply the 5 dimension filters as genuine cross-filters now — pick Customer = Acme Corp anywhere, and the Dashboard's "Top 5 Item" pie, the Sales by Item table, and the Performance screen's Item figures all narrow to Acme Corp's item sales specifically, which was structurally impossible before this migration. A dimension filter that happens to match a screen's own display dimension (e.g. viewing Sales by Customer with a global Customer filter active) isn't special-cased away — it just narrows the table to that one entity's row, the plain, explainable consequence of filtering and grouping by the same field.

Year and Month needed a genuine per-screen judgment call, since "Year"/"Month" don't mean the same thing on every screen:

Performance already had its own Year and Month dropdowns, so those are now the SAME state as the global filter (two-way bound) — no separate copy exists anymore. Sales Analysis' Table tab / Quote Analysis / Sales Order Analysis got a real Year and Month filter for the same reason (a line-level detail table has an obvious, unambiguous "one fiscal month" meaning).

Sales by [Dimension] and YTD Comparative / Sales Analysis' Graph tab apply the global Month filter — it restricts every column's sum down to that one fiscal month, so "FY2026" becomes "FY2026, that month" rather than the full year, still compared side by side against the other fiscal years — but deliberately do NOT apply the global Year filter. Both screens exist specifically to show a fixed trailing-3-fiscal-year comparison computed from today; forcing them down to a single year would remove the entire reason to open them. Flagged rather than silently ignored — if this isn't the behaviour Craig wants (e.g. "Year should instead pick which 3 years the window ends on"), it's a small, well-scoped follow-up.

The Dashboard applies neither Year nor Month — its MTD/YTD pies and KPI row are already "as of right now," relative to `DateTime.now()`, and a global Year/Month filter has no well-defined meaning layered on top of that without redefining what "today" means for the whole screen. Only the 5 dimension filters apply there.

Budgets is the one screen this migration only partially wires up, and that's a real data limitation, not an oversight: `budget_figures`/`sales_forecast` are keyed by exactly one dimension + entity_code + fiscal_month, with no other dimension's code recorded against a budget row at all — there is no data for "this customer's budget, filtered by item" to exist in the first place, cube view or not. What Budgets DOES do now: arriving on a dimension that has a matching global filter (e.g. Budgets — Sales Person with a global Sales Person filter active) auto-selects that entity in the left-hand list instead of leaving it unselected. A filter for any OTHER dimension is silently inapplicable here — no banner or warning added yet, since the filter bar itself (visible on this screen like every other) already shows what's active; worth adding an explicit "not applicable to Budgets" note if that reads as confusing in practice.

**Files changed**: `docs/schema/011_wyzesales_global_filters.sql` (new — not yet run against Supabase), `core/constants/fiscal.dart` (new `calendarMonthStartFor` helper), `core/filters/global_filters.dart` (new), `shared/widgets/global_filter_bar.dart` (new), `shared/widgets/app_shell.dart` (mounts the filter bar, new `showGlobalFilters` param), `data/repositories/sales_repository.dart` (`filters` param on 3 methods, `fiscalMonth` param on `fetchSalesDocuments`), `shared/widgets/document_analysis_view.dart` (dropdowns bound to global state, new Month dropdown), `features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart`, `features/dimension_templates/screens/sales_by_screen.dart`, `features/dimension_templates/screens/performance_screen.dart`, `features/ytd_comparative/screens/ytd_comparative_screen.dart`, `features/sales_analysis/screens/sales_analysis_screen.dart` (Graph tab), `features/dimension_templates/screens/budgets_screen.dart`, `features/settings/screens/settings_screen.dart` + `features/admin/screens/platform_admin_screen.dart` (`showGlobalFilters: false`).

**Verification**: all 14 touched/new Dart files parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check; the SQL migration was checked by hand against schema/001's and schema/002's actual view/table shapes (column names, join keys, RLS model) rather than run anywhere, since this sandbox has no live Postgres to test against. **Three things genuinely need Craig's own environment before this is done**: running `011_wyzesales_global_filters.sql` against wyzesales-staging (nothing above works without it — every RPC call will 404 until it's applied), a real `flutter analyze`/build (this is the largest single change this session has made — a new provider, a new shared widget, and edits across eight existing screens — and cross-file plumbing like this is exactly where a real compiler catches things tree-sitter structurally cannot), and a visual/behavioural check that a filter set on one screen actually shows up and narrows the data on another, which is the entire point of the feature and not something any static check can confirm.

### 18a. Branch filter not applying — the refetch trigger was a race, not a query bug (2026-08-26)

Craig, with 4 screenshots of Sales Analysis' Table tab: "It is not working. When I select a branch nothing is filtered but when I clear the filter then it filters." Selecting Branch = Durban left the table showing all 448 unfiltered rows (with the chip and the local dropdown both correctly showing "Durban" selected); clearing the filter back to "All" then showed 149 rows, all Durban — i.e. the fetch that should have run the moment Durban was selected only actually landed on screen one action later, after clearing.

**Root cause.** Every screen wired into the global filters (`document_analysis_view.dart`, the Dashboard, Sales by [Dimension], Performance, YTD Comparative, and Sales Analysis' own Graph tab) used the same hand-rolled trigger: compare `ref.watch(globalFiltersProvider)` against a `_lastFilters` field saved on the `State`, and if it changed, schedule the actual refetch via `WidgetsBinding.instance.addPostFrameCallback`. That extra frame of indirection is exactly where a second filter change (e.g. selecting Durban, then quickly clearing) can overtake the first — the callback scheduled for the first change can still be sitting in the queue by the time the second change's own `build()` has already run and moved `_lastFilters` on, so the refetch that finally executes doesn't line up with what's on screen at that moment. The query logic itself (`SalesRepository.fetchSalesDocuments`'s `.eq('branch_code', ...)` chain) was never the problem — it was always reading the current filter state correctly whenever it actually ran.

**The fix.** Replaced the `_lastFilters`-diff-plus-`addPostFrameCallback` pattern with Riverpod's own `ref.listen`, which exists specifically to run a side effect exactly once, immediately, whenever a watched provider's value changes — no extra frame, no hand-maintained "did it change" field to fall out of sync. Each of the 6 screens now does `ref.listen<GlobalFilters>(globalFiltersProvider, (previous, next) => _refetch());` (or the screen's equivalent reload method) directly in `build()`, and the `_lastFilters`/`_lastGlobalFilters` fields were removed entirely — nothing is left to race. `document_analysis_view.dart` and `performance_screen.dart` still separately call `ref.watch(globalFiltersProvider)` alongside the `ref.listen`, since those two also render the current filter values directly (dropdown selections); the other four don't need the value at all in `build()`, so the `ref.watch` there was dropped along with the field.

**Files changed**: `shared/widgets/document_analysis_view.dart`, `features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart`, `features/dimension_templates/screens/sales_by_screen.dart`, `features/dimension_templates/screens/performance_screen.dart`, `features/ytd_comparative/screens/ytd_comparative_screen.dart`, `features/sales_analysis/screens/sales_analysis_screen.dart` (Graph tab).

**Verification**: all 6 files parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check; `ref.listen` is Riverpod's documented primitive for exactly this "run a side effect when a watched provider changes" case, so this isn't a speculative workaround — it removes the specific race the manual pattern was exposed to. Still needs Craig's own `flutter analyze`/build and, most importantly, an actual re-test of the Branch filter (and worth spot-checking a couple of the other 5 dimension filters too, since they all shared the same broken trigger).

## 19. Punch-list batch — search, elastic filters, totals rows, formatting, Dashboard target chart (2026-08-26)

Craig's full punch list, actioned item by item below. Four items were clarified up front (AskUserQuestion): "Dimension selection must apply to entire dashboard" is already done — no work needed; the new Dashboard chart is two bar-pairs, MTD and YTD; "the timing wheel stops turning" means the loading spinner shown on every screen while data is in flight; and each table's totals row recomputes derived figures (percentages, variances) from the totalled base numbers rather than averaging the row-level derived values.

### 19a. Thousand separators everywhere

**The ask**: "All numbers must have a thousand separator." `formatters.dart`'s three formatters (`_randFormat`, `_randFormatPrecise`, `_qtyFormat`) were already built on `intl`'s `NumberFormat.currency`/`NumberFormat.decimalPattern`, which do grouping automatically — but they were constructed with `locale: 'en_ZA'`, and `intl`'s `en_ZA` locale data groups by thousands using a **space**, not a comma (South African convention), which reads as "no separator at all" at a glance. Switched all three to `locale: 'en_US'`, which uses the comma grouping Craig's asking for, while leaving the `R` currency symbol and decimal-place rules untouched (those come from the format pattern, not the locale's grouping character).

**Files changed**: `core/utils/formatters.dart`.

### 19b. Elastic (server-side) search on every entity filter

**The ask**: "Elastic search on filters" (Sales Analysis, Quote Analysis, Sales Order Analysis, Sales by, Performance).

**What was actually broken**: `ReferenceDataRepository`'s `branches()`/`salesReps()`/`customers()`/`categories()`/`items()` methods already accepted an optional `{String? search}` parameter that does a server-side `.ilike('name', '%$search%')` — but nothing in the UI ever passed it. Both `FilterBar` (the per-screen dropdowns on Sales/Quote/Sales Order Analysis) and `global_filter_bar.dart`'s "Add Filter" entity picker loaded the **entire** list once with no search term, then filtered it client-side as the user typed. `customers()` and `items()` are capped at 200 rows when queried without a search term (to avoid pulling the whole table on every screen load) — so any customer or item alphabetically past the first 200 was silently unreachable through the filter UI no matter what was typed, with no error or indication anything was missing.

**The fix**: a new shared widget, `entity_search_field.dart` (`EntitySearchField` + `showEntitySearchDialog()`), that debounces keystrokes by 300ms and re-queries `ReferenceDataRepository` with the typed text as the `search` parameter on every debounced change — so the search actually runs against the full table server-side instead of a stale, capped, client-held list. Both `FilterBar` and `global_filter_bar.dart`'s picker were rewritten to use it, so this one fix covers every screen: the per-screen dropdowns on the three document-analysis screens, and the global "Add Filter" dialog that Sales By and Performance rely on (those two have no filter controls of their own beyond the global bar).

**Files changed**: `shared/widgets/entity_search_field.dart` (new), `shared/widgets/filter_bar.dart` (rewritten to take `FilterSelection?`/`CodeName?` per field instead of raw strings), `shared/widgets/global_filter_bar.dart` (`_EntityPickerDialog` removed, routed through `showEntitySearchDialog`), `shared/widgets/document_analysis_view.dart` (call site update).

### 19c. Document search + filter

**The ask**: "Include Document in Top Bar search" (General) and "Include filter for Month and Document" (Sales/Quote/Sales Order Analysis) — Month was already covered by the existing global Month filter, so this item is specifically about Document.

Added `ReferenceDataRepository.searchDocuments(query)`, which does an `.ilike('document', '%$query%')` against `v_sales_documents` (capped at 6 results, deduplicated by document+kind) and returns a new `DocumentSearchResult` (document number, document kind). The top bar search (`top_bar_search.dart`) now runs this alongside the existing dimension search and merges the results, tagging each document hit with a route pre-resolved from its kind (`quote` → Quote Analysis, `sales_order` → Sales Order Analysis, everything else → Sales Analysis) plus a new `?document=<number>` query parameter. Each of the three document-analysis screens gained an `initialDocument` constructor parameter (read from `state.uri.queryParameters['document']` in the router) and a Document text field of their own in `document_analysis_view.dart`, so the same field serves both "search from the top bar and land pre-filtered" and "filter by document number while already on the screen."

**Files changed**: `data/models/reference_data.dart` (new `DocumentSearchResult`), `data/repositories/reference_data_repository.dart` (new `searchDocuments`), `shared/widgets/top_bar_search.dart` (rewritten), `shared/widgets/document_analysis_view.dart` (`initialDocumentSearch` param, Document field, `_filterByDocument`), `features/sales_analysis/screens/sales_analysis_screen.dart`, `features/quote_analysis/screens/quote_analysis_screen.dart`, `features/sales_order_analysis/screens/sales_order_analysis_screen.dart` (`initialDocument` param, passed through), `core/router/app_router.dart` (reads the new query param on those 3 routes).

### 19d. Timing wheel freezing during load

**The ask**: "The timing wheel stops turning / updating" — confirmed to mean the loading spinner on any screen while its data is in flight.

**Root cause**: `CircularProgressIndicator` drives its own spin via an implicit `AnimationController`, but it still sits inside whatever widget tree surrounds it. Any unrelated rebuild upstream — a sibling's `setState`, a filter chip re-laying-out, anything in the same repaint chain — forces the spinner through a relayout/repaint pass of its own alongside that ancestor change, which visibly stutters or freezes the animation even though the controller itself never stopped ticking. `async_section.dart` is the one shared loading widget nearly every screen in the app uses, so this one fix reaches everywhere.

**Fix**: wrapped the spinner in a `RepaintBoundary`, isolating it into its own compositing layer so ancestor rebuilds no longer force it through a relayout/repaint of its own.

**Files changed**: `shared/widgets/async_section.dart`. (The Dashboard's two smaller inline spinners — the dimension-breakdown reload indicator and the dimension-data placeholder — got the same `RepaintBoundary` treatment while touching that file for the Sales Target chart below, since they're the same widget doing the same thing.)

### 19e. Column totals row on every table

**The ask**: "Can we have total for each column in each table."

Added a bold totals `DataRow`, appended last (after sorting) to every data table in the app: the document table shared by Sales/Quote/Sales Order Analysis, Sales By, Performance, YTD Comparative, and the Budgets month table. Per Craig's clarification, each totals row **recomputes** derived figures from the summed base numbers rather than summing or averaging the row-level derived values — e.g. a GP% total is `sum(profit) / sum(value) * 100`, not an average of each row's own GP%, and YTD Comparative's "vs FY" variance total is computed from each fiscal year's full-year total, not an average of 12 monthly variances (which would weight a quiet month the same as a blockbuster one). Performance's %Contribution column is the one exception, summed directly rather than recomputed, since contributions are additive shares by construction and averaging/recomputing would be wrong there. Budgets' totals row sums the *saved* month values (`widget.data.budget`/`forecast`), not the live, unsaved text-field contents — the table doesn't rebuild on keystroke (only on save), so a total built from the controllers would show a stale figure indefinitely rather than genuinely updating live.

**Files changed**: `shared/widgets/document_analysis_view.dart`, `features/dimension_templates/screens/sales_by_screen.dart`, `features/dimension_templates/screens/performance_screen.dart`, `features/ytd_comparative/screens/ytd_comparative_screen.dart`, `features/dimension_templates/screens/budgets_screen.dart`.

### 19f. Sales Analysis: default to Table tab

**The ask**: "Toggle Table / Chart defaults to Table." Changed `sales_analysis_screen.dart`'s `DefaultTabController` from defaulting to index 0 (Graph) to `initialIndex: 1` (Table).

**Files changed**: `features/sales_analysis/screens/sales_analysis_screen.dart`.

### 19g. Rounded trend line

**The ask**: "Format line chart to be rounded and not pointy" (Sales Analysis' Graph tab). The line was drawn as straight `lineTo` segments between points, giving a sharp angle at every data point. Replaced with a Catmull-Rom-to-cubic-Bezier conversion (tension 1/6) that draws a smooth curve through every point exactly (rather than a simpler quadratic-midpoint smoothing, which only passes *near* each point) — each contiguous run of points in a series gets one smooth `Path` built from `cubicTo` segments instead of straight lines.

**Files changed**: `shared/widgets/trend_line_chart.dart`.

### 19h. Dashboard: Actual Revenue vs Sales Target replaces Quick Actions

**The ask**: "Remove Quick actions and replace with Actual Revenue vs Sales Target as a bar chart," clarified as two bar-pairs — MTD and YTD.

The Quick Actions tile row (Sales Analysis / YTD Comparative / Quote Analysis / Sales Order Analysis / Budgets shortcuts) was removed outright rather than relocated — all five destinations are already reachable from the app shell's nav drawer, so it was a pure duplicate shortcut, not a unique navigation path.

In its place: a new dependency-free `ActualVsTargetChart` widget (same "no third-party charting package" reasoning as `SimpleBarChart`/`TrendLineChart` — this sandbox can't verify a charting package's API actually compiles), rendering one Actual/Target bar pair per group. The Dashboard feeds it two groups, MTD and YTD.

**Actual** comes from `v_consolidated_sales` (the same whole-company source Sales Analysis' Graph tab uses). **Target** comes from `budget_figures` filtered to `dimension = 'company'` — a value already allowed by that table's schema CHECK constraint (`schema/001`) alongside the 5 per-entity dimensions, but not yet wired into any screen; it exists specifically for a whole-company target with no per-Branch/Customer/etc breakdown, which is exactly this chart's use case. `budget_figures` has no `fiscal_year` column (it's keyed by `fiscal_month` label only and reused every year), so Target rows are matched to Actual months via a new `fiscalMonthLabelFor(DateTime)` helper — the reverse of the existing `calendarMonthStartFor`.

**Deliberate scope decision**: both Actual and Target on this chart are whole-company and **not** filtered by the 5 global dimension filters, unlike the KPI tiles and pie charts above it on the same screen (which do respect them). A `dimension = 'company'` budget row has no per-Branch/Customer/etc breakdown to filter against — filtering only the Actual side while Target stayed whole-company would produce a mismatched, misleading comparison (e.g. a filtered Actual that's naturally smaller than an always-unfiltered Target, with no way to tell that's why). This is called out in a code comment on `_TargetChartData` so it doesn't read as an oversight later.

**Files changed**: `shared/widgets/actual_vs_target_chart.dart` (new), `core/constants/fiscal.dart` (new `fiscalMonthLabelFor` helper), `features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart` (`_QuickActions` class removed, new `_TargetChartData`/`_loadTargetChart`, chart wired in and refreshed alongside the KPI row).

**Verification (all of 19a-19h)**: every touched/new file (21 in total) parses clean with the tree-sitter Dart check. As with every batch this engagement, this is a syntax check, not a compiler — the three things that still need Craig's own environment are a real `flutter analyze`/build (this batch touches or creates 21 files, including two new shared widgets used from multiple screens), a manual click-through of each item above against the actual data (especially the elastic search against real customer/item counts past 200, and the Sales Target chart once a `dimension = 'company'` row actually exists in `budget_figures` — until one is entered, the chart will correctly show a Target of zero, not an error), and confirming the Document search/filter resolves to the right screen for a few real quote/sales-order/invoice numbers.

## 20. Global search becomes a real filter; filter-box sizing and duplicate controls cleaned up (2026-08-27)

Craig, after clicking through the punch-list batch, sent two screenshots — Sales Analysis' Table tab (Year/Month/Document/Category/Item/Sales Person/Branch filter row) and the Sales By screen (its "No filters applied · Add filter" strip, with a "Showing Sarah Naidoo pinned to the top" banner from a top-bar search) — with two follow-up requests.

### 20a. Top-bar search now sets a real global filter instead of "pinning to the top"

**The ask**: "Can we treat this the same way as the filters. I.e. If I do a global search on Sarah, it show me sales person Sarah. If I then select her it should then filter all of the data and only show me data for sales person Sarah. Exactly the same way as if I filtered on Sales Person Sarah. This will then also populate the filters applied on the top and we can then eliminate the line 'Showing Sarah Naidoo pinned to the top'."

Picking a dimension result from the top-bar search used to navigate to `/sales-by/:dimension?highlight=<code>` — the same "pin this entity to the top of the table, don't filter anything else out" destination the Dashboard's pie-chart drill-down uses. That's a different, weaker guarantee than an actual filter: the rest of the dimension was still visible, nothing else in the app was narrowed, and it didn't survive navigating away. `top_bar_search.dart`'s `_selectResult` now calls `globalFiltersProvider.notifier.setDimension(dimension, FilterSelection(entity.code, entity.displayLabel))` directly — the exact same call `global_filter_bar.dart`'s "Add filter" picker makes — and does **not** navigate anywhere, matching the fact that setting a filter from "Add filter" doesn't navigate either; it just re-filters whatever screen is already open. The result: picking "Sarah Naidoo — Sales Person" from the search bar now shows up as a "Sales Person: Sarah Naidoo" chip in GlobalFilterBar, filters every screen the same way manually adding that filter would, and stays applied while navigating — indistinguishable from having set it by hand. Document results are unaffected (still navigate, via `?document=`), since a document number has no global-filter equivalent to set.

The old highlight/pin mechanism (`SalesByScreen.highlightCode`, the `_HighlightBanner`, `?highlight=` on the route) is left in place for the Dashboard's pie-chart drill-down, which is a genuinely different feature (Craig separately asked for that one to pre-sort Sales By to match whichever Top 5/Bottom 5/Diminishing 5/Growth 5 chart was clicked, which a plain filter can't express) — only the top-bar search's own behaviour changed.

**Files changed**: `shared/widgets/top_bar_search.dart` (`_TopBarResult` now carries `dimension`/`entity` instead of a route for dimension matches; `_selectResult` branches on that), `features/dimension_templates/screens/sales_by_screen.dart` (doc comments only — `highlightCode` is now pie-chart-drill-down-only).

### 20b. Filter-box sizing, consistency, and a genuine duplicate

**The ask**: "Can you check the sizing and consistancy of all of the filter boxes across the application. Some of them are inconsistent with the others and are double labelled. Prefer to keep them as small as reasonably possible."

**The "double labelled" part** turned out to be a real duplicate, not a cosmetic one. Sales Analysis, Quote Analysis, and Sales Order Analysis each had their own inline Category/Item/Sales Person/Branch/Customer boxes (`FilterBar`, built during the "Elastic search on filters" work in Section 19b) sitting directly below `GlobalFilterBar` — which AppShell already mounts above the body of every screen, including these three. Both read and wrote the exact same `globalFiltersProvider` state, so a Sales Person filter was editable — and, once set, *displayed* — in two different boxes on the same screen at once, one showing "Sales Person: Sarah Naidoo" as a chip up top and an identical "Sales Person" box with "Sarah Naidoo" inside it further down. Unlike Year/Month (which show a real default — "today's" fiscal year/month — that GlobalFilterBar's chip-only display can't convey until a value is explicitly set), Category/Item/Sales Person/Branch/Customer had no such default: both boxes showed "All" when unset, so the inline copy added nothing. `FilterBar` was removed from `document_analysis_view.dart` outright — those five filters are now set and viewed in exactly one place, GlobalFilterBar, on every screen including these three. The now-orphaned `FilterBar` widget (`filter_bar.dart`) and the `EntitySearchField` boxed-widget class (`entity_search_field.dart`) were removed as dead code; the server-backed search dialog they both opened (`showEntitySearchDialog`) is unaffected and is now called directly by GlobalFilterBar's "Add filter" picker only.

**The "inconsistent" part** was sizing: dimension-switcher boxes (the "pick a dimension" dropdown on Sales By, Performance, Budgets, and the Dashboard's breakdown picker) ranged from 100px to 260px wide with no shared logic behind any particular number, and Performance's own inline Year/Month boxes had no label above them while Sales/Quote/Sales Order Analysis' equivalent Year/Month boxes did (a leftover from when they were built at different times, not a deliberate difference). Standardized to a small set of consistent widths app-wide, per Craig's "keep them as small as reasonably possible": every dimension-switcher/selector box (Sales By, Performance, Dashboard's breakdown and rank-mode pickers, GlobalFilterBar's "Add filter") is now 160px; Year is 110px and Month is 100px everywhere either appears (both already fit "FY2027"/a 3-letter month label comfortably, since Performance's copies were already that size); the Document field shrank from 180px to 140px (with its hint shortened from "Doc number…" to "Doc #…" so it still reads cleanly at the smaller width); and Performance's Year/Month boxes gained the same "Year"/"Month" labels their Sales/Quote/Sales Order Analysis counterparts already had. Budgets' own dimension dropdown (236px) was left as-is — it lives inside a fixed-width master-list sidebar rather than a horizontal filter row, a different enough layout that forcing it to match risked leaving an odd gap for no real benefit.

**Files changed**: `shared/widgets/document_analysis_view.dart` (`FilterBar` removed, Year/Month/Document resized), `shared/widgets/filter_bar.dart` (emptied to a pointer comment — nothing imports it any more), `shared/widgets/entity_search_field.dart` (`EntitySearchField` class removed, `showEntitySearchDialog` kept), `features/dimension_templates/screens/performance_screen.dart` (dimension-switcher resized, Year/Month labeled and left at their existing size), `features/dimension_templates/screens/sales_by_screen.dart` (dimension-switcher resized), `features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart` (breakdown/rank-mode dropdowns resized), `shared/widgets/global_filter_bar.dart` ("Add filter" resized).

**Verification**: all 8 touched files parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check; grepped the whole `lib/` tree afterward to confirm nothing still references the removed `FilterBar`/`EntitySearchField` widgets. As always, needs Craig's own `flutter analyze`/build and a visual pass — box widths and wrapped-row reflow are exactly the kind of thing this sandbox's syntax-only check can't confirm actually looks right.
