Quote Analysis: drop Year, Month, Document. Sales Order Analysis: drop Year, Month, Document. Performance: drop Year, Month. Then make the bar charts on the Dashboard horizontal and replace grey colour with the blue colour. Change Graph to Chart on Sales Analysis and turn Chart and Table into a toggle conforming with the others ones in the app."

Section 20b kept Year/Month boxes inline on Sales/Quote/Sales Order Analysis and Performance as one deliberate exception — they showed a real default (today's fiscal year/month) that GlobalFilterBar's chip-only display couldn't convey until a value was explicitly set. Craig's read on that tradeoff: consistency and "as small as reasonably possible" wins over showing the default. This section removes that exception entirely, so every filter in the app — all 5 dimensions, Year, Month, and now Document — lives in exactly one place.

### 21a. Document promoted to a real global filter

**The ask**: "We need to add Document to the Filters dropdown."

Document had been a screen-local text field on `document_analysis_view.dart` (Section 19c) — deliberately kept out of `GlobalFilters` at the time because a document number "felt like a one-off lookup rather than something to keep pinned while navigating." Craig's ask here reverses that: Document is now a first-class field on `GlobalFilters` (`final String? document`), with a `setDocument`/`clearDimension`-equivalent method on the notifier, counted in `isEmpty`/`activeCount` like every other filter, and cleared on sign-out same as the rest.

`GlobalFilterBar`'s "Add filter" dropdown gained a "Document" entry alongside the 5 dimensions and Year/Month. Picking it opens a new `_DocumentSearchDialog` — same debounced (300ms), server-backed search pattern as the entity search dialog (`entity_search_field.dart`), but built separately rather than folded into that shared dialog: it calls `ReferenceDataRepository.searchDocuments` instead of `entitiesFor`, and returns a plain `String` (a document number has no separate code/label pair to carry). Unlike the entity dialog, there's no "browse everything" list before typing — `searchDocuments` itself returns nothing for an empty query — so this one shows a "Type a document number to search" prompt on open instead of an empty list. A picked document shows up as a "Document: <number>" chip like every other filter, removable the same way.

`document_analysis_view.dart` (the shared table behind Sales/Quote/Sales Order Analysis) dropped its own Document text field and now reads `filters.document` from `globalFiltersProvider` directly, filtering the already-loaded rows client-side exactly as the old local field did — only where the value comes from changed. The top-bar search's Document results (Section 19c) now do both: set `GlobalFilters.document` (the same call GlobalFilterBar's own picker makes) *and* still navigate to the matching analysis screen, since — unlike the 5 dimensions — a document number only means anything on those three screens; setting the global filter alone wouldn't land you there. The `?document=<number>` query parameter this used to ride in on is gone: the three route builders (`/sales-analysis`, `/quote-analysis`, `/sales-order-analysis`) went back to plain `const XScreen()`, and each screen's `initialDocument` constructor parameter was removed.

## 22. Server-side pagination for the line-level Document Analysis tables; Totals moved to the top app-wide (2026-08-27)

Craig, watching Sales Analysis' Table tab load 448 lines in one shot: "What happens when there a significant number of lines? e.g. Sales Analysis now has 448 lines. What happens when there 4000 lines? What is considered the norm in apps like this?"

**What was actually happening**: `SalesRepository.fetchSalesDocuments` fetched up to 1000 rows in a single query (newest first), with no pagination and nothing on screen indicating a filtered set past 1000 lines would silently lose everything older than the 1000th row. On top of the row cap, the table itself was Flutter's `DataTable`, which builds every row's widgets eagerly (unlike a lazily-built `ListView.builder`) — a genuine performance cost that scales directly with row count, independent of the 1000-row cap. Both the Document substring filter and the Totals row were computed client-side against whatever happened to already be in memory, which only ever worked because every row used to be fetched at once.

**The norm**, per the (already-delivered) explanation: real server-side pagination (a page at a time, not a single capped fetch), sorting pushed to the query rather than done client-side on a partial data set, a visible "Showing X of Y" indicator, and — separately — a Totals figure that reflects the true filtered result set regardless of pagination, the way Xero/QuickBooks-style report grids handle it. Craig confirmed: "This is what we need to do."

### 22a. Server-side pagination (schema/012)

Two new Postgres functions, additive only (no changes to `v_sales_documents` or any existing view/function):

- **`fn_sales_documents_page`** — one page of line-level rows (`LIMIT`/`OFFSET`, newest first), taking the same filter parameters `fetchSalesDocuments` already built from `GlobalFilters`, plus the `document` substring filter moved here from client-side. Returns the exact same column shape as `v_sales_documents`, so `SalesDocument.fromMap` needed no changes.
- **`fn_sales_documents_totals`** — `COUNT`/`SUM(quantity/value/profit)` over EVERY row matching those same filters, ignoring `LIMIT`/`OFFSET` entirely. This is the full-filtered-result-set aggregate the Totals row and the "Showing X-Y of Z" indicator are both sourced from.

Both are `language sql`, no `security definer` — same reasoning as schema/011's `fn_*_filtered` functions: they run as the calling role, so RLS on `sales_document_facts` still applies per-caller through `v_sales_documents` exactly as it does on a plain view query today.

`SalesRepository.fetchSalesDocuments` was replaced by `fetchSalesDocumentsPage` (one page, via `fn_sales_documents_page`) and `fetchSalesDocumentsTotals` (the aggregate, via `fn_sales_documents_totals`) — both called through `.rpc(name, params: {...})`, the same client-call shape already proven to compile in this exact file for schema/011's functions, rather than reaching for newer PostgREST client APIs (`.range()`/`.count()`) this codebase had never exercised before and couldn't verify against the real package here.

`document_analysis_view.dart` was rewritten around a fixed 100-row page size: `_pageFuture` (the current page, refetched on every page turn AND every filter change) and a separately-tracked `_totals`/`_totalsLoading`/`_totalsError` trio (refetched ONLY on a filter change, mirroring the Dashboard's existing `_dimensionData`/`_dimensionLoading`/`_dimensionError` pattern for exactly the same reason — a different refresh cadence than the main future) — so turning pages stays cheap instead of re-running the full aggregate query every click. A new `_PaginationBar` shows "Showing 1–100 of 4,238" with Previous/Next icon buttons, Next disabled once a page comes back short of a full page or (once the totals aggregate resolves) once the current page's last row would already be at or past the true total. A filter change resets back to page 0, since the page index the user was on may no longer exist against a narrower (or wider) result set.

**Column-header sorting on this one table is temporarily gone, not silently left broken.** The old sort re-ordered whatever page's rows happened to already be in memory — correct only because every row used to be fetched at once. With real pagination, sorting has to happen in the same query that produces the page (`ORDER BY` the requested column) to mean anything across pages; sorting just the visible page would quietly reintroduce the exact same "looks right, isn't" problem this whole change exists to fix. Safely parameterizing which column to sort by inside a plain `language sql` function needs either a fixed `CASE`-per-column expression or validated dynamic SQL — flagged as a real, separate follow-up rather than rushed into this migration. Rows are fixed to newest-first (`doc_date` descending), matching the SQL function's own `ORDER BY` and this table's original default order.

**Files changed**: `docs/schema/012_wyzesales_sales_documents_pagination.sql` (new), `data/repositories/sales_repository.dart` (`fetchSalesDocuments` replaced by `fetchSalesDocumentsPage`/`fetchSalesDocumentsTotals`), `data/models/sales_document.dart` (new `SalesDocumentTotals`), `shared/widgets/document_analysis_view.dart` (rewritten: pagination, server-side Document filter, `_PaginationBar`, column-sort removed from `_DocumentTable`, which is now a plain `StatelessWidget`).

### 22b. Totals moved to the top — everywhere, not just the paginated tables

**The ask**: "In addition what about the totals? Does it make sense to have the Totals as the first line in a view? Will the Total only be for the displayed lines or for all lines regardless or wether or not they are presented in the view?"

Two decisions, both confirmed by Craig before implementing:

**Scope — the full filtered result set, never just the current page.** A page-local sum would be actively misleading rather than merely incomplete once pagination is real: paging from page 1 to page 2 would visibly change the total shown, which reads as a bug even though it'd technically be correct for that page alone. `document_analysis_view.dart`'s Totals row now sources its figures from `fn_sales_documents_totals` (22a above) — the full-filtered-set aggregate — never from `rows` (whichever page happens to be on screen). GP% is still recomputed from the totalled Revenue/GP rather than averaged, unchanged from Section 19e's original rule.

**Position — first line, not last, and app-wide for consistency.** With pagination, "last row of the table" stops being a well-defined place — there's no single last row on screen if the total spans pages you haven't turned to yet. Pinning it to the top instead makes it visible immediately on landing on the screen and unambiguous that it covers the whole filtered set rather than whatever's below it. Craig confirmed this should apply consistently across the app, not only the newly-paginated tables, so the Totals row also moved to the top of Sales By, Performance, and YTD Comparative — none of which are paginated (each is a bounded rollup: one row per entity or per fiscal month, not per transaction, so none had the truncation problem 22a fixes) — purely for visual consistency with the tables that now are. Budgets' own totals row was NOT included in this pass — Craig's question named Sales By, Performance, and YTD Comparative specifically, not Budgets; worth a follow-up ask if he wants it made consistent too.

**Files changed**: `shared/widgets/document_analysis_view.dart` (totals sourced from the new aggregate, see 22a), `features/dimension_templates/screens/sales_by_screen.dart`, `features/dimension_templates/screens/performance_screen.dart`, `features/ytd_comparative/screens/ytd_comparative_screen.dart` (totals row moved from the end of the `rows` list to the start; no change to what each totals row computes, only where it sits).

## 23. Sticky headers/Totals app-wide; server-side column sorting restored; First/Last page controls (2026-08-27)

Craig, same day as Section 22, immediately after it shipped: "1. Please can we lock the Headers and Totals so we don't lose them when scrolling down. 2. The column sorting is a real issue we need to be able to sort on all columns. 3. Can we have a control the goes to the first page and one that goes to the last page to avoid having to go back or forwards only 1 page at a time." All three items are covered here.

### 23a. Column sorting (schema/013)

Section 22a deliberately left column-header sorting out of the paginated Document Analysis table, flagging it as "a real, separate follow-up" rather than an oversight — sorting a single page in memory only ever worked because every row used to be fetched at once, and doing it correctly once pagination is real needs the `ORDER BY` pushed into the query itself. This is that follow-up.

`fn_sales_documents_page` (schema/012) is rebuilt in schema/013 as `language plpgsql` with validated dynamic SQL, replacing the plain `language sql` version — a plain SQL function has no way to make `ORDER BY`'s target itself a bind parameter, and casting every sortable column to a common text key so a single `CASE` expression could pick between them would break numeric ordering (text-sorting "9" after "10"). The safe pattern instead: `p_sort_column` is matched against a fixed `CASE`/`WHEN` list of hardcoded column expressions — never the raw parameter itself — to build `v_sort_expr`, which is the only thing substituted into the query via `format()`'s `%s`; every actual filter value still goes through a genuine parameterized `USING` clause. There is no path from user input to a raw SQL fragment: `p_sort_column` can only ever select one of twelve fixed expressions, matching this table's twelve columns exactly (`document`, `document_kind`, `doc_date`, `sales_person`, `branch`, `category`, `item`, `customer`, `quantity`, `value`, `profit`, `profit_percent`) — "sales_person"/"branch"/"category"/"item"/"customer" sort by the same coalesced display name/code the table already shows in that column, not silently by a different underlying code.

Because Postgres treats a changed parameter list as a different function identity, schema/013 `drop`s the old 11-parameter `fn_sales_documents_page` before creating the new 13-parameter version (added `p_sort_column text default 'doc_date'`, `p_sort_ascending boolean default false`), rather than `create or replace` — this guarantees exactly one version of the function exists afterwards regardless of whether schema/012 was ever actually applied. `fn_sales_documents_totals` is untouched; an aggregate over the whole filtered set has no sort order to parameterize.

On the Flutter side, `SalesRepository.fetchSalesDocumentsPage` gained `sortColumn`/`sortAscending` parameters (defaults matching the SQL function's own defaults), and `document_analysis_view.dart`'s `_DocumentTable` is sortable again: a new `sortColumnKeys` list (index-aligned with its 12 `DataColumn`s, and with schema/013's `CASE`/`WHEN` list — the two must stay in step) maps a clicked column back to the key the RPC expects. Clicking a header now triggers a real re-fetch of page 0 under the new order, not a re-sort of whatever page is already on screen.

**Follow-up bug, same day, after Craig applied schema/013**: Sales Analysis' Table tab failed with `PostgrestException(... structure of query does not match function result type ... Returned type document_kind does not match expected type text in column 1.)`. This is the SAME enum-vs-text issue schema/012's header comment already flagged once (`document_kind` is a Postgres ENUM, not `text`) — but that first fix only ever touched the WHERE clause; the SELECT list itself had selected the bare, uncast `v.document_kind` since schema/012, while `returns table (document_kind text, ...)` declares that column `text`. This went unnoticed through both schema/012 (which never got far enough to run — its WHERE-clause bug was a parse-time error) and schema/013 (which fixed the WHERE clause but carried the SELECT-list bug over unchanged), only surfacing now that the function could finally execute for real. **schema/014** fixes it: `v.document_kind::text` in the SELECT list, matching what the WHERE clause and the sort CASE expression already did. Same 13-parameter signature as schema/013, so this is a plain `create or replace`, not a drop+create.

### 23b. First/Last page controls

`_PaginationBar` gained First (`Icons.first_page`) and Last (`Icons.last_page`) buttons alongside the existing Previous/Next, so jumping across a large result set doesn't mean clicking Next dozens of times. Last is disabled until the totals aggregate resolves (the true last-page index isn't known before then) and once already on the last page; First is disabled on page 0 — the same "null the callback rather than leave it enabled and do nothing" pattern Previous/Next already used.

**Files changed**: `docs/schema/013_wyzesales_sales_documents_sort.sql` (new), `docs/schema/014_wyzesales_sales_documents_page_cast_fix.sql` (new, same-day follow-up fix), `data/repositories/sales_repository.dart` (`fetchSalesDocumentsPage` gained `sortColumn`/`sortAscending`), `shared/widgets/document_analysis_view.dart` (`_DocumentTable` sortable again with `sortColumnKeys`, `_PaginationBar` gained First/Last).

### 23c. Sticky headers and Totals — every table, not just the paginated ones

Flutter's stock `DataTable` has no native support for a frozen header row or a frozen "totals" row while the body scrolls — a real gap, not a small tweak. Two real ways to close it were put to Craig: a hand-rolled rewrite of `ResponsiveDataTable` (more work, but stays inside code this project can actually verify compiles, and fixes every table in the app at once) versus adopting a third-party package like `data_table_2` (less work, but a new dependency this sandbox has no way to check against — no pub.dev access, no local Flutter SDK — the same risk this project already steered away from once with `fl_chart`). Craig chose the hand-rolled rewrite.

`ResponsiveDataTable` no longer renders a single `DataTable` when it needs a sticky header. Instead it builds TWO `DataTable` instances from identical inputs (same `columns`, same `rows`, same `columnSpacing`, same fixed row heights) — which is what guarantees their column widths land pixel-identical without measuring or hard-coding any column width by hand, since `DataTable`'s column sizing is a pure function of those inputs. The first instance is clipped down to just the header (plus a caller-specified number of leading "pinned" rows — the Totals row, where a screen has one) and never scrolls. The second is the real scrolling body, shifted up by exactly the pinned block's height so its own copy of the header/pinned rows sits off-screen above the visible area, and clipped so that hidden copy is never visible or interactive. `headingRowHeight`/`dataRowMinHeight`/`dataRowMaxHeight` are now pinned to fixed values (matching `DataTable`'s own historical defaults) rather than left implicit, because the pixel math behind the split depends on knowing those heights exactly.

A new `pinnedRowCount` parameter tells `ResponsiveDataTable` how many leading rows to freeze — set to 1 on Document Analysis, Sales By, Performance, and YTD Comparative (all four have their Totals row as `rows[0]`, per Section 22b), 0 everywhere else (header-only sticky). **Budgets is the one exception**: its table cells are live `TextField`s each bound to a per-month `TextEditingController`, and the two-`DataTable` technique above requires mounting the same row content into two `DataTable`s at once — safe for plain `Text` cells, unsupported for a shared `TextEditingController` mounted into two `TextField`s simultaneously. Budgets passes a new `stickyHeader: false` flag and keeps its original single-`DataTable` rendering entirely unchanged; it does not get a frozen header from this change.

**Files changed**: `shared/widgets/responsive_data_table.dart` (rewritten: `stickyHeader` and `pinnedRowCount` params, two-`DataTable` pinned-header split), `shared/widgets/document_analysis_view.dart`, `features/dimension_templates/screens/sales_by_screen.dart`, `features/dimension_templates/screens/performance_screen.dart`, `features/ytd_comparative/screens/ytd_comparative_screen.dart` (all four pass `pinnedRowCount: 1`, or `1`/`0` conditionally on Performance where the Totals row itself is conditional), `features/dimension_templates/screens/budgets_screen.dart` (`stickyHeader: false`, otherwise untouched).

**Same-day follow-up: DISABLED after a real crash, then re-enabled with a targeted fix.** Craig hit a real, blocking bug on Sales Analysis' Table tab: a repeating `Assertion failed: .../mouse_tracker.dart:199:12`, with the table failing to render at all — this is exactly the "not visually confirmed on a real device yet" caveat every layout fix this session has carried, materializing for real on the single most structurally involved change made to this widget. `stickyHeader`'s default was flipped from `true` to `false` in `responsive_data_table.dart` to restore a known-working app immediately, with no other file touched — sorting (23a/23b) and pagination (22a) were unaffected throughout, since neither depends on the sticky-header split. Craig confirmed the app was stable again on that revert.

**Second attempt, same day, after Craig confirmed the revert was stable.** Without a stack trace to diagnose against (this sandbox has no way to run Flutter), the most likely cause was reasoned out from the design itself: the hidden, permanently-clipped-away copy of the header — built into the scrolling body instance purely so its column widths match the visible pinned copy — still carried real `onSort` callbacks it could never actually be clicked through. `DataTable` only wraps a column header in a live, hoverable `InkWell`/`MouseRegion` when it has `onSort` set, so this meant two simultaneously-mounted sort-arrow regions for every sortable column, one of them invisible — the most plausible source of a `MouseTracker` assertion. The fix: `_buildTable` gained an `interactive` flag that strips `onSort` (and so the `InkWell`/`MouseRegion`) from that hidden copy entirely, plus the same hidden copy is now also wrapped in `IgnorePointer` as a second, low-cost layer of the same fix (it excludes that whole shifted-off-screen subtree from hit-testing without touching the ancestor `SingleChildScrollView` that actually captures scroll gestures). `stickyHeader` defaults back to `true`; this was Craig's explicit choice between retrying this targeted fix versus switching to the `data_table_2` package, having already seen one hand-rolled attempt fail on a real device.

**Third attempt, same day.** The `onSort`/`IgnorePointer` fix above stopped the `mouse_tracker` crash, but Craig immediately hit a second, DIFFERENT crash on the same screen — this time with a full stack trace to work from: `RenderConstrainedOverflowBox object was given an infinite size during layout`, pointing squarely at this file's `OverflowBox`es. Root cause: both left `minWidth`/`maxWidth` unset (meaning "inherit the incoming width constraint"), and the incoming width there is genuinely unbounded — this whole table sits inside the horizontally-scrolling `SingleChildScrollView` in `build()`, which deliberately gives its child unbounded width so a wide table can size itself past the viewport and scroll sideways. A bare `DataTable` tolerates that fine (it always reports its own finite intrinsic width regardless of how much space is "available"), but `OverflowBox`'s own reported size tracks the incoming constraint's max — an unbounded max made it try to BE infinitely wide, which a `RenderBox` may never do. Fix: both `OverflowBox`es now get an explicit `minWidth`/`maxWidth` of a newly-tracked `_tableWidth` — a concrete, finite number derived in `_scheduleMeasurement` (the larger of the table's natural width and the viewport width — the same number a bare `DataTable` would already have sized itself to at this point), falling back to the viewport width for the one frame before that measurement resolves.

That was a third, still-unverified-on-a-real-device attempt at the same feature, having crashed twice in two different ways already. Craig tested it and confirmed it was STILL throwing errors — a third distinct failure of the same hand-rolled mechanism.

### 23d. Switched to the `data_table_2` package

Three real, different crashes from one hand-rolled mechanism — `mouse_tracker` assertion, then an infinite-size `OverflowBox`, then a third failure on the same fix — is a pattern, not bad luck, and this sandbox's complete inability to run Flutter (every one of those three attempts shipped unverified until Craig ran it) made it clear that continuing to iterate blind on custom clip/transform layout code was the wrong way to spend Craig's testing time. Craig asked directly: "What is the norm? How do other similar apps overcome this header/total issue?" The honest answer: production apps essentially never hand-roll a frozen header over raw table primitives — they use a purpose-built grid component that's already had this exact problem solved and tested against real devices (AG Grid and MUI's DataGrid are the equivalent choice on the web). Craig confirmed `data_table_2` has no cost (free, BSD-3-Clause, no paid tier) before choosing to switch rather than take a fourth blind swing at the hand-rolled version.

`data_table_2` is a documented in-place substitute for `DataTable`/`PaginatedDataTable` (its own README: "add '2' to the names of stock widgets... and that is it") — every existing `DataColumn` (including its `onSort` callback) and `DataRow`/`DataCell` across every screen is used completely unchanged; only `shared/widgets/responsive_data_table.dart` itself changed. `ResponsiveDataTable` now wraps `DataTable2` directly, keeping its exact prior public API (`columns`, `rows`, `sortColumnIndex`, `sortAscending`, `scrollsVertically`, `stickyHeader`, `pinnedRowCount`) so none of the five call sites (Document Analysis, Sales By, Performance, YTD Comparative, Budgets) needed to change at all. `pinnedRowCount`/`stickyHeader` now map onto `DataTable2`'s own native `fixedTopRows` (`fixedTopRows: stickyHeader ? 1 + pinnedRowCount : 0` — `fixedTopRows` counts the header itself, so its own default of `1` already matches this widget's old "always at least the header" baseline) instead of this file's own deleted clip/transform mechanism — the actual pinning behavior is now `data_table_2`'s, battle-tested by its own users, not a mechanism unique to this codebase that only Craig's device could ever actually exercise.

The other thing this deletes: the old two-pass `GlobalKey`-based width measurement that grew `columnSpacing` to eliminate blank space after the last column (Section 17's original fix). `data_table_2` already spreads plain `DataColumn`s evenly across the available width on its own — no measurement needed for that anymore. What's still needed is a floor under that stretching, so a many-column table on a narrow window doesn't just keep squeezing every column thinner instead of switching to horizontal scroll (the original, explicit ask). `ResponsiveDataTable._minWidth` supplies that floor as a plain, static estimate per column (based on its label's text length — "Sales Person" gets more room than "Qty" — clamped to a sane range), fed into `DataTable2.minWidth`. This is deliberately rough, deliberately not a runtime measurement of anything, and has no `GlobalKey`/render-tree dependency to get wrong — if a table looks too tight or too loose once Craig sees it, that's a quick, low-risk visual tweak, nothing like the three render-tree crashes the previous approach produced.

Budgets keeps `stickyHeader: false`, but the REASON changed: the old reason (mounting the same `TextField`-bound row content into two simultaneously-live `DataTable`s at once was unsafe) no longer applies — `data_table_2` renders the row list once, not twice, so that specific hazard is gone. Budgets stays unpinned because its Totals row deliberately stays at the bottom (Section 22b: Budgets was never included in the "Totals to the top" scope), not because of any remaining technical constraint — a frozen header alone would be safe to add there today if Craig ever wants it, flagged as an easy follow-up rather than applied unasked.

**New dependency**: `data_table_2` in `pubspec.yaml`. Originally pinned to `^2.8.0`, deliberately below the newer `3.0.0` (published only ~10 days before this was added, and a fresh major-version bump that pulls in a new `material_ui` package this project has no reason to take on). That still broke: `flutter pub get` failed — Craig's installed Dart is `3.12.1`, and `2.8.0` (like `3.0.0`) needs Dart `>=3.13.0`. Re-pinned to **`^2.7.2`** rather than asking Craig to upgrade Flutter for this — `2.7.2` only needs Dart `>=3.0.0`/Flutter `>=3.27.0` (comfortably below what he's already running), has no `material_ui` dependency either, and already had `fixedTopRows` (added in `2.3.0`, per the package's own changelog) well before this version — everything this migration actually uses. Flutter's own error message suggested this exact downgrade (`flutter pub add data_table_2:^2.7.2`), which is a reassuring, independent confirmation this was the right fix rather than a guess.

**Files changed**: `shared/widgets/responsive_data_table.dart` (fully rewritten — now a thin, stateless wrapper around `DataTable2`; the entire hand-rolled clip/transform/`OverflowBox`/`IgnorePointer`/two-pass-measurement mechanism from the three prior attempts is deleted), `pubspec.yaml` (`data_table_2: ^2.8.0` added), `features/dimension_templates/screens/budgets_screen.dart` (comment only — explains why `stickyHeader: false` still applies, for a different reason than before; no behavior change).

**Verification**: all Dart files touched across items 1–3 (and this switch) parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check; the new SQL migrations (23a, plus the schema/014 cast fix) were checked by eye against schema/001's column list and schema/002's `fiscal_month_label` helper, but — like every SQL migration this engagement — have NOT been run against a real Postgres/Supabase instance from this sandbox; Craig needs `012`, `013`, then `014` applied in that order. `data_table_2` itself is a real, unverified-in-this-sandbox new dependency — the same category of risk this project has flagged before (`fl_chart`) — but unlike a hand-rolled mechanism, it's one other developers have already exercised against real devices, which is the entire reason for switching to it after three straight hand-rolled failures. **Confirmed**: `flutter pub get` resolved cleanly against `^2.7.2` (`+ data_table_2 2.7.2`), and `flutter analyze` reported "No issues found!" — the first real Dart analyzer run this migration has had, independent of this sandbox's own tree-sitter-only syntax check. Craig confirmed the click-through: "it is working correctly now." Sections 22, 23a, 23b, and 23c/23d — pagination, sorting, First/Last, and the frozen header/Totals row — are all done and confirmed working on a real device, closing out Craig's original three-item request ("1. lock the Headers and Totals... 2. column sorting... 3. First/Last page controls") after the `data_table_2` switch.

## 24. Performance screen: Year/Month filters not applying live (2026-08-27, in progress)

Craig, with 4 screenshots of Performance — Sales Person: identical totals and per-salesperson figures whether "No filters applied" or "Year: FY2027, Month: Jul" was active. A follow-up screenshot with a genuinely different period (Year: FY2026, Month: Apr) showed the exact same numbers again — ruling out the first theory tried (that "no filter" was coincidentally already resolving to the current fiscal year/month, which does span the whole of FY2027 given `fiscalYearFor`'s Mar-start rule). The seed data (schema/010) generates real per-month randomized sales figures, and `budget_figures`/`R Target` is month-scoped, so three different Year/Month combinations landing on byte-identical Value/Target/Profit figures cannot be coincidence.

Craig's next report narrowed it further: **"If I select a year and month in Sales Analysis and then go to Performance the filters works but if I clear the filter on performance it does not clear and if I select year and month in performance it doesn't filter."** That's the key data point — a FRESH mount of Performance (arriving from another screen with filters already set) correctly shows filtered data, but CHANGING or CLEARING a filter while already on Performance does nothing. Since `initState()`'s `_load()` and every in-place refetch both ultimately call the same `SalesRepository.fetchDimensionPerformance` with the same `.eq('fiscal_year', ...)`/`.eq('fiscal_month', ...)` query (verified correct by re-reading it, and `v_dimension_performance`'s definition in schema/002), the query/view logic itself was ruled out — this is specifically about the live refetch trigger not firing (or not taking effect) while mounted.

**The likely root cause, found by re-reading this project's own history.** Section 18a above (2026-08-26, the Branch filter race-condition fix) explicitly documents: *"`document_analysis_view.dart` and `performance_screen.dart` still separately call `ref.watch(globalFiltersProvider)` alongside the `ref.listen`, since those two also render the current filter values directly (dropdown selections)."* One day later, Section 21b ("Year/Month dropped from Performance") removed Performance's own inline Year/Month dropdowns — the thing that `ref.watch(globalFiltersProvider)` was feeding. The current file has no `ref.watch(globalFiltersProvider)` call left at all, only `ref.listen`. The most likely explanation: once the dropdowns were deleted, the `filters` local variable that fed them had nothing left reading it, `flutter analyze` would have flagged it as an unused variable, and the `ref.watch` line was removed along with the dropdowns — without anyone realizing, a year later in this file's own doc comment, that this line had been documented as doing double duty (rendering AND, apparently, something that made the paired `ref.listen` reliable for this specific screen). Whether `ref.listen` strictly requires a same-provider `ref.watch` to fire reliably isn't something this sandbox can verify against a real Riverpod runtime — but restoring the exact configuration this screen was originally verified with, at zero cost, is the safest fix available without a real device to test against.

**What changed**: `ref.watch(globalFiltersProvider);` (return value discarded — nothing in `build()` renders it since the dropdowns are gone, only the subscription/dependency registration matters) was added back into `build()`, directly below the existing `ref.listen`. Also fixed, while in this file: `didUpdateWidget`'s dimension-change branch reassigned `_future` without a `setState()` wrapper — the exact "pre-existing bug" `sales_by_screen.dart`'s own `didUpdateWidget` already had fixed ("so a dimension switch via the switcher dropdown didn't reliably repaint the AsyncSection into its loading state straight away") — Performance had the identical unwrapped reassignment and never got the matching fix; now wrapped in `setState()`.

**Belt-and-suspenders, in case the `ref.watch` restoration alone isn't the whole story**: a new `_loadGeneration` counter, bumped on every refetch and passed as `AsyncSection`'s `key`, forces a genuinely fresh `FutureBuilder` identity on every refetch rather than relying solely on `FutureBuilder` detecting that its `future:` parameter changed (which is supposed to be sufficient on its own, per Flutter's documented `FutureBuilder` behavior, but costs nothing to reinforce given this bug has already defied two rounds of static analysis).

**Temporary diagnostics — remove once confirmed fixed**: `debugPrint` calls in `_load()` (logs the dimension, effective Year/Month, and every raw filter value on every fetch, plus the row count once it completes) and `_refetch()` (logs that `ref.listen`'s callback actually fired). These are no-ops in a release build but visible in `flutter run`'s console. If the `ref.watch` restoration is the actual fix, Craig should see `_refetch()`'s log line appear the moment he changes or clears a filter on Performance, followed by `_load()`'s log line showing the new values, and the table should update to match. If `_refetch()` never logs at all even now, the bug is deeper in Riverpod's provider-listening mechanics than this specific regression, and the console output will make that immediately obvious rather than requiring another round of screenshots.

**Files changed**: `features/dimension_templates/screens/performance_screen.dart` (`ref.watch(globalFiltersProvider)` restored, `didUpdateWidget` `setState()` fix, `_loadGeneration`/`AsyncSection` key, temporary `debugPrint` diagnostics).

**Verification**: parses clean with the tree-sitter Dart check. Not yet verified against a real device — needs Craig's `flutter run` (not a release build, so the diagnostic `debugPrint` calls are visible) and a repeat of the exact steps that reproduced this: on Performance, apply a Year/Month combination different from whatever's currently showing, confirm the table updates, then clear it and confirm it reverts. Whatever the console shows should make it possible to say definitively whether this is fixed or whether the fault lies somewhere this sandbox's static reading genuinely couldn't reach.

## 25. Dashboard cosmetics + a real WyzeSales icon (2026-08-27)

Three quick items from Craig: drop the icon badges from the 4 Dashboard KPI tiles ("Cosmetics. Please remove the icons from the dashboard Sales mtd, Ytd, Gp mtd and ytd"), add a "% achieved" reading to the Actual Revenue vs Sales Target chart, and design a real WyzeSales icon in code to replace the long-standing placeholder (Section 6 originally flagged the outlined-circle-plus-`show_chart` mark as "to be designed later, not blocking").

**KPI tile icons removed.** `StatCard.icon` is now optional (`IconData?` instead of a required `IconData`) — when omitted, the tile collapses back to a plain label + big value + optional subtitle, no circular badge. The 4 Dashboard calls (Sales MTD/YTD, Gross Profit MTD/YTD) simply stop passing `icon:`. `StatCard` has no other call sites (`platform_admin_screen.dart`'s `_StatCard` is a separate, private class), so nothing else was affected.

**% achieved on the Target chart.** `ActualVsTargetChart` now shows a small pill next to each group's own label (MTD, YTD) reading "XX.X% achieved" — computed from that specific group's own actual/target, not the bars' drawn pixel widths (which scale against the shared cross-group max and would read wrong for whichever group isn't the larger one). Green at/above 100%, amber below, matching the positive/caution colour pairing used everywhere else in the app for over/under performance. Shows "—" instead of a division-by-zero when a period has no budget entered yet.

**A real icon.** Rather than design blind, four concepts were built as a quick SVG comparison board (published as a Cowork Artifact, not shipped as app code) so Craig could see them side by side on light and dark grounds and at real sidebar/favicon scale before anything was committed to Flutter: an "Ascending W" (bars whose top edges trace a W), a "Growth Ring" (a circle pierced by an upward trend arrow), a "Wyze Aperture" (an eye/lens with a trend-line iris — the only one that visualizes "Wyze" rather than "Sales"), and "Signal Spark" (a hex badge with a heartbeat-style pulse line). Craig asked to see the Ascending W inside a gold ring as a fifth variant, which was added to the same board; after comparing all five, he picked **Signal Spark**. Trade-off flagged and accepted: unlike the W-based concepts, Signal Spark doesn't reference "Wyze"/"Sales" on its own — it's meant to be read together with the wordmark it's always paired with, not standalone.

Implemented as `_SignalSparkPainter`, a `CustomPainter` in `shared/widgets/app_logo.dart` — a hexagon (solid navy fill on light grounds, white outline on the dark sidebar, since a filled hex would go flat/invisible against navy) with a 6-point pulse line through it in the brand gold. Coordinates are the exact same 0-72 design-grid numbers from the concept board Craig actually reviewed, scaled by `size.width / 72`, not a re-interpretation of them. No image asset, no icon font glyph — plain `Path` fills/strokes, so it stays crisp at both the ~32px sidebar mark and a favicon-sized rendering. `AppLogo`'s public API (`iconSize`, `fontSize`, `onDark`) is unchanged, so its two call sites (`login_screen.dart`, `app_shell.dart`'s sidebar) needed no edits.

**Files changed**: `shared/widgets/stat_card.dart` (`icon` made optional), `features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart` (drop `icon:` on the 4 KPI tiles), `shared/widgets/actual_vs_target_chart.dart` (`_AchievedBadge`), `shared/widgets/app_logo.dart` (rewritten icon mark — `_SignalSparkPainter`).

**Verification**: all 4 files parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check. Not yet confirmed on a real device/`flutter analyze` — worth a quick visual check that the hex badge renders correctly at both sidebar scale (dark, outline) and Login-screen scale (light, filled), since a `CustomPainter` is new territory for this codebase (every other visual in this app so far has been built from stock Material widgets) and this sandbox has no way to render it to confirm the proportions look right in practice.

## 26. Sidebar highlight missing on 4 screens; Sales Analysis line chart breaking on real zeros (2026-08-27)

Two small but real bugs, both found by re-reading the actual code rather than guessing: Craig noticed the sidebar only highlights the active item (name + icon turning gold) for some screens, not others, and that the Sales Analysis line chart's line disappears wherever a month has a 0 value instead of drawing through it.

**Sidebar highlight.** `_NavTile` in `app_shell.dart` only lights up when the `currentRoute` it's given matches (or, for the dimension-templated screens, starts with) the `AppShell.currentRoute` the current screen passed in — Section 6's rollout of this ("Not yet wired up on every screen — this first pass only updates the Dashboard") was apparently never finished. Checking every `AppShell(...)` call site turned up exactly 4 that never pass `currentRoute` at all: `sales_analysis_screen.dart`, `ytd_comparative_screen.dart`, `quote_analysis_screen.dart`, and `sales_order_analysis_screen.dart` — every other screen (Dashboard, Sales By, Performance, Budgets, Settings, Platform Admin) already had it. Fixed by adding the matching `currentRoute` to each (`/sales-analysis`, `/ytd-comparative`, `/quote-analysis`, `/sales-order-analysis` respectively) — plain, exact-match routes, no `activePrefix` needed since none of these four carry a dynamic path segment the way `/sales-by/:dimension` etc. do.

**Sales Analysis line chart breaking on real zeros.** `TrendLineChart` (its own doc comment) deliberately draws `null` as a gap in the line — meant for "this month hasn't happened yet" (a future month within the current, still-partial fiscal year). The bug: `_GraphTab._buildChart`'s `valueFor` returned `null` for ANY month with no matching row, not just future ones. `fetchConsolidatedSales` is a `GROUP BY` over the underlying invoice/credit_note facts — a month with genuinely zero sales activity never produces a row at all (there's nothing to sum), which is indistinguishable, by absence alone, from a future month that simply hasn't been invoiced yet. Both cases were being treated as the same "no data yet" gap, which is wrong for the first one — a completed month that really did total zero should plot as 0 and let the line carry through it, per Craig's ask.

Fixed by computing "today"'s fiscal month index (`fiscalMonthOrder.indexOf(fiscalMonthLabelFor(DateTime.now()))`) once per chart build, and only treating a missing row as a gap when it's a month of the current fiscal year (`_fiscalYears.last`, always the newest/partial one of the trailing 3) that's still AHEAD of that index — every other missing row (either of the two fully-elapsed prior fiscal years, or an already-elapsed month of the current one) now resolves to a real `0` instead of `null`.

**Files changed**: `shared/widgets/app_shell.dart` — no change needed, the bug was entirely in call sites; `features/sales_analysis/screens/sales_analysis_screen.dart` (`currentRoute` added, `valueFor` fixed), `features/ytd_comparative/screens/ytd_comparative_screen.dart`, `features/quote_analysis/screens/quote_analysis_screen.dart`, `features/sales_order_analysis/screens/sales_order_analysis_screen.dart` (`currentRoute` added to each).

**Verification**: all 4 files parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check. Not yet confirmed on a real device — worth Craig clicking through all 4 previously-unhighlighted sidebar entries to confirm they now light up, and re-checking the Sales Analysis Chart tab for a fiscal year/month combination known to have zero recorded sales to confirm the line now runs through it at 0 instead of gapping.

## 27. Dashboard KPI redesign — 6 tiles, 4 with MTD/YTD toggles (2026-08-27)

Craig asked "What do you think are the 5 kpi's that need to be on a sales analysis dashboard," was shown an HTML mockup (built with the `artifact-design` skill, matching this app's own `AppColors`/Roboto rather than a fresh look) before anything real changed, iterated on it twice — first "is it possible to have a toggle... but then the bar chart below gives this," then "Customer Concentration must default to year" plus a direct question about how MTD figures behave at the start of a new month — and finally said "Go ahead and build this please." This section is that build.

**The 6 tiles.** Revenue Target Attainment, Gross Profit Margin, Quote → Order Conversion, Top 5 Customer Concentration, and Rep Target Attainment replace the old 4-tile row (Sales MTD/YTD, GP MTD/YTD); those 4 aren't dropped, just folded into a 6th tile, "Revenue & Gross Profit" — one row, six tiles, matching the middle-path Craig confirmed over the mockup's "replace vs. second row" open question. Four of the six (GP Margin, Quote → Order Conversion, Customer Concentration, Rep Target Attainment) got a real MTD/YTD `SegmentedButton` toggle. Revenue Target Attainment deliberately did NOT — the Actual Revenue vs Sales Target chart directly below it already shows MTD and YTD permanently side by side (Section 25's `_AchievedBadge`), so a toggle on this tile would just re-answer a question the chart already answers a few pixels down; it shows the MTD % as its headline number with a YTD reading in the subtitle instead, pointing at the chart. Customer Concentration defaults to YTD per Craig's explicit instruction ("Customer Concentration must default to year"); every other toggled tile defaults to MTD.

**New shared widget**: `shared/widgets/toggle_stat_card.dart` — `ToggleStatCard`, a KPI tile that can flip between an MTD and a YTD reading of the same metric. Both periods' value/subtitle/color are computed once by the Dashboard (from data it already fetched) and handed to the widget together; flipping the toggle is a pure local `setState`, never a refetch — same philosophy as the existing `ValueGpToggle`. `showToggle: false` renders just the MTD side with no switch, used for Revenue Target Attainment. `stat_card.dart`/`StatCard` is now unused (its only call site was the old 4-tile row) but left in place rather than deleted, in case a future non-toggle tile wants it back.

**Quote → Order Conversion — how it's computed, and its real limitation.** `v_sales_documents` tags every line `quote`, `sales_order`, `invoice`, or `credit_note`, but there is no field anywhere in the schema linking a specific quote to the sales order it becomes. This metric is therefore a same-period count ratio — distinct quote documents raised in a period vs. distinct sales-order documents raised in that same period — not a matched per-quote conversion rate; a quote from one month that converts the next month is invisible to it. Flagged with a "†" on the tile and a footnote line printed under the whole KPI row so the caveat is visible wherever the number is, not just in this doc. Getting a true matched rate would need a schema change (a reference field captured at extract time), not attempted here.

New SQL needed for this one metric: `fn_document_counts` (`docs/schema/015_wyzesales_document_counts.sql`) — `count(distinct document)` grouped by `document_kind`, filtered the same way `fn_sales_documents_totals` (schema/012) already is. Existing `fn_sales_documents_totals` couldn't be reused: its `total_count` is `count(*)` over the LINE-level view, so a 3-line quote would count as 3, not 1. **This migration needs to be run against Supabase before Quote → Order Conversion will work** — everything else in this build reads from views/functions that already existed. New repository method: `SalesRepository.fetchDocumentCounts` (`data/repositories/sales_repository.dart`), returning a `Map<String, int>` keyed by document_kind, defaulting every requested kind to 0 (a kind with zero matching documents gets no row back at all from a plain SQL `group by`, which is expected right at the start of a new month).

**The other 3 new metrics needed no new SQL** — all built from existing repository methods:
- **Top 5 Customer Concentration**: `fetchDimensionMonthlySales(dimension: customer, ...)`, same call `_loadDimension`'s own breakdown already makes, just fetched independently of whichever dimension the breakdown picker currently shows. Top-5-by-value sum ÷ total-of-all-customers sum, for MTD and for the whole fiscal year to date.
- **Rep Target Attainment**: `fetchBudget(dimension: 'sales_person')` (the true rep roster + targets, since a rep with a target but zero sales in a month has no row in `v_dimension_performance` at all — that view is generated FROM the sales rollup, so it would silently under-count the "of N reps" denominator) cross-referenced against `fetchDimensionMonthlySales(dimension: sales_person, ...)` for actuals. A rep counts toward "at/above target" once their actual ≥ their target for the period; the denominator is every rep who has a target set for that period, whether they hit it or not. Deliberately whole-company, not filtered by the Dashboard's 5 dimension filters — same reasoning as the Actual vs Target chart (`_TargetChartData`'s doc comment): `budget_figures` has no per-Branch/Category/etc split on top of the rep themselves.
- **Gross Profit Margin** and **Revenue Target Attainment**: both fall straight out of data the KPI row already fetched (`salesMtd`/`profitMtd`/`salesYtd`/`profitYtd`, and a new whole-company Actual-vs-Target fetch shared with the chart below via a new `_fetchWholeCompanyTarget` helper — called twice per load/refresh on purpose, once for the KPI tile and once for the chart's own independent `AsyncSection`, trading one small extra query for not restructuring two already-working, independently-timed loaders).

**How MTD metrics behave at the start of a new month** (Craig asked directly): there's no special-cased "new month" logic anywhere, old or new — every MTD figure is just "sum of whatever's landed in `v_consolidated_sales`/the rollup views for the current calendar month, as of right now," the same way Sales MTD/GP MTD always worked. At 00:01 on the 1st, MTD is naturally small or zero and climbs through the day/month as the nightly extract lands real transactions — nothing resets or waits. The one thing that needed real handling: the four new metrics are ratios, and a ratio's denominator can genuinely be zero early in a month (no quotes raised yet, no sales yet). Added `ratioPercent()` to `core/utils/formatters.dart` — `numerator / denominator * 100`, returns `null` on a zero/missing denominator, same "let the UI render '—'" convention `variancePercent()` already established — so every new ratio tile shows "—" instead of a misleading 0% or a crash for the first little while of a month, then reports real numbers as soon as data exists. Rep Target Attainment isn't a ratio in that sense; it just honestly reads "0 of N" (or "—" if literally no rep has a target for the period) rather than pretending otherwise.

**Files changed**: `features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart` (rewritten KPI row + `_loadKpis`/`_KpiData`/new `_fetchWholeCompanyTarget`/`_WholeCompanyTarget`), `shared/widgets/toggle_stat_card.dart` (new), `data/repositories/sales_repository.dart` (`fetchDocumentCounts`), `core/utils/formatters.dart` (`ratioPercent`), `docs/schema/015_wyzesales_document_counts.sql` (new — **run this one in Supabase**).

**Verification**: all 4 changed/new Dart files and the SQL migration parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check; delivered and byte-verified (sha256) against both `/Users/craig/projects/wyzesales/...` and this doc's own two locations. Not yet confirmed on a real device or against `flutter analyze` — worth Craig running the schema/015 migration first, then reloading the Dashboard and checking: all 6 tiles render with sensible numbers, all 4 toggles flip cleanly between MTD/YTD with no flash of the wrong data, Customer Concentration opens on YTD, and the † footnote reads clearly under the row.

**Verification**: all 4 files parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check. Not yet confirmed on a real device — worth Craig clicking through all 4 previously-unhighlighted sidebar entries to confirm they now light up, and re-checking the Sales Analysis Chart tab for a fiscal year/month combination known to have zero recorded sales to confirm the line now runs through it at 0 instead of gapping.

## 28. Dashboard KPI tiles — three rounds of real-device feedback (2026-08-27/28)

Section 27's 6-tile build looked right in the mockup but needed three follow-up rounds once Craig actually ran it on a device — each round diagnosed from the specific symptom in his screenshots rather than guessed at, since this sandbox has no way to render Flutter and see the layout for itself.

**Round 1 — toggles too wide.** Screenshot showed the 6 tiles wrapping to 4-then-2 rows instead of the intended single row. Craig: "If we can make the toggles considerably smaller then maybe the tiles will all fit into the screen?" The `SegmentedButton` MTD/YTD toggle was wider than it looked like it should need to be — root cause turned out to be `showSelectedIcon`, which defaults to `true` and reserves visible width for a checkmark on the selected segment; that checkmark, not padding, was most of the extra width. Fixed by `showSelectedIcon: false` plus `SegmentedButton.styleFrom()` (not a raw `ButtonStyle`, so padding/minimumSize/textStyle/visualDensity all apply consistently through Material 3's resolved segment style) and the tile width nudged down (210px → 185px).

**Round 2 — truncated labels, uneven tile heights.** Two more screenshots, red arrows pointing at cut-off text: "Revenue Target Attain...", "Quote → Order Conversi...", "Top 5 Customer Concentrati...", and a truncated compound value "R 1,475,556 · R 4...". Craig: "Cannot truncate. Needs to resize accordingly. The tiles all need to be the same size as well." Two separate bugs, both in `toggle_stat_card.dart`: (1) every `Text` had `overflow: TextOverflow.ellipsis` with no `maxLines`, so anything past a single line got cut off rather than wrapping — fixed by adding `maxLines: 2` everywhere, keeping ellipsis only as a last-resort safety net past 2 lines; (2) `Wrap` doesn't equalize its children's cross-axis size the way `GridView`/`Row` would, so each tile was sizing itself off its own content, and Revenue Target Attainment's longer subtitle (full Rand actual/target figures plus a YTD percentage) made it visibly taller than the other 5 — fixed by wrapping every tile in an identical fixed `SizedBox(width: _kpiTileWidth, height: _kpiTileHeight)` and shortening that one subtitle down to a single short line pointing at the Actual vs Target chart below it. Craig signed off with "Not correct yet. We can continue tomorrow" pending this round's delivery.

**Round 3 — remove a redundant tile, halve the toggles, cut whitespace (2026-08-28).** Craig, next morning: *"Please can we remove the Revenue & Gross Profit tile as it is repetitive. In it's place can we insert this tile as per your suggestion: a returns/credit-note rate (credit note value as a % of gross invoiced value) as a quality signal. Then can we halve the size of the toggles. Remove the unnecessary white space on the tiles."*

- **Revenue & Gross Profit → Returns / Credit Note Rate.** The old 6th tile (Sales·GP MTD/YTD, folded from the pre-Section-27 layout) was dropped for being repetitive — Sales and Gross Profit are already visible elsewhere on the Dashboard (the KPI tiles' own underlying data feeds GP Margin, plus the Actual vs Target chart). In its place: credit note value as a % of gross invoiced value, for MTD and YTD, as a data-quality signal — high returns relative to sales is worth flagging. Computed as `ratioPercent(creditNoteTotal.abs(), invoiceTotal)` for each period. Credit notes are stored with a **negative** `value`/`quantity`/`cost` in `sales_document_facts` (confirmed against schema/010's seed-data INSERT statements, which generate credit-note rows as `-round(v_value * 0.3, 2)` etc.), so the credit note total needs `.abs()` before it means anything as a positive "% returned" figure. Unlike the other ratio tiles (higher is better), higher here is worse — `returnsRateColor()` flags anything above 3% as `AppColors.caution` rather than treating a high percentage as good.

  Deliberately reused the existing `fetchSalesDocumentsTotals` (schema/012's `fn_sales_documents_totals`) rather than writing a new SQL function — that function combines every `document_kind` passed to it into a single row (no `group by document_kind`), so it can't return an invoice/credit-note split in one call, meaning this tile costs 4 separate calls in `_loadKpis`'s `Future.wait` (invoice MTD, credit_note MTD, invoice YTD, credit_note YTD) instead of 2. Accepted that extra round-trip specifically to avoid handing Craig a second new migration to run in Supabase on top of schema/015 (Section 27), which he hadn't yet confirmed running as of this round.

- **Toggles halved.** In `toggle_stat_card.dart`: the `SegmentedButton`'s reserved height went 24px → 12px, `minimumSize` `Size(0, 24)` → `Size(0, 12)`, horizontal padding 8 → 4, and its text size 11sp → 9sp.

- **Whitespace removed.** Same file: the `Card`'s own padding went from 14/12 (horizontal/vertical) down to 10/6, and the gaps between label→toggle, toggle→value dropped from 8px to 4px, value→subtitle from 2px to 1px.

- **Tile footprint shrunk to match.** `dashboard_screen.dart`'s `_kpiTileWidth`/`_kpiTileHeight` dropped from 200×190 to 180×145 — safe now that Returns / Credit Note Rate (a short single-line percentage) replaced the one tile that ever needed a 2-line compound Rand value ("R 1,475,556 · R 469,790").

**Files changed this round**: `shared/widgets/toggle_stat_card.dart` (toggle dimensions, Card padding, inter-row spacing), `features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart` (`_KpiData` gained `grossInvoicedMtd`/`creditNoteMtd`/`grossInvoicedYtd`/`creditNoteYtd`, 4 new `fetchSalesDocumentsTotals` calls in `_loadKpis`, `returnsRateMtd`/`returnsRateYtd`/`returnsRateColor` in `build()`, the old "Revenue & Gross Profit" tile replaced with "Returns / Credit Note Rate", `_kpiTileWidth`/`_kpiTileHeight` reduced).

**Verification**: both files parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check; delivered and byte-verified (sha256) against `/Users/craig/projects/wyzesales/lib/...`. No new SQL migration this round — schema/015 (Section 27) remains the only outstanding one, still needed for Quote → Order Conversion. ## 30. Dashboard KPI tiles — Wrap replaced with a fixed 3x2 / 2x3 grid (2026-08-28)

Craig, after seeing 5 tiles land on one row and a lone 6th stranded on its own row below: "The standard layout of 4 tiles on top and then one below. Does it perhaps make sense to have two rows of three each?" Then, once told the trade-off (a rigid 3-column grid needs a reasonable minimum window width, whereas `Wrap` degrades more gracefully by just adding rows at narrow widths): "Yes please, let's try this."

**Why the old `Wrap` produced an uneven split.** `Wrap` fits as many fixed-width tiles as the available width allows onto a line before starting a new one — with 6 tiles at a fixed width, the resulting split (5+1, 4+2, 6, ...) was purely a function of whatever the window's current width happened to be, not a deliberate layout choice. That's how a single tile ended up stranded alone on its own row.

**The fix — `_KpiTileGrid`, a new private widget in `dashboard_screen.dart`.** It lays a fixed list of tiles into rows of `columns` each, wrapping to as many rows as needed. Since there are exactly 6 tiles and `columns` is always 3 or 2, that's always exactly 2 or 3 full rows — never a stray leftover. Each tile stretches to fill an equal share of its row's width via `Expanded` (wrapping a `SizedBox(height: _kpiTileHeight)` to keep the height fixed while the width flexes), rather than sitting at the old fixed 180px — that's what fills a wide monitor properly instead of leaving whitespace down the sides, and what lets a tile shrink gracefully on a narrower window instead of forcing a width that might not fit. `_kpiTileWidth` is gone entirely; only `_kpiTileHeight` (still 150, per Section 29) remains fixed.

**The breakpoint.** `_kpiGridBreakpoint = 900` — 3 columns above it, 2 columns at or below it. Deliberately reuses the *exact* threshold already used twice elsewhere in this app: `AppShell`'s own `_sidebarBreakpoint` (collapses the sidebar into a drawer below 900px) and this same Dashboard screen's rankings grid a few sections down (2 columns vs 1, also at 900px). One width decision for the whole app to reason about, rather than a third bespoke number specific to this row.

**Files changed**: `features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart` (`_kpiTileWidth` removed, `_kpiGridBreakpoint` added, new `_KpiTileGrid` widget, KPI row rebuilt on top of it instead of `Wrap`).

**Verification**: parses clean with the tree-sitter Dart check; delivered and byte-verified (sha256) against `/Users/craig/projects/wyzesales/lib/features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart`. Not yet confirmed on a real device — worth Craig checking both sides of the 900px breakpoint: a normal/wide window should show a clean 3-across, 2-row grid with tiles stretched to fill the row, and narrowing the window below 900px should reflow to 2-across, 3 rows without any tile getting stranded alone or clipped.

## 31. Dashboard KPI tiles — content centered instead of top-left anchored (2026-08-28)

Craig, after the grid switch (Section 30): "Getting there! The info is all bunched up in the top left corner of each tile leaving unnecessary white space to the right and at the bottom. Can we position the info in the tile better."

**Root cause.** `ToggleStatCard`'s internal layout (label/toggle/value/subtitle, left-aligned, top-anchored) was tuned back when tiles sat at a fixed, narrow 180px width — every pixel of that width was needed just to keep text from wrapping past 2 lines. Once Section 30 switched the KPI row to a grid where each tile stretches to fill an equal share of a much wider row, almost all of that same text now fits on a single line inside the wider tile — but the layout itself didn't change, so the (now-short) block of text just sat pinned in the top-left corner of a much roomier card, exactly as Craig's screenshot showed.

**Fix.** `ToggleStatCard`'s `Column` now centers on both axes (`mainAxisAlignment`/`crossAxisAlignment: center`) instead of defaulting to top/left, so the whole label-toggle-value-subtitle block sits in the middle of whatever height/width the tile actually has. `textAlign: TextAlign.center` was added to the label, value, and subtitle `Text` widgets too, so anything that DOES still wrap to 2 lines (a long label at the narrower 2-column/≤900px layout, say) stays centered rather than reverting to left-aligned lines inside an otherwise-centered block. Card padding grown back slightly (10/6 → 14/10) and the inter-row gaps widened (4/4/1 → 8/8/4) now that there's real room to give the content some breathing space instead of packing it as tightly as Section 29's crunch required.

**Files changed**: `shared/widgets/toggle_stat_card.dart` (Column alignment, Text `textAlign`, padding/gap values).

**Verification**: parses clean with the tree-sitter Dart check; delivered and byte-verified (sha256) against `/Users/craig/projects/wyzesales/lib/shared/widgets/toggle_stat_card.dart`. Not yet confirmed on a real device — worth Craig checking that each tile's content now reads as a centered block with balanced margins on every side, rather than pinned to one corner, at both the 3-column and 2-column (≤900px) layouts.

## 32. Revenue Target Attainment gets its toggle back; sign-in errors trapped (2026-08-28)

Two unrelated items from Craig in one message.

**Revenue Target Attainment toggle.** "I know we agreed not to but for conformance purposes can we build in the MTD/YTD toggle on the Revenue Attainment tile as well." This tile was the deliberate exception among the 6 (Section 27): the Actual vs Target chart directly below it already shows MTD and YTD permanently side by side, so a toggle on the tile itself just re-answers a question the chart already answers a few pixels down. That reasoning hasn't changed — but matching the other 5 tiles mattered more to Craig than avoiding that slight redundancy, so the toggle is back. `revenueAttainmentColor` (previously a single value computed off MTD only) became a function of whichever percent is passed, same pattern as every other tile's color function, and the tile now supplies `ytdValue`/`ytdColor`/`ytdSubtitle` alongside the existing MTD ones. Subtitles on both sides stay short ("of MTD/YTD target — full detail below") for the same reason as before: the full Rand actual/target figures already live in the chart underneath, and repeating them here is what made this tile taller than the other 5 back in Section 26's truncation fix.

**Sign-in error wording.** A screenshot of the real login screen showing, verbatim: `Sign-in failed: AuthApiException(message: Invalid login credentials, statusCode: 400, code: invalid_credentials)`. `login_screen.dart`'s catch block was interpolating the raw exception object into the error text (`'Sign-in failed: $error'`), which is exactly what `Object.toString()` produces for any uncaught exception type — never intended to be user-facing wording.

Added `_friendlySignInError()`, switching on `error.code` (Supabase's own docs name this as the stable, machine-readable field to match on — `message` is free text for logs/developers and isn't guaranteed to stay worded consistently across Supabase releases). Covers `invalid_credentials` (the one actually hit — "Incorrect email or password," deliberately not saying which one is wrong, so a sign-in attempt can't be used to probe which email addresses have accounts), `email_not_confirmed`, `user_banned`, `user_not_found` (folded into the same "incorrect email or password" wording as `invalid_credentials`, for the same no-enumeration reason), and `over_request_rate_limit` — the other codes Supabase's docs list as reachable from a password sign-in attempt. Anything else (a non-`AuthException` error, or an `AuthException` with an unrecognized/absent code) falls through to a generic but still-readable message rather than ever showing the exception object again.

**Files changed**: `features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart` (`revenueAttainmentColor` become a function, Revenue Target Attainment tile gets `ytdValue`/`ytdColor`/`ytdSubtitle`), `shared/widgets/toggle_stat_card.dart` (class doc comment updated — no tile uses `showToggle: false` any more, though the option stays available), `features/auth/screens/login_screen.dart` (`_friendlySignInError()` added, `supabase_flutter` imported for `AuthException`).

Please try again." instead of the raw exception text.

## 34. Platform Admin & Settings — client/company editing, user editing, annual_price recalculation (2026-08-28)

Craig sent a batch of five requirements in one message, referencing the sibling SeaWyze codebase throughout:

```
Platform Admin:
Clients:
- Requirement to edit a client. Same as Seawyze
Licenses:
- Annual price needs to be recalculated on SAVE
Pricing:
- Annual price needs to be recalculated on SAVE
Settings:
Company:
- Requirement to edit a Company. Same as Seawyze with all of the fields as per Seawyze but without the Company Documents function.
Users:
- Requirement to Add, Edit and Delete Users. Same as Seawyze.
License:
- Fed from Platform Admin. Needs to be updated / recalculated each time the Annual Price is Saved.
```

SeaWyze's own equivalent screens (`platform_admin_screen.dart`, `settings_screen.dart`) were read to port the exact behavior rather than guess at it.

**The annual_price bug.** `license.annual_price` is a plain stored numeric column with no database trigger behind it — nothing recalculated it automatically. Reading WyzeSales' own `_EditLicenseDialog`/`_EditPlanDialog` save handlers confirmed neither one ever touched it: License-edit only wrote `max_users`/`discount_percent`/`status`, and Pricing-edit only called `createPricingPlan`/`updatePricingPlan`. So a license's stored annual price went stale the moment its seat count, discount, or its plan's rates changed — exactly the two symptoms Craig named.

Fixed with one shared formula rather than SeaWyze's own two separate copies of the math: `PricingPlan.annualPriceForSeats(seats, discountPercent)` = `monthlyForSeats(seats) * (1 - discountPercent/100) * 12`, reusing the existing `monthlyForSeats` rather than re-deriving base+additional-users arithmetic a second time. `_EditLicenseDialog._save()` now writes `annual_price` using this formula whenever the license has a plan attached (a planless license has nothing to compute from, so it's left as-is). `_EditPlanDialog._save()`, on an existing plan's save, now also calls a new `PlatformAdminRepository.recalculateLicensesForPlan(plan)`, which re-fetches every license on that plan and rewrites each one's `annual_price` at the plan's just-saved rates.

**The cascade is deliberately unconditional** — a license with its own `discount_percent` still gets recalculated, at *its* discount against the plan's new rates, not skipped or frozen. This matches both SeaWyze's actual behavior (confirmed: SeaWyze's plan-save cascades to every license on the plan with no override flag in existence) and WyzeSales' own original multi-tenancy design decision that `discount_percent` is a percentage off the plan-derived total specifically so a negotiated rate keeps tracking the plan, not a frozen absolute number.

**Client editing — two different-sized dialogs, matching SeaWyze's own split.** SeaWyze itself has two separate edit dialogs for a client/company: Platform Admin's is a small 3-field dialog (name, contact email, contact number) via a direct Supabase call; Settings' is a much fuller dialog covering most of `CompanyModel`. WyzeSales now mirrors that split:

- **Platform Admin > Clients** gets `_EditClientDialog` (`platform_admin_screen.dart`) — the same 3 fields as SeaWyze's Platform Admin dialog, saved via the already-existing `PlatformAdminRepository.updateClient`. A new edit icon on `_ClientRow` opens it; `_ClientsTabState` now reloads the list on close the same way `_LicensesTab`/`_PricingTab` already did.
- **Settings > Company** gets `_EditCompanyDialog` (`settings_screen.dart`), replacing the old name-only `_RenameClientDialog` entirely — full field set: name, contact name, contact number, contact email, address lines 1–3, city, country, postal code. Deliberately includes address2/address3/postal_code even though SeaWyze's *own* Settings dialog doesn't expose those either: "all of the fields as per Seawyze" is read here as a request for full field parity with SeaWyze's `CompanyModel`, not an instruction to also copy SeaWyze's dialog's own incompleteness. No Documents URL field and no "Company Documents" card — Craig was explicit that function is out of scope.

New migration **`schema/016_wyzesales_client_profile_fields.sql`** adds the nine new columns (`contact_name`, `contact_number`, `contact_email`, `address1`, `address2`, `address3`, `city`, `country`, `postal_code`) to `clients`. No RLS or grant changes needed — `clients_adminuser_update` (schema/008) is a row-level policy with no column list, and the table-level `grant update, insert on clients to authenticated` already covers any column added here. **This migration still needs to be run in Supabase before either edit dialog will work** — unlike some earlier migrations, this one is a plain `alter table add column`, so it is not safe to run twice (a second run errors with "column already exists"), so please run it exactly once.

A new `Client.fromMap`/model gained all nine fields as nullable — a client created before schema/016, or one whose admin hasn't filled them in yet, renders them as "—" rather than crashing. `SettingsRepository.updateCompanyProfile(clientId, data)` replaced the old `updateClientName(clientId, name)` — this is the one call-site change that mattered: `_RenameClientDialog` was deleted along with it, since it was the only caller of the removed method.

**Settings > Users — the missing Edit leg.** Add and Delete already existed; only Edit was missing. New `_EditUserDialog` (`settings_screen.dart`), wired into `_UserRow` via a new edit icon next to the existing pause/resume button: full name, contact number, level dropdown (User/RegUser/Admin), rep code, branch code — saved via the already-existing generic `SettingsRepository.updateUser(id, data)`, no repository change needed. Deliberately does **not** include an Active toggle the way SeaWyze's own equivalent dialog does: `_UserRow` already has a dedicated pause/resume icon button doing exactly that, so duplicating it in the dialog would just be two controls for the same thing. Email and password are also left out, matching SeaWyze (neither is editable there either, and WyzeSales has no change-email/reset-password flow to wire either field to).

**Settings > License tab already met the "fed from Platform Admin, reflects the recalculation" requirement with no code change** — `_LicenseTabState.initState()` already calls `getLicense(clientId)` fresh on every mount, so once Platform Admin recalculates and persists `annual_price`, the next time a client's own Settings > License tab is opened it reads the new value straight from the database. Confirmed by reading the existing code rather than assumed.

**Files changed**: `lib/data/models/client.dart` (nine new nullable fields), `lib/data/models/pricing_plan.dart` (`annualPriceForSeats` added), `lib/data/repositories/platform_admin_repository.dart` (`recalculateLicensesForPlan` added), `lib/data/repositories/settings_repository.dart` (`updateClientName` replaced by `updateCompanyProfile`), `lib/features/admin/screens/platform_admin_screen.dart` (`_EditLicenseDialog._save()` and `_EditPlanDialog._save()` recalculate `annual_price`; `_ClientRow` gets an edit icon + `onSaved` callback; new `_EditClientDialog`), `lib/features/settings/screens/settings_screen.dart` (`_CompanyTabState` shows the full field set and opens the new dialog; `_RenameClientDialog` replaced by `_EditCompanyDialog`; `_UserRow` gets an edit icon; new `_EditUserDialog`), `docs/schema/016_wyzesales_client_profile_fields.sql` (new).

**Verification**: all six touched Dart files parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check; delivered and byte-verified (sha256) against `/Users/craig/projects/wyzesales/lib/...`, and the new migration delivered to both `docs/schema/` locations. Not yet confirmed on a real device or against a real Supabase project — worth Craig running migration 016 first, then checking: Platform Admin > Clients' new edit icon opens and saves the 3-field dialog; editing a License's seats/discount and Pricing plan rates both leave `annual_price` looking correct afterward (including on a license that already had its own discount); Settings > Company's edit dialog shows and saves all ten fields; and Settings > Users' new edit icon lets a name/contact/level/rep-code/branch-code change save without disturbing the separate active/inactive toggle.

## 35. Two follow-ups from Craig's first look at Section 34 (2026-08-28)

Craig sent screenshots of Settings > License and Settings > Users right after Section 34 landed, with two questions.

**"5 additional users" on a 10-seat license that's fully within its 10-seat base plan.** His License tab showed "Users used: 0 of 10 licensed," "Standard base plan (10 users) — R72,000," then a second breakdown line reading "5 additional users × R350/mo — R21,000" — with the Monthly/Annual totals below correctly showing R6,000/R72,000 (i.e. ignoring that second line entirely, since `additionalUsers` doesn't actually feed into either total). Root cause: `license` (the *row*, not the plan) carries its own `base_users` column, snapshotted once and never kept in sync with the plan it's linked to — this test license's own copy was `5` while the plan's `base_users` was `10`. `License.additionalUsers` was computing `maxUsers - license.baseUsers` (10 − 5 = 5) instead of `maxUsers - plan.baseUsers` (10 − 10 = 0), so the breakdown showed a phantom line the actual pricing math never used. Fixed by changing the getter to prefer the joined plan's `baseUsers` whenever a plan is present, falling back to the license row's own value only when there's no plan to read from: `(maxUsers - (plan?.baseUsers ?? baseUsers)).clamp(0, 999999)`. Same self-correcting philosophy as `discountedMonthly` (recompute from the live plan rather than trust a value that can drift).

**No Edit/pause/Delete icons showing on either Users row.** Both rows visible in Craig's screenshot — "WyzeSales Support" and his own "Craig" login — carry the `is_platform_admin` flag (shown as the purple "Support" badge), and `_UserRow` was hiding Edit, pause/resume, and Delete entirely for any row flagged that way, a restriction that predates this round (it already applied to pause/resume before Section 34 added Edit). Since every row in this particular test client happens to be flagged that way, no controls were visible on anything. Given the choice between matching SeaWyze exactly (SeaWyze's own `_EditUserDialog` only excludes Delete for a support/platform-admin row, not Edit) or leaving WyzeSales' broader exclusion in place, Craig chose the former. Edit now shows on every row regardless of `isPlatformAdmin`; pause/resume and Delete remain hidden for platform-admin rows exactly as before, since a support login still needs to keep working and stay undeletable from this screen.

**Files changed**: `lib/data/models/license.dart` (`additionalUsers` prefers `plan.baseUsers`), `lib/features/settings/screens/settings_screen.dart` (`_UserRow`'s edit icon moved outside the `isPlatformAdmin` gate; pause/resume and Delete still inside it).

**Verification**: both files parse clean with the tree-sitter Dart check; delivered and byte-verified (sha256) against `/Users/craig/projects/wyzesales/lib/...`. Worth Craig re-checking the same License tab (the "5 additional users" line should now disappear, since this license's plan and max_users both sit at 10) and confirming an edit icon now appears on both Users rows, including the two Support-flagged ones.

## 36. Rep pipeline coverage / shortfall — deferred, no schema data to build it on (2026-08-28)

Craig asked, after Section 34/35 shipped: "Is there anything you feel is missing? e.g. Does a Rep have enough cover to meet target or what is the shortfall in cover?" — i.e. a sense of whether a rep's open quotes give them a realistic shot at their target, or how far short they are.
