**The Server Side Extract program** — the part of Craig's request task #90 deliberately deferred to this task — needed the trailing sales-data window it pulls from IQRetail sized off this same setting, not just the app's own display. `Domain/FiscalDate.cs`'s `ThreeFiscalYearWindowStart(today)` became `FiscalYearWindowStart(today, historyYears = 3)` (default preserved so the original QVS-equivalence proof in `SelfTest.cs` still validates the exact same 3-year default it always has — `SelfTest` now calls it explicitly as `FiscalYearWindowStart(today, historyYears: 3)` to make that pinning obvious). `StockMovementFactsBuilder.Build` gained a `historyMonths = 36` parameter (both the window-start calculation and the trailing-months loop that used to hardcode 36). `SupabaseWriter` gained `LoadDataHistoryYearsAsync(clientId)`, reading `fiscal_year_settings.history_years` live from Supabase — mirroring the existing `LoadExcludedAccountsAsync` pattern rather than duplicating the value into `appsettings.json`, so the extract program and the app's own Settings screen can never drift out of sync — and falls back to 3 if a client has no settings row yet, matching every other default in this feature. `ExtractRunner.RunOnceAsync` now loads that value right after the exclusion list, uses it for both the sales-document window start (including the `FiscalYear.OverrideYear` manual-testing branch, which also generalized from a hardcoded `-3` to `-historyYears`) and the stock-movement window (`historyYears * 12` months), and logs the window it resolved to for visibility in the run log.

**Not touched, deliberately**: `dashboard_screen.dart`'s `_loadDimension()` `fiscalYears: [currentFy - 1, currentFy]` — a separate, smaller 2-year year-over-year comparison, not the 3/5-year trailing window this task is about. `document_analysis_view.dart` and `performance_screen.dart` — both single-fiscal-year screens with no window array to resize. `sales_by_screen.dart`'s `.take(3)` for `recentMonths` and Budgets' month tables — an unrelated "trailing 3 months" concept, not fiscal years.

**Verification**: all 8 changed Dart files (plus the 3 whose only change was a stale code comment — `sales_by_screen.dart`, `sales_analysis_screen.dart`, `ytd_comparative_screen.dart` — parse clean with the tree-sitter check; a project-wide grep after the edits found no remaining hardcoded 3-year-window assumption in `lib/` outside the deliberately-untouched cases above. The 9 Flutter/schema files were byte-verified (sha256) against Craig's Mac and committed to git (`5c5a5f7`) — held back from push per the standing batching arrangement. The .NET side could not be compiled in this sandbox (no network access to nuget.org to restore Npgsql and the other package references) and has not yet been delivered anywhere, because **where the live WyzeSalesExtract program actually runs has not been established this session** — Craig's Mac (the only device this session can currently reach) holds just a zipped reference copy (`WyzeSalesExtract.zip`) under the docs mirror, not an editable working copy; the real program almost certainly runs on a Windows machine at Water Components SA (it talks to IQRetail's ODBC driver directly), which this session has no access to. **Needs Craig's input**: where the live WyzeSalesExtract install/source lives and how he'd like the updated `.cs` files delivered there (a zip via this chat, a git remote, RDP into that machine, etc.) — plus, as always, running `020_wyzesales_data_history_window_settings.sql` in Supabase's SQL Editor before the new Settings field can save anything, and giving every client's `fiscal_year_settings` row a value (defaults to 3, so nothing breaks for existing clients in the meantime).

## 50. Task #91 testing feedback: newest-first year columns, distinct chart colours, no-data greying (2026-09-01)

Craig tested Section 49's data history window feature and came back with three follow-ups, all fixed the same day: (1) "On the Sales by screens and the YTD Comparative screen the oldest year appears first on the left moving to the newest year on the right resulting in the user having to scroll to right to view the most recent data. Can we turn these all around newest first (left) oldest (right)." (2) "Line chart colours. Please can we have a separate colour for each set of data." (3) "Year and Month filters. Can we apply the no data rule shaded grey. E.g. 2027 has no data for Sept forward therefore these should be greyed out and there is no data for 2023 and 2024."

**(1) Newest-first columns**: `sales_by_screen.dart` and `ytd_comparative_screen.dart` both build their fiscal-year columns by walking `data.fiscalYears`/`_fiscalYears` — which stays oldest-first internally (`fiscalYearWindow`'s own convention, unchanged) — so every column-building loop (`_columnPositions`, `_columnValueExtractors`, `_buildTable`'s header/cell loops, `_totalsRow`, `_buildExportData`) was flipped to walk `for (var i = length - 1; i >= 0; i--)` instead of ascending, while every "vs" variance still compares to `fiscalYears[i-1]` — the chronologically PRIOR year, not whichever column now sits to its left. Net effect: the current fiscal year's value column is always the very first data column after the row-name column, regardless of whether the history window is 3 or 5 years, and the recent-months columns (Sales By's own separate "3 most recent months" section) were deliberately left in their existing order — Craig's report was specifically about fiscal years.

A related latent bug caught and fixed in passing, not itself reported: `sales_by_screen.dart`'s initial (pre-any-sort) `_sortColumnIndex` was hardcoded to `4`, a position that only meant "current FY, descending" back when the window was always exactly 3 years — with a 5-year window it would have silently landed on the wrong column (a "vs" variance column, not the current year's value) the very first time a client picked 5 years and opened this screen without ever clicking a header. Reordering to newest-first made this trivial to fix properly rather than work around: the current FY's value column is now ALWAYS column 1, for any window length, so the hardcoded default became `1` — correct unconditionally rather than needing a runtime lookup.

**(2) Distinct chart colours**: `sales_analysis_screen.dart`'s trend chart used to fade every fiscal year older than the second-to-last into the same grey, which read fine for the original fixed 3-year window but made a 5-year window's two oldest lines indistinguishable. Replaced with the same 5-colour palette `SimplePieChart` already uses for other multi-series breakdowns elsewhere in the app (`[info, positive, teal, accentPurple, caution]`) — reused rather than invented, for visual consistency with the rest of the app's "how do we tell N series apart" convention, and exactly enough distinct hues for the largest window this app offers (5 years) without ever repeating one.

**(3) No-data greying**: added `fiscalYearDataAvailabilityProvider` (`app_providers.dart`) — reuses the exact same `fetchConsolidatedSales` company-wide monthly rollup YTD Comparative already reads (no new SQL/RPC needed), fetched once for the client's configured fiscal-year window, and reduced in Dart into two sets: `yearsWithData` (any fiscal year in the window with at least one row on record — this is what makes 2023/2024 grey in Craig's example, since this client's data simply doesn't go back that far) and `currentYearMonthsWithData` (which calendar months of the CURRENT, possibly still in-progress fiscal year have at least one row — this is what greys "Sept forward" once the current fiscal year hasn't reached those months yet, or the extract hasn't loaded them). `global_filter_bar.dart`'s shared `_pickFromList` dialog gained an optional `isEnabled` callback — an option it returns false for stays visible (so it's clear that period exists) but renders in the theme's disabled colour and can't be tapped — wired into both the Year and Month "Add filter" pickers; `availability == null` (still loading) means nothing is greyed, so the check never blocks the picker on a slow first load.

**A deliberate scope call worth flagging**: the Month picker's greying is checked ONLY against the current fiscal year, not whichever Year filter (if any) happens to be active — Year and Month are independent chips in this app's filter model (not a combined picker), and a calendar month name like "Sept" genuinely does have data for most past fiscal years even in the same window where the CURRENT year's Sept hasn't happened yet. Greying it globally whenever the current year lacks it (rather than only relative to a selected Year) matches Craig's own example exactly (current-year Sept forward, greyed) without requiring a redesign of how Year/Month filters interact — flagged here in case Craig finds a case where he wants a month greyed/ungreyed relative to a specific past year instead of always the current one.

**Files changed**: `lib/core/app_providers.dart`, `lib/shared/widgets/global_filter_bar.dart`, `lib/features/dimension_templates/screens/sales_by_screen.dart`, `lib/features/ytd_comparative/screens/ytd_comparative_screen.dart`, `lib/features/sales_analysis/screens/sales_analysis_screen.dart`.

**Verification**: all 5 files parse clean with the tree-sitter check; a grep confirmed no remaining ascending (`for (var i = 0; i < ...fiscalYears.length; i++)`) column loop left in either `sales_by_screen.dart` or `ytd_comparative_screen.dart`. Byte-verified (sha256) against Craig's Mac and committed to git (`56880ab`) — held back from push per the standing batching arrangement. No schema change, so nothing new needs running in Supabase's SQL Editor for this one. Worth testing: switching the history window to 5 years and confirming FY columns read newest-to-oldest left-to-right on both screens, the trend chart shows 5 visibly distinct line colours, and the Year/Month "Add filter" lists show the expected years/months greyed out and un-clickable.

**Same-day correction — Month greying was checked against the wrong fiscal year**: the "deliberate scope call" flagged above turned out to be a genuine bug, not just a documented trade-off. Craig asked directly: "If I select Month September then Yes it will not be greyed out but I have selected Year 2027 and Month September or October then it will be greyed out. Correct?" — surfacing that the answer given in chat ("a month like September will show as available... because past years' Septembers do have data") was actually backwards from what the code did. The real behaviour was that September/October showed greyed in the Month picker UNCONDITIONALLY, regardless of whether Year 2027 or Year 2025 (which has real September data) was selected — because the check only ever consulted `currentYearMonthsWithData` (FY2027's own months), never whichever Year filter the user actually had active. That would have silently blocked a perfectly valid combination (an older Year + a month FY2027 hasn't reached yet) from ever being selectable.

**Fixed properly, not worked around**: `FiscalDataAvailability.currentYearMonthsWithData` (a single `Set<String>`) became `monthsWithDataByYear` (a `Map<int, Set<String>>`, one entry per fiscal year in the window) plus an explicit `currentFiscalYear` field. `global_filter_bar.dart`'s Month picker now resolves `referenceYear` as `filters.fiscalYear ?? availability.currentFiscalYear` — checking whichever Year filter is currently active, if any, and falling back to the current (most recent) fiscal year only when no Year filter is set, since "hasn't happened yet" only means something for a bare month with no year attached. So: Year unset + Month September → checked against the current fiscal year (greyed, matching Craig's original report); Year 2025 + Month September → checked against FY2025 specifically (available, since that year has real September data); Year 2027 + Month September/October → checked against FY2027 (greyed, correctly, since that's the year genuinely missing that data).

**Files changed**: `lib/core/app_providers.dart`, `lib/shared/widgets/global_filter_bar.dart`.

**Verification**: both files parse clean with the tree-sitter check; grepped for any remaining reference to the removed `currentYearMonthsWithData` field name (found only in a doc comment explaining the correction, no stale code). Byte-verified (sha256) against Craig's Mac and committed to git (`165b170`) — held back from push per the standing batching arrangement. Worth testing: select Year 2025 (or whichever older year has full data) then open the Month picker and confirm September/October are no longer greyed for that year specifically, while they still grey correctly with Year 2027 selected or with no Year filter at all.

**Second same-day correction — a bare Month filter was still checked against the wrong scope**: the fix above still fell back to "current fiscal year's months" whenever no Year filter was active, which was itself wrong. Craig: "If I only select September then it must not be greyed out and it must filter on and show data for all of the past septembers." A bare Month filter (no Year attached) genuinely does apply across every fiscal year currently in scope everywhere else in the app — it restricts each fiscal year's own total down to that one calendar month, it doesn't pin the whole filter set to just the current year — so greying it against "current year only" was inconsistent with what the filter itself actually does once applied.

**Fixed by removing the "current year" concept entirely from this path**: `FiscalDataAvailability.currentFiscalYear` (added in the first correction, now dead) was removed again. `global_filter_bar.dart`'s Month picker now resolves its check purely from `filters.fiscalYear`: if a Year filter is active, check that year's own months (unchanged from the first correction); if none is active, union `monthsWithDataByYear` across every fiscal year in the window and check against that — so a plain "September" is enabled the moment ANY year in the configured history window has September data, and only greys out if literally none of them do. Net behaviour, confirmed against all three scenarios Craig described: Year 2025 + September → checked against FY2025 alone (available). Year 2027 + September/October → checked against FY2027 alone (greyed, correctly — that year genuinely lacks it). September alone, no Year → checked against the whole window (available, since at least one year has it) — this is the one that changed from the first correction.

**Files changed**: `lib/core/app_providers.dart`, `lib/shared/widgets/global_filter_bar.dart`.

**Verification**: both files parse clean with the tree-sitter check; grepped for any remaining reference to the removed `currentFiscalYear` field (none found, code or comments). Byte-verified (sha256) against Craig's Mac and committed to git (`78872f2`) — held back from push per the standing batching arrangement. Worth testing: select Month September alone (no Year filter) and confirm it's now selectable and returns data from every past September that has any, then re-confirm the Year 2025/Year 2027 combination cases still behave as before.

## 51. Fundamental bug — a bare Month filter only summed the current fiscal year, not every year (2026-09-01)

The three greying corrections in Section 50 were all about which options the Month/Year pickers show as available — they never touched what the filters actually do once applied. Craig then reported a separate, more serious bug in that second half: "There is one fundamental error. In Sales Analysis if I filter on August then it must filter on and display and sum all transactions for all of the augusts not just the last one. Same for Quote Analysis, Sales Order Analysis, Performance Analysis etc. through out the application. Please check and ammend."

**Root cause**: `document_analysis_view.dart` (the shared table+totals view behind Sales/Quote/Sales Order Analysis) and `performance_screen.dart` both built their query's fiscal year argument as `filters.fiscalYear ?? fiscalYearFor(DateTime.now(), startMonth: startMonth)` — i.e. "use the selected Year, or if none is selected, default to the current one." That default fires regardless of whether a Month filter is active, so selecting Month "August" with no Year chosen silently sent the CURRENT fiscal year's August as the query's year argument, not "every August on record." The underlying SQL was never at fault: `fn_sales_documents_page`/`fn_sales_documents_totals` (schema/012) and `fn_dimension_performance_filtered` (schema/011) already treat a null `p_fiscal_year` as "no year restriction" and correctly filter on month alone in that case — this was purely a Dart-side default defeating an already-correct query before it ever saw a null.

**Fix — `document_analysis_view.dart`**: added `_effectiveFiscalYear(filters, startMonth)`, which only falls back to the current fiscal year when NEITHER Year nor Month is set at all; if Month is set with no Year, it now passes `null` straight through. All three call sites (`_loadPage`, `_loadTotals`, `_buildExportData`) switched to call it instead of the old inline `??` default. Since the RPC functions already handle a null year correctly, no SQL change was needed — this screen's fix is the whole fix for Sales/Quote/Sales Order Analysis (all three share this same view).

**Fix — `performance_screen.dart`**: same `_effectiveFiscalYear` change, but this screen's own query (`fn_dimension_performance_filtered`) normally returns exactly one row per entity (one fiscal year, one month) — so passing a null year now returns one row PER MATCHING FISCAL YEAR per entity, and the screen needed a new aggregation step to collapse those back into the single row per entity the table/export expect. Added `_mergeAcrossYears(rawRows)`, only invoked when `effectiveYear == null` (a no-op whenever a Year is selected, since every entity already has exactly one row in that case — deliberately built this way to carry zero regression risk for the existing single-period behaviour): groups by `entityCode`, sums `actualValue`/`actualQuantity`/`actualProfit` across the matched years (never averaging), recomputes `gpPercent` from the summed profit/value, and recomputes `targetPercent` from the summed target (only when at least one contributing row actually has a target set). This reuses the same "sum raw values, recompute ratios, don't average percentages" convention already established for totals rows in `sales_by_screen.dart`, `ytd_comparative_screen.dart`, and this same `performance_screen.dart`'s own `_totalsRow`.

**A design choice worth Craig's confirmation — `contributionPercent`**: `fn_dimension_performance_filtered` normally computes this as each entity's share of ITS OWN fiscal-year-and-month's grand total (`100.0 * value / sum(value) over (partition by fiscal_year, fiscal_month)`). Once several years are merged into one "August" row per entity, there's no longer a single period for that percentage to be relative to, so `_mergeAcrossYears` recomputes it as each entity's share of the SUMMED grand total across every matched August — i.e. "what fraction of all-time August business (across the whole history window) does this entity represent," which is the most natural reading of "contribution" once the row itself represents multiple years merged together, but is a genuinely different number than what the same field means when a single Year is selected. Flagging this explicitly in case Craig would rather see it left blank/N/A for a merged row than redefined.

**Files confirmed NOT affected, checked deliberately rather than assumed** — each already uses a fixed multi-year window unaffected by any Year filter, or doesn't read the Month/Year global filters the way that caused this bug: `sales_by_screen.dart`, `ytd_comparative_screen.dart` (both window-based, per Section 49/50), `sales_analysis_screen.dart`'s Graph tab (`fetchConsolidatedSales` over the configured window, same as the two above — its Table view IS `document_analysis_view.dart` and is covered by this fix), `dashboard_screen.dart`'s `_loadDimension` (fixed 2-year YoY window, doesn't apply the Year filter at all), `budgets_screen.dart` (doesn't read `filters.fiscalYear`/`filters.fiscalMonth`).

**Files changed**: `lib/shared/widgets/document_analysis_view.dart`, `lib/features/dimension_templates/screens/performance_screen.dart`.

**Verification**: both files parse clean with the tree-sitter check. Byte-verified (sha256) against Craig's Mac and committed to git (`c172ec6`) — held back from push per the standing batching arrangement. No schema or SQL change, so nothing new needs running in Supabase's SQL Editor for this one. Worth testing: on Sales Analysis (and Quote/Sales Order Analysis, and Performance Analysis), clear the Year filter, select Month "August" alone, and confirm the totals now reflect every August on record summed together rather than only the most recent one; then re-select a specific Year alongside August and confirm it narrows back down to that single year+month combination as before.

## 52. Performance's R Target formula, Budget→Forecast fallback, thousands separators everywhere (2026-09-01)

Section 51 fixed the *actuals* summing across years for a bare Month filter. R Target turned out to need three attempts of its own, plus two related requests — the process worth recording here explicitly, since Craig's own feedback mid-thread changed how this session works, not just what got built.

**Attempt #1**: Craig asked "When filtering on month = August where does it get the R Target from?" — a factual question that surfaced a real bug: `budget_figures` (schema/001) has no `fiscal_year` column at all — its primary key is `(client_id, dimension, entity_code, fiscal_month)` — so every fiscal year's row for a given entity/month carries the IDENTICAL budget figure. `_mergeAcrossYears` (Section 51) was summing that identical figure once per matched year, silently multiplying a single real target by however many years had August data. Fixed to take one representative value instead of summing (commit `ce035cb`) — but see attempt #2.

**Attempt #2**: taking the single unscaled value fixed R Target's own displayed number but broke the ratio the other way — Craig: "I see what you have done but the analysis now is rubbish because it is comparing three years august sales with 1 august target inflating the % target." Fixed by scaling the single target UP by `periodsCount` (the number of fiscal years actually contributing a row for that entity), landing R Target and R Value on the same N-year basis (commit `6d32cf8`).

**Attempt #2 was itself wrong, and this is the important part**: Craig — "I'm not sure why you decided to go ahead and change this. In future before making any changes, let's decide on what that change should be. The way you have done it now in my opinion is still incorrect." Worth being honest here: scaling an identical repeated value by N is mathematically the exact same number attempt #1's bug already produced (summing the same figure N times = multiplying it by N) — the "fix" had changed how the number was computed without actually changing the number. Craig proposed the real formula instead: "add R Value (Actual sales) for the past Augusts to the current target for this August giving a R Target." Confirmed with a worked example (3 years, R100k/R120k/R90k actual, R150k target on file → R Target = R220k past-actual + R150k target = R370k, not R450k) before anything was built, plus two clarifying questions Craig answered directly: (1) when the current fiscal year's period hasn't happened/loaded yet, every existing row counts as "past" and R Target legitimately exceeds R Value until it does — expected, not a bug; (2) this only applies to Performance Analysis, confirmed by checking every other place `target_value`/budget figures feed a calculation (Dashboard's Revenue/Rep Target Attainment tiles are hardcoded to the current fiscal year's MTD/YTD and deliberately ignore the 5 global filters entirely; Budgets screen doesn't read Year/Month filters at all) — neither can ever hit this multi-year merge.

**Final, agreed design**: `_mergeAcrossYears` now takes `currentFy` (the client's actual current fiscal year, computed once in `_load` — not just "the newest year that happens to have a row," which matters for the "hasn't happened yet" case above) and computes, per entity, `pastActual` = sum of actualValue for every contributing row where `fiscalYear != currentFy`, then `target = pastActual + singleTarget`. R Value (the real actual total, unchanged from Section 51) stays the sum across every contributing year including the current one. Commit `5263c95`.

**Budget→Forecast fallback**: separately, Craig asked "If any of the Sales Budget fields have not been populated then the calculations all take the calculated Seasonal Forecast. Correct?" — answered honestly: no, that was not what the app did. `sales_forecast` was already joined into both `v_dimension_performance` (schema/002) and `fn_dimension_performance_filtered` (schema/011) and its `forecast_value`/`forecast_confidence` were already carried onto the `DimensionPerformance` model — just never read anywhere; `target_value` was `budget_figures.budget_value` alone, null when no budget row existed. Confirmed as wanted ("the Seasonal Forecast value must be used in these calculations") and built as **schema/021**: both SQL objects now resolve `target_value` as `coalesce(nullif(budget_value, 0), forecast_value)` — treating an explicit 0 the same as "no row," since the app has no way to distinguish an adminuser typing 0 from never entering anything (the Budgets screen itself already collapses "no row" to a displayed 0). `target_percent` is recomputed from that same resolved figure. Both objects were restructured through an inner subquery/CTE so the coalesce expression isn't repeated three times over. This single SQL-layer fix covers Performance Analysis's R Target/%Target regardless of which of the two query paths (plain view vs RPC) a given filter combination routes through — see `fetchDimensionPerformance`'s own doc comment (`sales_repository.dart`) for why there are two paths at all.

**Sales Budget input formatting + right-justify**: Craig, from a screenshot of Budgets — Sales Person: "The Sales Budget input value is not formatted to 591,080... can you right justify these values as well." Every other number in the app already comma-formats via `formatRand`/`formatQuantity` (both `en_US`-locale `NumberFormat`, dating to Craig's 2026-08-26 "All numbers must have a thousand separator" request) — this was the one place displaying a raw editable number, because it's the app's only live-editable numeric field rather than a read-only `Text`/`DataCell`, so those two static-display helpers didn't apply to it. New `_ThousandsInputFormatter` (a `TextInputFormatter`) re-formats to comma-grouped text on every keystroke; digits are stripped back out before parsing in `_save()`, so the persisted value in `budget_figures` is unchanged. `textAlign: TextAlign.right` added to match the read-only Seasonal Forecast column beside it — `DataColumn(numeric: true)` only auto-right-aligns a plain `Text` cell, not a `TextField`.

**Thousands separators, the remaining gaps**: Craig's broader ask ("format all numbers with the thousand [separator]... This must apply to all numbers in the application") prompted a systematic audit of every numeric display in `lib/` rather than guessing where the gap was. Confirmed clean: every DataCell across Sales By, Performance, YTD Comparative, Budgets, and Sales/Quote/Sales Order Analysis, every CSV/PDF export builder, and every chart/tooltip. Genuine gaps, all raw `int` interpolations instead of `formatQuantity`, all fixed: Dashboard's Quote → Order Conversion tile (quote/order counts, MTD and YTD — YTD being the one most likely to actually reach a 4+ digit count and match Craig's "591080" example), Rep Target Attainment tile (rep counts), Top 5 Customer Concentration tile (account counts, capped at 5 so cosmetic rather than functional); Platform Admin's client/license count tiles; Settings' seat-usage and base/additional-user counts.

**Files changed**: `lib/features/dimension_templates/screens/performance_screen.dart`, `docs/schema/021_wyzesales_target_forecast_fallback.sql` (new), `lib/features/dimension_templates/screens/budgets_screen.dart`, `lib/features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart`, `lib/features/admin/screens/platform_admin_screen.dart`, `lib/features/settings/screens/settings_screen.dart`.

**Verification**: all 5 Dart files parse clean with the tree-sitter check. Byte-verified (sha256) against Craig's Mac and committed to git (`5263c95`). **Needs the same manual step as every schema change**: Craig running `021_wyzesales_target_forecast_fallback.sql` in Supabase's SQL Editor before the fallback takes effect. Worth testing: on Performance Analysis with a bare Month filter (no Year) where at least one contributing year has no Sales Budget entered for that month, confirm R Target now reflects the Seasonal Forecast figure instead of showing "—"; confirm the worked-example arithmetic (past actuals + current target, not scaled by year count); on Budgets, type a large figure into a Sales Budget cell and confirm it comma-formats live and sits right-aligned; and spot-check the Dashboard's Quote → Order Conversion / Rep Target Attainment tiles, Platform Admin's stat cards, and Settings' seat-usage figures for comma grouping on any count large enough to show it.

## 53. Budgets: a real data-corruption bug, a batch Save button, three attempts at column alignment; login subtitle; Dashboard MTD pie fix; WyzeSalesExtract task #91 delivered (2026-09-01)

Section 52 shipped the Sales Budget comma-formatting/right-justify fix; testing it immediately surfaced four more problems in the same screen, one of them a real data-corruption bug, plus two unrelated fixes elsewhere. Recorded together since they landed in one testing pass.

**Decimals reappearing on the input**: Craig saw decimal values on Sales Budget cells that should display as whole Rand. `_ThousandsInputFormatter` was built on `NumberFormat.decimalPattern('en_US')`, which preserves real stored cents on rows where `budget_figures` already held a decimal value (pre-existing data) — the field used to hard-round via `.toStringAsFixed(0)` and lost that guarantee when the formatter was swapped in. Fixed by switching to `NumberFormat('#,##0', 'en_US')`, which always renders whole Rand regardless of what's actually stored.

**A real data-corruption bug, caught while investigating the above**: `_save()`'s digit-stripping used `replaceAll(RegExp(r'[^\d]'), '')` to remove the comma grouping before parsing — this also strips the decimal point. A value like `591,080.13`, re-saved without being edited, would parse as `59108013` (over 64 million) and overwrite the correct figure. This exactly explains a garbage value Craig had seen in an earlier screenshot. Fixed by stripping only commas (`replaceAll(',', '')`), leaving any decimal point intact.

**Save-on-Enter only saved the last-edited field**: each Sales Budget cell auto-saved individually on `onSubmitted`, so entering several months' figures in a row and pressing Enter after each only persisted whichever field was saved last before navigating away — Craig: "you have to input a budget number then enter for it to save before inputting the next one. If you input more than one at a time it only saves the last one. What about a Save button." Removed the per-field auto-save; `onSubmitted` now just advances focus to the next field (`FocusScope.of(context).nextFocus()`). Added a `_saveAll()` method that saves every month's figure at once (`Future.wait`) behind a single "Save" button below the table, matching the Save button convention already used on the Settings screen, shown only when the signed-in user has edit rights.

**Column alignment — three attempts**: Craig reported the Sales Budget column, and separately its Total row, as not right-aligned, across three rounds of feedback ("not right aligned"; then, after a fix, "The Total is still not aligned." — twice). This sandbox cannot render Flutter, so every layout change here ships unverified until Craig actually sees it; attempts #1 and #2 were both plausible-looking `Align(alignment: Alignment.centerRight, ...)` wrappers — first around the input `TextField`, then, when Craig said the Total specifically was still off, around the Total row's `Text` too, on the theory the two just weren't using the same mechanism. Both were reasoning about `data_table_2`'s internal column-sizing without a way to check it, and Craig's second "still not aligned" report means at least one of those two guesses was wrong. Attempt #3 abandoned guessing at internals entirely: added `DataColumn2(fixedWidth: 150)` (from the `data_table_2` package, opted into for the first time in this codebase — see `responsive_data_table.dart` for the rest of that package's history in this app) so the column has one real, deterministic width instead of whatever the auto-stretch algorithm assigns it, then wrapped both the input cell and the Total cell in the *identical* `SizedBox(width: 150, child: Align(alignment: Alignment.centerRight, ...))` structure, driven off one shared constant (`_budgetColumnWidth`) so the two rows are provably matched rather than independently trusted to agree. **Not yet confirmed by Craig** — worth explicitly checking on next test.

**Login subtitle**: Craig, circling it on a screenshot: "can we change this to say '360 degree Sales Analysis' or something like this." Changed from "Sales & Budget Analysis" to "360° Sales Analysis."

**Dashboard MTD pie chart showing 5 customer names against R0**: Craig, testing on 1 September with no data loaded yet for the new fiscal month: "This is incorrect as we have no MTD data for September yet." The Customer — MTD pie's legend was showing 5 real customer names, each next to R0 — reading as "these are this month's top 5 customers" when the actual situation was that no extract had run for September at all. Root cause in `_pickSlices()`: when the current period's data map is completely empty, every rank mode's sort is comparing 0 against 0 — a no-op — so `.take(5)` on the union-with-previous-period set silently surfaced 5 arbitrary previous-month customer codes as though they still applied. Fixed with an early `if (current.isEmpty) return const [];` guard, so `SimplePieChart`'s existing "No data for the current filters" fallback fires instead. Deliberately left the pre-existing union-of-current-and-previous-keys logic untouched below that guard — it exists for a genuinely different, legitimate case: one customer that had activity last period and none this period (dropped to zero) is exactly what "Diminishing 5" mode should be able to surface, and that case still has real data in the period overall, unlike a period with zero rows on record.

**WyzeSalesExtract task #91 (configurable 3/5-year history window) delivered**: Craig confirmed the live extract program "lives in the Wyzesales folder on my desktop" — resolving a previously-open question about where the .NET program that feeds Supabase actually runs. The reference copy there was still the pre-task-91 build (byte-identical, confirmed by hash, to the version this session had on file as the last-delivered baseline). Task #91's actual code change — reading `fiscal_year_settings.history_years` from Supabase at the start of each run instead of a hardcoded 3-year window, feeding both the sales-document window (`FiscalDate.FiscalYearWindowStart`) and the stock-movement window — had been built earlier but never delivered. Delivered now as `WyzeSalesExtract_v2_2026-09-01.zip` alongside (not replacing) the existing reference `WyzeSalesExtract.zip`, so nothing on Craig's Desktop was overwritten without his say-so; the two can be diffed or swapped once he's confirmed it builds. This sandbox has no Windows host and no route to nuget.org (restore fails against `api.nuget.org` from this network), so the change could only be checked by hand — brace/paren balance, and confirming every call site (`ExtractRunner.LoadDataHistoryYearsAsync`, `FiscalDate.FiscalYearWindowStart`) matches its declaration and the actual `fiscal_year_settings.history_years` column (schema/020) — not by an actual `dotnet build`. **Craig still needs to build and deploy this on the Windows machine that runs the live service** before task #91 takes effect in production.

**Files changed**: `lib/features/dimension_templates/screens/budgets_screen.dart`, `lib/features/auth/screens/login_screen.dart`, `lib/features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart`. WyzeSalesExtract delivered as a separate zip, not part of the Flutter git history.

**Verification**: all 3 Dart files parse clean with the tree-sitter check and are byte-verified (sha256) against Craig's Mac; committed to git across three commits (`5149c97`, `4e0dd51`, `6d1f136`) plus this session's dashboard fix (`2e70bdb`). Still open: Craig confirming the third alignment attempt actually looks right, and confirming the batch Save button saves every edited month correctly in one pass.

## 54. Budgets alignment attempt #4 (still slightly off, accepted); a pushed deploy; task #92's regression tests; a real fiscal-year bug the tests caught (2026-09-02)

**Budgets alignment, attempt #4**: Craig confirmed attempt #3 (the fixed-width `DataColumn2`) still didn't line up the input boxes with the Total row. Pixel-measuring Craig's own screenshot (rather than guessing a fourth time) found the actual cause was different from width at all: a bare `TextField` doesn't shrink to its text the way a `Text` widget does — it fills the box it's given, then draws its digits inset from that box's right edge by `InputDecoration.contentPadding`, which Material keeps non-zero even with `isDense: true` (that flag only shrinks vertical padding). `Align(centerRight)` around a plain `Text` has no such inset. Zeroed the input field's horizontal content padding to close that gap. Measuring Craig's follow-up screenshot the same way (pixel-for-pixel, both before and after confirming he'd actually pushed and redeployed this fix) showed the gap shrank but didn't fully close — about 6-8px remains. Craig: "It is slightly out. No big deal really!" — deprioritized rather than pursued further; the next concrete idea if it's ever worth a fifth attempt is making the Total row render through the exact same (disabled) `TextField` widget as the input rows, so whatever residual quirk exists applies identically to both instead of being guessed at again. Also fixed the 2 `const` lints (`prefer_const_literals_to_create_immutables`/`prefer_const_constructors`) from Craig's own `flutter analyze` output on the same section. Committed `02fc18e`.

**First actual push of the session**: 24 commits (everything from Section 51 through the alignment fix above) had been sitting local-only under the standing batching arrangement. Craig asked to push and deploy now, to test live rather than guessing further from local screenshots. This sandbox has no GitHub credentials and can't push on Craig's behalf (`git push` failed with "could not read Username for 'https://github.com'") — Craig ran `git push origin main` himself from his Mac, and Netlify's git-based CI (`netlify.toml`) picked it up from there.

**WyzeSalesExtract task #91 rolled into the reference copy**: per Craig's go-ahead, the task #91 build delivered last session (as a separate `WyzeSalesExtract_v2_2026-09-01.zip`, kept alongside rather than overwriting anything without his say-so) is now `/Users/craig/Desktop/Wyzesales/WyzeSalesExtract.zip` itself — the pre-task-91 build kept as `WyzeSalesExtract_pre_task91_backup.zip` in case of comparison. Craig: will build/deploy on the Windows machine "later when we are finished and need to get live data."

**Task #92 — regression tests around the fiscal-year/rollup logic**: Craig asked what this task actually meant; explained it as targeted test cases against the known-correct worked examples the logic had already been debugged against this session (the R220k+R150k=R370k example, the 3/5-year window, etc.), so a future change can't silently reintroduce a bug already fixed once. Asked Craig to choose the scope before building anything (AskUserQuestion): Dart unit tests only, Dart + a SQL regression script for the RLS/forecast-fallback logic, or SQL only. Craig chose **Dart unit tests only**.

`performance_screen.dart`'s R Target formula (`_mergeAcrossYears`) was a private method on a `State` class — uncallable from a separate test file without a live widget instance. Extracted verbatim (unchanged logic, a pure move) to a new public top-level function, `mergeAcrossYears()` in `lib/core/utils/performance_rollup.dart`, and updated `performance_screen.dart`'s two call sites/references accordingly. `fiscal.dart`'s helpers (`fiscalYearFor`, `fiscalMonthOrderFor`, `fiscalYearWindow`, `calendarMonthStartFor`, etc.) were already public top-level functions needing no extraction.

Two new test files: `test/core/utils/performance_rollup_test.dart` (the canonical worked example; explicit regression guards pinning both superseded bugs from Section 52 so they can't silently return; the "current period hasn't happened yet" case; null/zero-target handling; gpPercent recomputed from summed Rand figures rather than averaged; multi-entity isolation; contributionPercent against the grand total) and `test/core/constants/fiscal_test.dart` (month ordering for arbitrary start months, the fiscal-year boundary for both the default March start and an arbitrary mid-year start, the history-window bounds, and a calendar/fiscal round-trip check). Committed `b64719d`.

**A real bug the tests found, before a single test was even run**: while writing `fiscal_test.dart`, tracing `fiscalYearFor`'s formula (`date.month >= startMonth ? date.year + 1 : date.year`) against a January start month (`startMonth = 1`) found it always takes the `+1` branch — `date.month >= 1` is true for every month — so it labels every single date one full year ahead of where it belongs. A January-start fiscal year should be labelled by its own calendar year (Jan Y -> Dec Y ends in Y, not Y+1), unlike every start month from 2-12 (where the fiscal year genuinely spans two calendar years and the `+1` branch is correct). The identical formula exists server-side as `fiscal_year()` (schema/001), called from `v_sales_documents` — meaning this wasn't just a Dart-side slip, it was a real, shared bug that would mislabel every fiscal year for any client who picked a January start month, which the brand-new (2026-09-01, Section 48) Settings > Company field makes freely selectable. Reported to Craig rather than fixed unilaterally, since it's a correctness change touching both sides — Craig: "We need to fix this please."

**Fixed in both places, same day**: `fiscalYearFor` (Dart) and `fiscal_year()` (SQL) both special-case `startMonth = 1` to return the date's own calendar year outright, leaving the existing `+1`/unchanged logic untouched for every other start month (already correct there). No backfill needed server-side — `fiscal_year` is never stored, `v_sales_documents` computes it live from `doc_date` on every query, so replacing the function retroactively fixes every dependent view/rollup the moment it's run. **No client has ever been configured with `start_month = 1`** (every real client started life hardcoded to March, per Section 48), so nothing live was actually mislabelled — this was caught before it could bite anyone, which is the entire point of task #92. New migration: `docs/schema/022_wyzesales_fiscal_year_january_start_fix.sql`. The regression test written to originally document this as a known, deliberately-pinned issue was rewritten to assert the corrected result instead, once the fix was agreed and built. Committed `ae244df`.

**A second, unrelated gap task #92 surfaced**: running the new tests (`flutter test`) for the first time this entire engagement — every prior round only ever ran `flutter analyze`, which doesn't compile anything — revealed `test/widget_test.dart` fails to even compile: `main.dart` imports `flutter_web_plugins` (for clean URLs on web, `usePathUrlStrategy()`) which needs `dart:ui_web`, a library that only resolves when compiling for the actual web platform, not Flutter's default test VM. Since WyzeSales is a web-only app (no other target ever built or deployed), the fix is how the suite is invoked, not app code: `flutter test --platform chrome` instead of the bare default. No project doc or CI config referenced `flutter test` before now, so nothing else needed updating.

**Final result**: `flutter test --platform chrome` — all 27 tests pass (26 new regression tests + the pre-existing smoke test), confirmed by Craig.

**Files changed**: `lib/features/dimension_templates/screens/budgets_screen.dart`, `lib/features/dimension_templates/screens/performance_screen.dart`, `lib/core/utils/performance_rollup.dart` (new), `lib/core/constants/fiscal.dart`, `test/core/utils/performance_rollup_test.dart` (new), `test/core/constants/fiscal_test.dart` (new), `docs/schema/022_wyzesales_fiscal_year_january_start_fix.sql` (new). WyzeSalesExtract's reference zip replaced on Craig's Desktop, outside the Flutter git history.

**Verification**: every Dart file parses clean with the tree-sitter check and is byte-verified (sha256) against Craig's Mac. Committed across `02fc18e`, `b64719d`, `ae244df` and pushed to `origin/main` by Craig — the first actual push this session, deployed via Netlify's git CI. `flutter test --platform chrome` run by Craig: 27/27 passing. **Needs the same manual step as every schema change**: Craig running `022_wyzesales_fiscal_year_january_start_fix.sql` in Supabase's SQL Editor — not urgent, since no client is live on a January start month yet, but worth doing before one is.

## 55. Task #93 — from "quote/order lifecycle status" to "Sales Coverage": Quote/Sales Order Analysis removed entirely, replaced by R Gap / % Coverage Needed (2026-09-02)

Task #93 started life as "add lifecycle status to quotes and sales orders (raised → approved/rejected → converted to order), and link a quote to the order it becomes." It ended up as a completely different, data-driven feature, after the investigation into that original scope turned up a foundational problem this session hadn't previously flagged.

**The investigation**: asked Craig how quote/order lifecycle status is actually tracked, offering three options (AskUserQuestion) — he chose "check IQRetail first" rather than guessing at a design. He came back with the real answer: WyzeSalesExtract has only ever pulled quotes/sales orders from `C:\IQRetail\IQEnterprise\002` (the consolidated database, accurate for invoices/credit-notes) — but quotes and sales orders actually live in the separate per-branch databases (`CPT`, `DBN`, `JHB`), never wired into any extract. Even pulling from the right databases for inspection, the `Status` field was `0` on every row, and nothing in the schema captures how or when a quote gets approved or turned into an order.

**The finding that changed the scope entirely**: Craig's follow-up was the important part — "In the current Wyzesales Application that WCSA use on a daily basis the Quotes and Sales Orders have been pulling from 002 which means there aren't any and WCSA have never questioned this. Therefore they are not using this." This isn't a WyzeSales rebuild gap — it's a reflection that WCSA's own daily-use IQRetail application has the identical limitation, and their real business process has never relied on the system to track quote/order lifecycle at all. Compounded by Craig's own real-world observation, later in the thread, that a lot of companies quote via Excel or Word outside any system entirely — undermining confidence in ANY IQRetail-sourced quote/order data even if the extraction gap were fixed.

**Craig's reframe**: "Instead of modifying our new application to cater for something that is not been used, maybe we should drop this but then how can we measure a gap in sales cover?" Clarified what "gap in sales cover" meant when asked directly (AskUserQuestion): not literally the old quote/order-value idea, but "the Gap between Actual Sales and Target Sales" — a measure built entirely on data this app already has reliably (invoices/credit-notes vs. budget/forecast targets), not on the quote/order data that turned out not to exist in practice.

**Building the formula — confirmed step by step, not guessed**: Craig confirmed "same-period totals" as the coverage definition (vs. an alternative also offered) and deferred the extraction-timing question to whenever WyzeSalesExtract work happens generally, rather than blocking this feature on it. The formula went through the same kind of iterative, worked-example-first refinement Section 52's R Target formula needed, front-loaded this time specifically to avoid repeating that saga:

- Started from an external sales-methodology idea Craig found (Isolate Target Gap → Historical Average Deal Value → Deals Needed → Reverse-Engineer Activity Goal / Audit Weekly). The last two steps were ruled out immediately — no meeting/activity/CRM data exists anywhere in this system to build them from.
- Craig asked whether "Deals Needed to close" could become a percentage instead, and how a brand-new sales person with no history should be handled. Converting to a percentage and normalizing against "Average Deals Per Period" turned out to mathematically simplify — the deal-count and deal-value terms cancel — to one clean formula that needs only revenue sums, no invoice/document counting at all: **% Coverage Needed = R Gap ÷ Historical Average Revenue Per Period × 100**, where R Gap = R Target − R Value (pure arithmetic, already available on every `DimensionPerformance` row) and Historical Average Revenue Per Period = an entity's total revenue over its trailing history window ÷ its number of active months (months that actually have a sales row — not calendar tenure, since nothing tracks hire dates).
- For the brand-new-entity case, Craig picked the fallback option outright: "I like 2. the fall back option and my guess is less than 3 months of history activates the fall back" — below 3 active months of an entity's own history, use the company-wide average instead, flagged in the UI when this happens. This mirrors the Budget→Forecast fallback shape Section 52 already established for R Target.
- Worked examples confirmed before anything was built: a brand-new rep with 1 active month and a R60,000 gap falls back to a R250,000/month company average → 24%, flagged; an established rep with 36 active months and their own R60,000/month average, R30,000 gap → 50%, own figure, not flagged. If R Gap ≤ 0 (already at or above target), the UI shows "On Target" rather than a percentage.

**Removing Quote Analysis and Sales Order Analysis entirely**: with the lifecycle-status scope gone, Craig asked directly whether those two screens (and the Dashboard's Quote → Order Conversion tile) were now redundant, and whether to replace the tile with the new company-wide Coverage calc. Offered the option to keep the screens as empty/placeholder versus removing them outright (AskUserQuestion); Craig chose full removal: "I think we should remove entirely. My experience is that a lot of companies do quotes on excel or word documents outside of the system so i'm not sure of the reliability of this data. We can always build it back later." Confirmed the whole four-part plan with "Yes please": (1) remove Quote Analysis/Sales Order Analysis entirely, (2) replace the Dashboard's Quote → Order Conversion tile with a company-wide Coverage %, (3) add R Gap and % Coverage Needed to Performance Analysis, (4) document the full reasoning trail here.

**(1) Removal**: deleted `lib/features/quote_analysis/` and `lib/features/sales_order_analysis/` entirely (both screen files and their feature directories — nothing else in the codebase referenced them, confirmed by a project-wide grep before deleting). `app_router.dart` lost both routes (`/quote-analysis`, `/sales-order-analysis`) and their imports, replaced with an explanatory comment pointing here. `app_shell.dart` lost the two matching nav tiles. `top_bar_search.dart`'s `_routeForDocumentKind` — which used to route a quote/sales-order document match to one of the two removed screens — now always returns `/sales-analysis`, the only screen left that shows document-level detail. `reference_data_repository.dart`'s `searchDocuments` (the top-bar document search) was scoped with `.inFilter('document_kind', ['invoice', 'credit_note'])`, since an unfiltered quote/sales-order search result would now route to a screen (Sales Analysis) that only ever displays those two kinds anyway — surfacing one would have silently landed on a screen showing nothing for it. `sales_repository.dart`'s `fetchDocumentCounts` (`fn_document_counts`, schema/015) was also removed — its only caller was the very Dashboard tile this task replaces; the underlying SQL function was left in place in Supabase since there's no cost to an unused function and dropping it wasn't necessary for anything here. Committed `735176b`.

**(2) SQL — one new function, deliberately thin**: `docs/schema/023_wyzesales_sales_coverage_history.sql` adds `fn_dimension_sales_history(p_dimension, p_fiscal_years)`, returning one row per entity code with `active_months` and `total_value` summed over the given fiscal years, read from `v_dimension_monthly_sales` (already restricted to actual sales — invoice/credit_note — same as everywhere else this rollup is used). Calling it with `p_dimension = 'company'` returns exactly one row, `entity_code = 'ALL'` — the fallback row the <3-active-months rule needs, via the same `'company'` branch `v_dimension_monthly_sales` already has (schema/002), no new SQL concept required. Deliberately returns raw numbers only — no average, no ratio, no fallback decision — all of that stays in Dart, same reasoning as `mergeAcrossYears` (Section 53/54). Deliberately built on the plain (unfiltered by the 5 cross-dimension filters) rollup, since an entity's own historical baseline shouldn't shift just because Performance Analysis happens to have a branch or item filter active right now — flagged as a possible future option if Craig ever wants "coverage within this filtered slice" instead. This is a dramatically smaller SQL footprint than the original quote/order-lifecycle design would have needed (a document_kind dimension threaded through 5 existing views/functions) — a direct benefit of the pivot, not just a consolation.

**(3) Dart — the calc, the fetch, and the two new columns**: `lib/core/utils/sales_coverage.dart` (new) holds `EntitySalesHistory` (wraps one `fn_dimension_sales_history` row, with an `avgRevenuePerPeriod` getter) and `computeCoverage()` — a pure function taking a target/actual pair plus an entity's own and the company's history rows, returning a `CoverageResult` (`rGap`, `coveragePercent`, and three mutually-exclusive flags: `onTarget`, `usedFallback`, `insufficientData`). Same "pure function over plain data, unit-testable without a widget" pattern `mergeAcrossYears` established. `sales_repository.dart` gained `fetchSalesHistory({dimension, fiscalYears})`, calling the new RPC. `performance_screen.dart`'s `_load()` now fetches the dimension's own history and the company's fallback history alongside the existing performance rows (same trailing window `fiscalYearWindow(currentFy, historyYears)` every other multi-year screen uses — computed once regardless of whether a specific Year is filtered, since an entity's historical baseline is a standalone figure, not something that should shrink to one year just because the screen itself is currently viewing one). `_PerformanceData` gained a `coverageFor(row)` helper wrapping `computeCoverage`. Two new columns, "R Gap" and "% Coverage Needed," were added in all four places a `DataColumn`-based screen enumerates its columns: `_buildTable`'s columns/cells, `_totalsRow` (the Total row's own % Coverage Needed is computed against the company's own history, not any one entity's, by passing `data.companyHistory` as both `own` and `company`), `_compareRows` (sortable, same as every other column), and `_buildExportData` (CSV/PDF). A fallback figure's cell gets a trailing `*`, with an italic caption below the table — "using company-wide average — under 3 months of this entity's own sales history" — shown only when at least one visible row actually uses it, per Craig's requirement that the fallback be "flagged/visible in the UI when this fallback is used."

**(4) Dashboard tile**: the Quote → Order Conversion `ToggleStatCard` (document counts that, per the investigation above, were reliably near-zero in practice) was replaced with a "Sales Coverage" tile using the identical Gap-over-average-revenue-per-period idea, computed whole-company rather than per-entity, so there's no entity/company fallback to decide — only how MTD and YTD should scale the average, since a YTD Gap covers more than one month's shortfall at once. Asked Craig directly (AskUserQuestion) rather than guessing: "Multiply average by elapsed months" — i.e. YTD Coverage % = YTD Gap ÷ (average monthly revenue × however many fiscal months have elapsed this year so far), keeping the same "how many average periods of revenue would close this gap" meaning MTD has, just scaled to match how many months the Gap itself spans. `_KpiData` gained `companyAvgRevenuePerPeriod` (from one new `fetchSalesHistory(dimension: 'company', ...)` call, replacing the two `fetchDocumentCounts` calls the old tile needed) and `elapsedMonthsYtd` (reusing the `elapsedFiscalMonths` set `_loadKpis` already computes for Rep Target Attainment). The tile shows "On Target" when the Gap is ≤ 0, colored green/amber the same way Revenue Target Attainment is, with a subtitle giving the Rand Gap (or Rand-above-target amount) for whichever period is toggled.

**Files changed**: `lib/core/router/app_router.dart`, `lib/shared/widgets/app_shell.dart`, `lib/shared/widgets/top_bar_search.dart`, `lib/data/repositories/reference_data_repository.dart`, `lib/data/repositories/sales_repository.dart`, `lib/core/utils/sales_coverage.dart` (new), `lib/features/dimension_templates/screens/performance_screen.dart`, `lib/features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart`, `test/core/utils/sales_coverage_test.dart` (new), `docs/schema/023_wyzesales_sales_coverage_history.sql` (new). Deleted: `lib/features/quote_analysis/`, `lib/features/sales_order_analysis/`.

**Verification**: every Dart file parses clean with the tree-sitter check; a project-wide grep confirmed no remaining code reference to the removed screens/routes/method (`fetchDocumentCounts`) outside historical doc comments explaining what changed and why. New unit tests (`sales_coverage_test.dart`) cover both of Craig's worked examples exactly (brand-new-rep-falls-back-to-24%, established-rep-own-average-50%), the exactly-3-active-months boundary, a null own history, R Gap ≤ 0 (`onTarget`, both at-target and above-target), no target set at all, and two zero-history edge cases (a brand-new client with no company data at all; a company row present but with zero active months) — all reporting `insufficientData` rather than a divide-by-zero. Byte-verified (sha256) against Craig's Mac and committed to git (`735176b` for the screen removal; the coverage feature's SQL/Dart batch delivered and committed alongside this section). **Needs the same manual step as every schema change**: Craig running `023_wyzesales_sales_coverage_history.sql` in Supabase's SQL Editor before R Gap/% Coverage Needed/the Dashboard's Sales Coverage tile have real data to show. Worth testing: `flutter test --platform chrome` for the new suite; opening Performance Analysis and confirming R Gap/% Coverage Needed appear, sort correctly, export to CSV/PDF, and that a genuinely new entity (if one exists in real data) shows the fallback flag; opening the Dashboard and confirming the Sales Coverage tile's MTD/YTD toggle both render sensibly against real target/actual figures; and confirming Quote Analysis/Sales Order Analysis are gone from the nav with no dead links anywhere.

## 56. Sales Coverage testing feedback: colour thresholds, a live-vs-past wording fix, and a genuine Year-only-filter bug (2026-09-02)

Craig ran `023_wyzesales_sales_coverage_history.sql`, tested Section 55's Sales Coverage feature live, and sent screenshots of Performance Analysis' new columns and the Dashboard's Sales Coverage tile (107.2% MTD). Three rounds of feedback followed, all against the shipped feature rather than anything still on paper.

**Colour thresholds**: Craig asked whether R Gap/% Coverage Needed could be coloured "to make them more understandable," e.g. "Johan Botha should be green, Mark Fischer I guess should be red." Offered threshold options via AskUserQuestion; Craig confirmed the recommended tiering: On Target, or under 25% coverage needed, is green (a small ask relative to a typical month); 25–50% is amber (a meaningful chunk of a typical month); over 50% is red (more than half a typical month's revenue still needed). `insufficientData` (no target set, or no usable average even after the company-wide fallback) gets the same neutral/muted colour every other tile in this app already uses for "nothing meaningful to show." Implemented as `_coverageColor` in `performance_screen.dart`, and the Dashboard's own `coverageColor` was updated to the identical three tiers (previously a simpler two-colour gap<=0/gap>0 split) so both screens read the same way. R Gap and % Coverage Needed share one colour per row — a row's Gap and its coverage percentage are the same underlying fact, so colouring them independently would only invite the reader to wonder why they disagree.

**"Aug 2027 has passed — are we saying we missed the target by 24.1%?"**: Craig, looking at a closed FY2027/August row, correctly identified that "% Coverage Needed" doesn't make sense for a period that's already over — "You cannot catch it up. Yes for the current period." Asked what a past period should say instead (AskUserQuestion); Craig picked "% Below Avg Month" — the same underlying number and colour, just relabelled from an actionable framing ("you need to close this gap") to a purely descriptive one ("this is how far below your average month this period landed"). "On Target" (a still-open period at or above its target) got the matching past-tense counterpart "Met Target."

This needed a genuine "is this period still live" check, not just "is a percentage available." `isLivePeriod` is true only when the filtered period could still change — for a Month-grain view, today's own fiscal month AND (no Year pinned, or the pinned Year is the current fiscal year); a specific past Year+Month, or the current Year with an earlier Month, are both fully closed. Verified algebraically that this holds even for a bare Month filter merged across several years (`mergeAcrossYears`, Section 51/52): its R Gap formula always nets down to exactly "the current fiscal year's target minus the current fiscal year's actual," regardless of how many historical years get folded in, so the existing check needed no special-casing for that shape.

Craig's own follow-up caught a real gap before it shipped: "What would it do if we only entered September? Current month but also taking into account the older past Septembers." Confirmed this was already handled correctly by the bare-Month-across-years case above (a live "September" merged with past Septembers is still live, because the current year's own September hasn't closed yet) — no extra code needed, just confirmed by working through it with Craig rather than assuming.

**The Year-only-filter bug**: separately, Craig reported "it does not recognise a only year filter. i.e. If I input Year = 2027 it will show no data." Root cause: `_effectiveFiscalMonth` defaulted to TODAY's calendar month whenever `filters.fiscalMonth` was unset — even when `filters.fiscalYear` WAS explicitly set — so "Year 2027 alone" silently became "Year 2027 + September" (today's fiscal month, only two days in when this was reported, so effectively no data yet). The mirror image of Section 51's bug (a bare Month filter used to only sum the current year); `_effectiveFiscalYear` had already been fixed for that case, but the symmetric fix was never made to `_effectiveFiscalMonth`. Both `v_dimension_performance` and `fn_dimension_performance_filtered` already correctly treat a null `fiscal_month` parameter as "no restriction" — confirmed by reading both, schema/002 and schema/011 — so this needed no SQL change at all, purely a Dart-side fix.

**The fix**: `_effectiveFiscalMonth` now only defaults to today's fiscal month when NEITHER Year nor Month is set — the same "only default when totally unfiltered" rule `_effectiveFiscalYear` already followed. That alone makes a Year-only filter return one row per entity PER FISCAL MONTH within that year (rather than zero, or one for the wrong month), which needed a new aggregation step: `mergeAcrossMonths` (`core/utils/performance_rollup.dart`), the whole-year counterpart to `mergeAcrossYears` — confirmed with Craig before building ("Yes" to summing the whole year's totals per entity, same shape as every other column). Genuinely simpler than `mergeAcrossYears`'s cross-year merge: `budget_figures`/`sales_forecast` are keyed by `(dimension, entity_code, fiscal_month)` with no `fiscal_year` column at all, so different fiscal months within the same year legitimately carry different targets — no "one target repeated N times" trap to correct for, the year's target is simply the sum of whichever months actually contributed a row. A month that hasn't happened yet within the current fiscal year never produces a row to sum in the first place, since both query paths are driven from the actual-sales rollup left-joined to budget/forecast — "future months contribute nothing" falls out for free.

**Craig's follow-up question, resolved before building**: "if it is the current year then we are also looking at % Coverage Needed vs % Below Avg Year???? or what if we made target? What would the label say then?" Worked through with Craig rather than guessed: a live current-year filter still just says "% Coverage Needed" (no year-specific wording needed — the live case is already the same framing regardless of grain); a past year says "% Below Avg Year," extending Craig's own "% Below Avg Month" pattern with the different noun; and "made target" was already correctly handled by the existing binary `onTarget`/"On Target"/"Met Target" logic regardless of grain, so no new label was needed there at all. `isLivePeriod` for a Year-grain filter is simply `effectiveYear == currentFy` — a year isn't over until its last day, independent of which month within it, unlike the Month-grain case.

A Year-grain Gap also spans several months at once, so `computeCoverage` gained an optional `periods` parameter (default 1, unchanged for every pre-existing call site) that multiplies the average-per-period denominator — the exact same "average × elapsed months" reasoning Section 55's Dashboard tile already established for its own YTD figure. `_PerformanceData` gained `isYearGrain` and `coveragePeriods` fields; `_load()` computes `coveragePeriods` as the count of elapsed fiscal months (via `fiscalMonthOrderFor`, the same pattern `dashboard_screen.dart`'s `elapsedFiscalMonths` already uses) when the pinned year is the current one, or 12 for a year that's entirely in the past.

**Files changed**: `lib/core/utils/sales_coverage.dart` (`periods` parameter on `computeCoverage`), `lib/core/utils/performance_rollup.dart` (new `mergeAcrossMonths`), `lib/features/dimension_templates/screens/performance_screen.dart` (`_effectiveFiscalMonth` fix; `_load()` branches on the Year-only case; `_PerformanceData` gained `isYearGrain`/`coveragePeriods`; `_coverageColumnLabel` gained the "% Below Avg Year" case; `_coverageColor`, `_coverageText`, `isLivePeriod` from the wording-fix round), `lib/features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart` (three-tier `coverageColor`), `test/core/utils/sales_coverage_test.dart` (new `periods` group), `test/core/utils/performance_rollup_test.dart` (new `mergeAcrossMonths` group).

**Verification**: every touched Dart file parses clean with the tree-sitter check. New unit tests cover `computeCoverage`'s `periods` parameter (default-1 unchanged behaviour, a full 12-period year, a partial 6-period YTD year, a scaled fallback average, and that `onTarget`/`insufficientData` are unaffected by `periods` since no average is involved there) and `mergeAcrossMonths` (summing across months for one entity; a month with no target contributing nothing to the summed target rather than crashing; no target on any contributing month leaving the merged target null rather than zero; a partial/YTD year summing correctly with no extra bookkeeping in the merge function itself; `gpPercent` recomputed from summed Rand figures rather than averaged; multi-entity isolation; `contributionPercent` against the grand total) — mirroring the depth of coverage `mergeAcrossYears`' own test suite already has. Worth testing on Craig's side: `flutter test --platform chrome`; filtering Performance Analysis to a Year only (both the current, still-open fiscal year and a fully past one) and confirming real data now appears, one row per entity, with sensible % Coverage Needed / % Below Avg Year figures; re-confirming the colour thresholds and past-period wording against real rows across all four filter shapes (Month+Year, Month only, Year only, neither).

## 57. A 6th dimension — "Company" — added to Sales by / Budgets / Performance's dimension switcher (2026-09-02)

Craig, moving on to the next roadmap item (#94, proactive alerting), stopped himself: "Before we do this. Something is concerning me about Performance. We can see all Dimensions except a Company wide Dimension???"

**The gap, confirmed**: `SalesDimension` (`core/constants/fiscal.dart`) — the enum every dimension switcher on Sales by, Budgets, and Performance Analysis is built from — only ever had 5 members: Sales Person, Category, Customer, Item, Branch. There was no way to select a whole-company, no-breakdown view on any of the 3 template screens.

**The good news, found while investigating**: `company` already existed as a fully first-class dimension value at the schema level, from the very first migration — `budget_figures` and `sales_forecast` (schema/001) both already allow `dimension = 'company'` in their own CHECK constraints, and `v_dimension_monthly_sales` (schema/002) already has its own `'company'` UNION branch, grouping the whole company's sales with no further breakdown at all (`entity_code = 'ALL'`). The cross-dimension-filter RPC layer (schema/011, added for the "iterative filters" work) goes further still — `fn_dimension_monthly_sales_filtered`'s CASE statement already falls through to `else 'ALL'` for any dimension it doesn't recognise by name, and `fn_dimension_performance_filtered` is built entirely on top of that function, joining budget_figures/sales_forecast generically by whatever `p_dimension` string it's given. In other words: every SQL view and function this app already had was written from day one to treat "company" as a real dimension, end to end — it simply had never been exposed as a selectable option anywhere in the UI. This was a pure Dart/UI gap, needing zero schema changes.

**The one design wrinkle**: `SalesDimension` does double duty across the app — it's both (a) "which breakdown am I viewing" (the 3 template screens' switchers, where Company clearly belongs) and (b) "which single entity do I want to narrow everything down to" (GlobalFilterBar's filter chips and "Add filter" dropdown, the top-bar's cross-dimension entity search, and the Dashboard's ranking/breakdown picker). Company doesn't belong in (b) at all — there's nothing to search for or narrow to, since "Company" already means "everything, no narrowing." Adding it to the bare enum without addressing this would have put a nonsensical "Company" entry into the "Add filter" dropdown (with no sensible picker behind it) and, worse, into the top-bar entity search, where picking a result calls the exact same `setDimension(...)` global-filter mechanism — silently doing nothing useful if triggered for Company.

**The fix**: added `company('company', 'Company')` as a 6th `SalesDimension` member, deliberately placed LAST (not first) — `SalesDimension.values.first` is relied on in a few places (`app_shell.dart`'s nav "quick" routes) to mean "the default real breakdown dimension," which needed to stay `salesPerson`, unchanged. Added a new `SalesDimension.filterable` static list — the original 5, explicitly excluding `company` — and switched every "pick an entity to filter by / rank within" call site over to it: `GlobalFilterBar`'s filter chips loop and "Add filter" dropdown, `reference_data_repository.dart`'s `searchAllDimensions` (the top-bar cross-dimension entity search), its `filterOptionCodes`' "any other dimension active" check, and the Dashboard's ranking/breakdown picker. Every "which breakdown am I viewing" call site — the dimension switcher dropdowns on Sales by, Budgets, and Performance Analysis themselves — was deliberately left iterating `SalesDimension.values` directly (unchanged), so all three automatically picked up Company as a 6th option with no per-screen edit needed there at all.

Two switch statements over `SalesDimension` (`GlobalFilters.forDimension`, `GlobalFiltersNotifier.setDimension`, both in `core/filters/global_filters.dart`) are exhaustive with no default case — Dart's compiler forced a `company` arm in both once the enum grew a 6th member. Both are documented no-ops (`forDimension` returns null, `setDimension` returns the unchanged state) since nothing in the UI ever actually calls either with `company` any more — `GlobalFilters` never gained a `company` field of its own, deliberately, since there's nothing per-entity to store for it.

`reference_data_repository.dart`'s `entitiesFor` switch also needed a `company` arm — unlike the other 5, which each query a real reference-data table, this one has no table to query (there's only ever one "entity"), so it returns a single synthetic row matching the `entity_code = 'ALL'` the SQL layer already uses: `[CodeName(code: 'ALL', name: 'Company')]`. That one addition is what makes `namesFor(company)` (Performance's row-label lookup) and Budgets' own entity list both work correctly for Company with no further special-casing anywhere — Budgets in particular needed nothing beyond this: selecting "Company" from its (now one-item) entity list opens the exact same 12-month budget-entry grid every other entity gets, letting Craig enter a genuine whole-company annual target for the first time.

**What was deliberately left alone**: considered, then dropped, adding logic to Sales by/Performance to hide their "Total" row when viewing Company (since with exactly one entity, "Total" and the one row are numerically identical, which could look like a rendering bug at first glance). Decided against it — the app already shows this same "Total row duplicates the one entity row" shape today, unremarked, whenever any of the original 5 dimensions happens to have exactly one entity for a client (a single-branch operation, say) — so Company is simply the same existing behaviour, one more time, rather than a new case needing new code. Flagging this here in case Craig would rather it read differently once he's looked at it live.

**Files changed**: `lib/core/constants/fiscal.dart` (new `company` member, new `filterable` list), `lib/core/filters/global_filters.dart` (`company` arms on both exhaustive switches), `lib/data/repositories/reference_data_repository.dart` (`entitiesFor`'s `company` arm; `searchAllDimensions`/`filterOptionCodes` switched to `filterable`), `lib/shared/widgets/global_filter_bar.dart` (chips loop and "Add filter" dropdown switched to `filterable`), `lib/features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart` (ranking/breakdown picker switched to `filterable`). No changes needed to `sales_by_screen.dart`, `budgets_screen.dart`, `performance_screen.dart`, `sales_repository.dart`, `app_router.dart`, `app_shell.dart`, or any SQL — all already generic enough to pick up Company for free. New tests: `test/core/constants/fiscal_test.dart` (a `SalesDimension` group — `company`'s db value/label, that it stays last, that `filterable` excludes it while `values` includes it) and a new `test/core/filters/global_filters_test.dart` (`GlobalFilters.forDimension` — the 5 real dimensions return their own field, `company` always returns null regardless of filter state, `isEmpty`/`activeCount` unaffected).

**Verification**: every touched Dart file parses clean with the tree-sitter check. Worth testing on Craig's side: `flutter test --platform chrome`; opening Sales by / Performance / Budgets and confirming "Company" now appears at the end of each dimension switcher, showing sensible whole-company figures with no per-entity breakdown; on Budgets specifically, entering and saving a whole-company budget figure for a month or two; confirming "Company" does NOT appear in GlobalFilterBar's "Add filter" dropdown, the top-bar search results, or the Dashboard's ranking picker — only on the 3 template screens' own switchers, as designed.

## 58. Does Revenue reconcile across every dimension? A real crash risk found while checking, hardened (2026-09-02)

Craig's follow-up question after Section 57: "If I add up Revenue and / or Targets across all Dimensions including Company, do they equal each other?"

**Revenue — yes in principle, with a caveat that turned into a real fix.** All 6 dimensions are just different `GROUP BY`s over the exact same underlying transaction rows (`v_sales_documents`, invoices/credit-notes only), so summing Revenue across every Sales Person, Branch, Category, Customer, or Item should always land on the same grand total as Company — nothing about which rows count changes, only how they're split. Checking this claim carefully surfaced a real gap: `sales_document_facts.invoice_rep_code` and `.warehouse_code` (schema/001) are both nullable, and an item can be missing from `items`/have no `department_code` mapped — so a line with none of those resolved would still count toward Company/Customer/Item (`account_code`/`item_code` are NOT NULL, always present), but produce a NULL `entity_code` specifically on the Sales Person/Branch/Category breakdowns.

That in itself wouldn't have been catastrophic — until checking the Dart side turned up something worse: `DimensionMonthlySales.fromMap` and `DimensionPerformance.fromMap` (`lib/data/models/`) both did a bare `map['entity_code'] as String` — a non-nullable cast. A single row like that wouldn't just be miscounted; it would throw and fail the ENTIRE fetch for that dimension/screen. Craig: "Yes please" to hardening this rather than leaving it to chance — genuinely unknown whether WCSA's real data ever hits this (IQRetail may require a rep and warehouse on every line, in which case it's moot in practice), but a crash risk sitting on an untested path is worth closing regardless of how likely it is to fire.
