**The fix, at the single source rather than patched per-screen**: `v_sales_documents` (schema/001 Section 9) is what every rollup in this app is ultimately built from — `v_dimension_monthly_sales`'s own `base` CTE and `v_sales_cube_monthly` (schema/011) both select straight from it — so `docs/schema/024_wyzesales_unassigned_entity_fallback.sql` wraps the 4 exposed code/name columns there in `coalesce(..., 'UNASSIGNED')`: `resolved_rep_code`, `branch_code`, `branch_display_code`, and `department_code`. Fixing it once at the source means `v_dimension_monthly_sales`, `v_dimension_performance`, and both schema/011 RPC functions inherit the fix automatically — no separate migration needed for any of them.

Deliberately did NOT touch `resolved_rep_code()`, the function itself — that's the same single source of truth the RLS policies on `sales_document_facts` call directly (schema/001 Section 8's own doc comment). Today a NULL return there makes `resolved_rep_code(...) = <a user's own rep_code>` evaluate to NULL (never true) for every 'user'-level person, so an unattributed line is safely invisible to everyone by default — coalescing the function itself would technically still be safe (no real rep_code will ever equal 'UNASSIGNED'), but it's a needless widening of a security-relevant function's behaviour for what's really a display-only concern. Coalescing only the view's OUTPUT column instead leaves the function, and every RLS policy built on it, completely untouched. The joins that produce `resolved_rep_name`/`branch_name`/`category_name` are likewise left exactly as they were, still matching on the RAW (possibly null) value — an unassigned line correctly still gets no name, not a spurious match against something coincidentally coded 'UNASSIGNED'.

**Dart-side defense in depth**: both `fromMap` factories changed from `map['entity_code'] as String` to `(map['entity_code'] as String?) ?? 'UNASSIGNED'` — matching schema/024's own sentinel exactly, so even if a future code path somehow reintroduced a null (a new view, a bypassed RPC), the app degrades to showing an "Unassigned" bucket rather than crashing the screen outright. `reference_data_repository.dart`'s `namesFor` now also maps the `'UNASSIGNED'` code to the friendlier display label "Unassigned" — but only for Sales Person, Branch, and Category, the 3 dimensions this can actually occur on (Customer and Item can never produce it, since `account_code`/`item_code` are NOT NULL on `sales_document_facts` itself). Deliberately injected in `namesFor` rather than in `entitiesFor` itself, so "Unassigned" shows up wherever a row's own label is looked up (Sales by, Performance) without also appearing as a pickable entity in Budgets' entity list (which queries `entitiesFor` directly) — there's nothing meaningful to set a sales target against for "sales with no rep/branch/category attached."

**Targets — no, and there's no reason to expect they would.** Each dimension's targets are a completely separate, manually-entered set of numbers on the Budgets screen — one full set of monthly figures per Sales Person, a separate full set per Branch, per Category, per Customer, per Item, and now per Company (Section 57). Nothing in the app ties these together or checks that they agree; entering a Company-wide target doesn't touch the Sales Person targets, and vice versa. Told to Craig as a plain factual answer, not something needing a code change — reconciling them, if ever wanted, would be a deliberate future feature (e.g. a warning when a dimension's targets don't sum to the Company figure), not a bug to fix.

**Files changed**: `docs/schema/024_wyzesales_unassigned_entity_fallback.sql` (new), `lib/data/models/dimension_monthly_sales.dart`, `lib/data/models/dimension_performance.dart` (both `fromMap`'s `entityCode` fallback), `lib/data/repositories/reference_data_repository.dart` (`namesFor`'s "Unassigned" label injection). New test: `test/data/models/dimension_models_test.dart` (both `fromMap` factories — a normal entity_code parses through unchanged; a null entity_code falls back to 'UNASSIGNED' instead of throwing).

**Verification**: every touched Dart file parses clean with the tree-sitter check. **Needs the same manual step as every schema change**: Craig running `024_wyzesales_unassigned_entity_fallback.sql` in Supabase's SQL Editor. Worth testing: `flutter test --platform chrome` for the new suite; since this fix targets a condition that may not exist in WCSA's real data today, there's nothing specific to visually confirm unless an unattributed line actually exists — the meaningful check is simply that Sales Person/Branch/Category Performance and Sales by screens still load and show the same figures as before (this is a pure hardening change, not expected to alter any currently-displayed number).

## 59. Leaked service-role key remediation: migrating 4 Edge Functions to Supabase's new secret-key system (2026-09-02)

A stale, uncommitted file on Craig's Desktop (`009_wyzesales_sales_document_facts_pk_fix.sql`, leftover terminal output rather than the real migration) turned up containing a plaintext `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` JWT — found while reconciling the Desktop folder against the git project (Section 60 covers that reconciliation). This key bypasses RLS entirely, so it needed neutralising regardless of anything else going on that session.

**Supabase has retired in-place rotation of the legacy key.** Checked current (2026) Supabase docs rather than relying on stale training knowledge — "it is no longer possible to rotate the legacy anon, service and JWT secrets" (Supabase troubleshooting docs). The current, correct remediation is: create a new secret key (`sb_secret_...` format, Dashboard → Project Settings → API Keys → "Publishable and secret API keys" tab) → migrate every code consumer off the old key → only then deactivate the legacy key. Deactivating first would have broken every one of the 4 places this app holds a full-access key before the code change shipped.

**The 4 consumers**: `create-client`, `create-user`, `delete-user`, `send-upgrade-request` (all in `supabase/functions/`) — confirmed via grep to be the only 4 places in the codebase reading `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, each needing it for the same reason (creating/deleting `auth.users` rows via the Admin API, which the Flutter client can never hold — see each function's own comment).

**The code change**: new `supabase/functions/_shared/service_key.ts` — a single `getServiceKey()` — rather than duplicating key-resolution logic across all 4 functions, the same reasoning `resolved_rep_code()` (schema/001) being one SQL function rather than a copy-pasted expression already established. Supabase's new secret keys arrive as a single `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEYS` env var — a JSON object keyed by whatever name the key was given at creation — rather than the legacy single-string env var. Craig's dashboard already had the legacy key effectively occupying the display name "default" in the Publishable/secret tab, so the new key was created as `wyzesales_edge` instead; `getServiceKey()` looks that name up specifically, falling back to the legacy `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` only when `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEYS` isn't set, doesn't parse, or doesn't contain that name — so shipping the code was itself a no-op until the new key actually existed as an Edge Function secret, and deploying it never risked breaking anything already working.

**A genuine Supabase-side gap, not a code bug, blocked the cutover initially.** Once the new key existed and all 4 functions were redeployed, every one of them started 403'ing with "Caller has no profile" — traced (via a temporary diagnostic patch added to `create-user` that surfaced the raw Postgres error and which key branch was actually in use, since the generic error message was giving zero signal) to a raw `permission denied for table profiles` (Postgres code `42501`), not an RLS block. Supabase's secret-key docs state the new key format bypasses RLS identically to `service_role` ("it also has the BYPASSRLS attribute"), but in practice the new key's underlying Postgres role didn't carry the same standing table-level GRANTs `service_role` has always had by default in this project. Fixed entirely on the database side — no further code change — by running the GRANT statements Postgres's own error hint suggested, for every table each of the 4 functions actually touches: `profiles`, `clients`, `license`, `pricing_plan`. Confirmed working end-to-end afterwards (create-user and delete-user tested directly; create-client verified separately, see Section 60). The temporary diagnostic patch was reverted once the cause was confirmed.

**A second, unrelated bug found and fixed while testing this**: Craig hit `create-user`'s foreign-key check (rep code / branch code must be a real row in `sales_reps`/`branches` — schema/001) and got the raw Postgres constraint-violation message dumped to the screen ("Users won't understand this"). Two separate things were wrong here, both fixed:
- `create-user/index.ts` was returning `profileError.message` as-is for every insert failure — fine for the seat-limit trigger's own message (already written to be user-facing) but not for a raw FK violation. Added `friendlyProfileInsertError()`, translating the two named constraints (`profiles_client_id_rep_code_fkey`, `profiles_client_id_branch_code_fkey`) into plain language; everything else still passes through unchanged.
- Deeper bug, affecting all 4 functions: `supabase_flutter`'s `FunctionsClient.invoke()` doesn't return a normal `FunctionResponse` for any non-2xx status the way each repository method's own `if (response.status != 200) throw Exception(...)` implied — it throws `FunctionsHttpException` itself, with the parsed JSON body sitting in `.details`. That made every one of those status checks dead code, and every screen's `e.toString()` was showing Dart's raw `FunctionsHttpException(status: ..., details: {...}, reasonPhrase: )` dump instead of the message each function had gone to the trouble of writing — including, it turned out, the seat-limit trigger case, which had never actually surfaced its friendly message either despite the code's own comment assuming it did. New `lib/core/utils/edge_function_errors.dart` (`EdgeFunctionError` + `friendlyEdgeFunctionError`) unwraps `.details['error']` back out; `EdgeFunctionError.toString()` returns the message with no `"Exception: "` prefix, since these messages are already written to be shown verbatim. Wired into all 4 call sites: `createUser`/`deleteUser`/`requestUpgrade` (`settings_repository.dart`) and `createClient` (`platform_admin_repository.dart`).

**Files changed**: `supabase/functions/_shared/service_key.ts` (new), `supabase/functions/create-client/index.ts`, `create-user/index.ts`, `delete-user/index.ts`, `send-upgrade-request/index.ts` (all switched to `getServiceKey()`; `create-user` also gained `friendlyProfileInsertError`), `lib/core/utils/edge_function_errors.dart` (new), `lib/data/repositories/settings_repository.dart`, `lib/data/repositories/platform_admin_repository.dart` (all 4 Edge Function call sites wrapped). New test: `test/core/utils/edge_function_errors_test.dart`.

**Resolved (2026-09-02, same day)**: `RESEND_API_KEY` was missing entirely from the project's Edge Function secrets — confirmed absent via the Dashboard's Secrets list, unrelated to this key migration but caught by the same testing pass — since `send-upgrade-request` needs it to call Resend's API directly, a separate mechanism from the SMTP configuration Supabase Auth already uses for password-reset emails. Craig created a new Resend key (Sending-access only, restricted to the wyzesales.com domain — least privilege, since this key only ever needs to send) and added it as the `RESEND_API_KEY` secret. First test appeared to hang (Resend accepted the send with a 200 immediately, but delivery lagged) — worth noting for the future: sending FROM support@wyzesales.com TO that same address, via a third-party sender, is a known trigger for extra anti-spoofing scrutiny on providers like Google Workspace/Microsoft 365 even when SPF/DKIM pass cleanly, which can add delivery delay. It arrived shortly after; no further action needed unless it recurs.

With all 4 functions confirmed working against the new `wyzesales_edge` secret key (create-user, delete-user, create-client, and now send-upgrade-request), Craig deactivated and then fully deleted the legacy `service_role` key in the Dashboard. **This closes out the original leaked-key incident** — the credential found in the stale Desktop file at the start of this thread can no longer be used by anyone, regardless of where that file or any other copy of it ends up. Confirmed via a final live test: send-upgrade-request succeeded with the legacy key already deleted and no fallback available, which — since all 4 functions share the exact same `getServiceKey()` code path — stands as end-to-end confirmation for all four, not just the one directly retested.

**Verification**: every touched TypeScript file verified with `tsc --noEmit` against a hand-written Deno type stub (no Deno runtime in the authoring sandbox); every touched Dart file parses clean with the tree-sitter check. Confirmed live by Craig: create-user (including the rep-code FK friendly-error path), delete-user, create-client (see Section 60), and send-upgrade-request all working end-to-end against the new `wyzesales_edge` key — the last of those tested with the legacy key already deleted entirely.

## 60. clients_select never learned about platform admins — a second RLS gap, found while confirming create-client works (2026-09-02)

While confirming `create-client` (Section 59) actually works end-to-end post-GRANT, Craig created a genuinely new second client (Water Components SA has only ever had one client to date) — it was created correctly in the database, but never appeared in the Platform Admin screen's Clients list, with no error shown either.

**Root cause: `clients_select` (schema/006) was never updated for multi-tenancy.** It was written back when this app had exactly one client, and only ever allows seeing a client row whose `id` matches your OWN profile's `client_id`:

```sql
exists (select 1 from profiles p where p.id = auth.uid() and p.client_id = clients.id)
```

schema/008 introduced `is_platform_admin()` and correctly added it to every other cross-tenant table the same Admin screen reads or writes — `license`, `pricing_plan`, `profiles` — and even to `clients`' own `clients_platform_admin_update`/`_insert` policies. `clients_select` itself was simply never revisited in that pass, so a platform admin could only ever see their own client row, never any other tenant's. This was invisible for the entire life of the project so far because Water Components SA was the only client that existed — the policy was accidentally correct for exactly as long as there was only one row it could ever apply to, not because it was actually scoped right. Craig's test of `create-client` was the first time this app ever had a second tenant to reveal the gap.

**The fix**: `docs/schema/025_wyzesales_clients_select_platform_admin.sql` — the same `is_platform_admin()` OR clause every other cross-tenant policy here already uses. Dropped and recreated (Postgres has no `ALTER POLICY ... ADD CONDITION`) — the same approach schema/018 used to replace `customers_select` for the identical reason.

**Files changed**: `docs/schema/025_wyzesales_clients_select_platform_admin.sql` (new).

**Verification**: **Needs the same manual step as every schema change**: Craig running `025_wyzesales_clients_select_platform_admin.sql` in Supabase's SQL Editor. Worth testing afterwards: reload the Platform Admin screen's Clients tab and confirm the previously-invisible client now appears; confirm the existing (first) client is still visible and unaffected; confirm a non-platform-admin adminuser still sees only their own client on their own Settings > Company screen (unrelated `clients_adminuser_update`/schema/006's `clients_select` own-client branch, both untouched by this migration beyond the added OR).

## 61. Sales Analysis's Graph tab gains a Target overlay (2026-09-03)

Craig: "On the Sales Analysis chart we have the actual revenue plotted for each year. I would like to add an overlay light coloured bar chart with the Target for each filtered Dimension including Company. Possible??"

**The shape of the existing chart mattered here.** Sales Analysis' Graph tab (`TrendLineChart`, a dependency-free custom-painted chart — this project deliberately avoids third-party chart packages since there's no way to verify one compiles from this sandbox) plots fiscal **months** on the x-axis, with one **line per fiscal year** in the trailing history window (3–5 years, Settings > Company). It had no bar-drawing capability at all before this.

**Two design questions, resolved with Craig before building anything:**

1. **Bar granularity** (AskUserQuestion): one bar per month per year (matching the lines' own granularity) vs. one flat bar per fiscal year. Craig picked monthly bars — "but we are only doing this for the current fiscal year. No point in doing this for prior years agreed?" — narrowing it further to only the current (most recent, possibly still-partial) fiscal year's line, since a target overlay is only meaningful for the year actually being tracked toward a goal.

2. **What "Target" means when 2+ dimension filters are stacked at once** (e.g. Customer AND Item together, which Sales Analysis' global filter bar allows, unlike Budgets/Performance/Sales By's single dimension+entity model): targets are never entered for a specific combination of dimensions, only for one dimension at a time (Section 58 — targets don't reconcile across dimensions). Craig's own proposal, confirmed with a worked example using his own screenshotted numbers: "we know what the % contribution is for actual sales... apply this factor to the target as well." Concretely: `derived target = companyTarget × (filteredActual ÷ totalActual)`, computed per fiscal month. Walked through his exact numbers (Customer + Item filtered August actual R3,029.74, whole-company August actual R1,475,555.60, whole-company August Sales Budget R1,845,948) to confirm alignment before writing any code — the derived figure comes to ≈R3,790.

**Three cases, all keyed off how many of the 5 real (filterable) dimensions are currently active** (Year/Month/Document don't count — they narrow which period is summed, not which dimension's target applies):
- **0 active**: the real, entered Company target (dimension `'company'`, entity `'ALL'`).
- **Exactly 1 active**: that dimension+entity's own real entered target — same `v_dimension_performance`/`targetValue` (budget, falling back to `sales_forecast`) every other screen already uses.
- **2+ active**: no real target exists for that exact combination — derived via the formula above, one fiscal month at a time.

**New `core/utils/target_overlay.dart`** — pure logic pulled out of the screen itself specifically so it's independently unit-testable, the same reasoning `performance_rollup.dart`/`sales_coverage.dart` are standalone utility files rather than private State methods: `activeDimensionFilterCount`/`singleActiveDimensionFilter` (the "which case applies" decision) and `deriveProportionalTarget` (the formula itself, null-safe against a missing company target, a missing/zero whole-company actual, or a missing filtered actual — returning null rather than a misleading zero when nothing could be derived).

**`TrendLineChart` gained bar-drawing** (`targetBars`, `targetLabel`, `targetColor`, `targetTooltip`) — bars are drawn behind the series lines (after gridlines, before the hover guide) so the actual-revenue lines stay the primary, topmost layer; each bar spans from the zero line to its value (not from the chart's padded bottom), so a month with heavier credit notes than invoices still draws sensibly even when the chart's range dips negative; target values are folded into the same min/max scale calculation as the lines so a target well above or below the actual figures is never clipped. The hover/tap detail row and the legend both gained a Target entry alongside the existing per-year series entries — a rounded-rect swatch rather than a circle, so the legend itself signals "this one's a bar."

**Visually distinguishing a real target from a derived estimate** (Craig, earlier in the same conversation: a proportionally-derived number "changes what Target means" since it partly moves with the filtered slice's own performance, not just with an actually-changed goal — that distinction shouldn't be silently blurred): the estimated case gets a lower-alpha fill, an "Estimated Target (FYxxxx)" label instead of "Target (FYxxxx)", and a `Tooltip` on the legend entry explaining the derivation in plain language. Both real and derived bars are tinted from the current fiscal year's own line colour (`seriesColors.last`) at low alpha, rather than an unrelated colour, so the overlay visually reads as "this year's own target."

**Scoped to R Value only, not R Gross Profit** — Target is a Rand-revenue figure (Sales Budget), so showing it against the R Gross Profit toggle would compare two different units; the overlay (and its export column) simply doesn't appear when that toggle is selected. Also hidden entirely (both chart and export) when every derived/fetched value comes back null — a failed fetch degrades to "no overlay" rather than breaking the actual-revenue chart, which has nothing to do with Targets and worked fine long before this feature existed; genuinely nothing entered anywhere for the current combination behaves the same way, rather than showing an empty legend swatch with no visible bars behind it.

**Files changed**: `lib/core/utils/target_overlay.dart` (new), `lib/shared/widgets/trend_line_chart.dart` (`targetBars`/`targetLabel`/`targetColor`/`targetTooltip`, bar-drawing pass, legend/detail-row entries, min/max scale), `lib/features/sales_analysis/screens/sales_analysis_screen.dart` (new `_GraphData` bundling actual-revenue rows with the target overlay; `_loadTargetBars` implementing the 3-case decision; `_buildChart`/`_buildExportData` updated to source and gate the overlay). New test: `test/core/utils/target_overlay_test.dart` (dimension-count/single-dimension detection across 0/1/2+ active; `deriveProportionalTarget` against Craig's own worked example and every null/zero edge case).

**Verification**: every touched Dart file parses clean with the tree-sitter check. Worth testing on Craig's side: `flutter test --platform chrome`; opening Sales Analysis' Chart tab with no filters (Company target bars for the current fiscal year), with exactly one dimension filter active (that entity's real target), and with 2+ filters stacked at once (the derived estimate, with its distinct lighter styling, "Estimated" label, and tooltip); toggling to R Gross Profit and confirming the overlay disappears; exporting the chart in each of those states and confirming the Target/Estimated Target column matches what's on screen.

## 62. Target overlay testing feedback: colour, September's missing bar (twice), and a genuine RLS performance gap surfaced by testing as a non-admin login for the first time (2026-09-03)

Testing Section 61 immediately surfaced a string of real issues, each traced to its own root cause rather than patched blind.

**1. Colour.** Craig: "change the colour to a light blue but also make it slightly darker so it is easier to read." The bar was tinted from the current fiscal year's own line colour (`seriesColors.last`), which for the default 3-year history window is `AppColors.teal` — an amber, despite the name — not blue at all. Changed to a fixed `AppColors.info` (the app's one blue) at a higher, more opaque alpha (0.45 real / 0.30 estimated), independent of the series palette.

**2. September had no target bar at all.** Craig asked whether that was simply because there was no data yet for September. More precisely: `v_dimension_performance`/`fn_dimension_performance_filtered` (schema/021) are built by joining budget/forecast figures ONTO actual sales, so a fiscal month with zero actual rows never gets a row at all — even though a real September company target already existed (budget_figures/sales_forecast carry no fiscal_year column at all, one figure per fiscal_month label, reused every year). Two fixes landed together: (a) the test-bed seed script (schema/010) always spread a full month's transactions across ~27 random days regardless of how much of the CURRENT month had actually elapsed, so re-running it mid-month would backdate invoices into days that hadn't happened yet — fixed with proration (`v_is_current_month`/`v_days_elapsed`/`v_days_in_month`/`v_day_span`/`v_prorate_factor`), verified against a scratch Postgres loaded with the full schema chain (September landed with 7 invoices/1 quote/1 sales order, all dated the 1st-3rd only); (b) confirmed via that same scratch database.

**3. The same gap, one layer down.** Craig then filtered to a single customer with zero September sales: "Actual Revenue = 0 and Target does not show" — the identical `v_dimension_performance` actual-driven-join gap, just at the per-entity level rather than company-wide, and no amount of seed data can fix it generally (a customer genuinely having zero sales some month is normal, not a data gap). Rather than reworking the shared view/RPC (Performance Analysis already depends on it and works correctly with it today), `_loadTargetBars` was rewritten to read `budget_figures`/`sales_forecast` directly — a new `BudgetRepository.fetchForecastValues` alongside the existing `fetchBudget`, and a new pure `resolveTarget()` in `target_overlay.dart` mirroring schema/021's own `coalesce(nullif(budget_value, 0), forecast_value)` — for both the single-dimension and whole-company-target lookups, so the Target overlay no longer depends on actual sales existing at all. Verified against the scratch database: found real customers with a September budget/forecast figure and zero September actual sales, confirmed `v_dimension_performance` returned 0 rows for them, confirmed the direct read returned the real figure.

**4. Testing as a non-admin login for the first time since schema/018 surfaced two more things, one a real design question and one a real performance bug:**

- Logged on as Johan Botha (a plain `'user'`-level profile), the Sales Analysis default (no-filter) view showed no Target bar, but filtering to "Sales Person: Johan" worked. Root cause: schema/018 (2026-09-01) deliberately excludes the `'company'` dimension from what `'user'`/`'reguser'` levels can see in `budget_figures`/`sales_forecast` — "Item/Category/Company... have no rep or branch owner, so a User/RegUser sees nothing for them," Craig's own explicit call at the time. The Target overlay's fix in point 3 above reads those same two RLS-protected tables directly, so it correctly inherited that restriction — which is exactly why it took a non-admin login, only now being tested for the first time, to surface it. This same restriction turned out to also be silently breaking the Dashboard's "Revenue Target Attainment" KPI tile (built 2026-08-27/28, reading `budget_figures` dimension `'company'` directly even then) for every non-admin login since schema/018 shipped — a pre-existing regression nobody had caught, not something introduced today. Raised back to Craig as a genuine design question rather than silently resolved either way, since a company-wide aggregate arguably isn't "someone else's individual data" the way another rep's or customer's own figures would be (schema/018's stated rule: "A User can only see their data and Performance... no one else's") — pending his answer.

- The same login then hit `PostgrestException(message: canceling statement due to statement timeout, code: 57014, ...)` reloading the Dashboard, which renders its entire body (KPI row + pie charts) behind one `AsyncSection`, so a single failed query among the ~10 Dashboard fires concurrently blanks the whole page. Traced with EXPLAIN ANALYZE against the scratch database: schema/018's `'user'`/`'reguser'` helper functions filter `sales_document_facts` by `invoice_rep_code`/`warehouse_code`, columns schema/001 never indexed — and `sales_document_facts_select`'s own pre-existing `'user'` branch already runs a correlated subquery into `customers` for every row it evaluates, which since schema/018 now ALSO evaluates `customers_select`'s own (newly non-trivial) RLS. Net effect: every `'user'`/`'reguser'` read of `sales_document_facts` — nearly every screen, Dashboard worst of all with its ~10 concurrent queries — went from "one cheap profiles lookup per row" (adminuser: no subquery at all) to a per-row cascade into a second RLS-protected table, un-load-tested under a non-admin login until today. Confirmed via EXPLAIN ANALYZE that adding indexes flips the relevant lookups from a Seq Scan to an Index Only Scan; didn't by itself reproduce a multi-second timeout against the small local seed dataset (a full scan ran in ~35-70ms even unindexed), so this may not be the sole cause, but it's a genuine O(n)-per-query cost that scales with data volume and firing 10 of them at once against a real, possibly cold, shared Postgres instance is a materially less forgiving environment than one query at a time on a warm local one. New migration `026_wyzesales_sales_document_facts_rls_indexes.sql` (purely additive: `(client_id, invoice_rep_code)`, `(client_id, warehouse_code)`, `(client_id, account_code, invoice_rep_code)`) — worth applying regardless of whether it's the whole story.

**Files changed**: `lib/features/sales_analysis/screens/sales_analysis_screen.dart` (colour, `_fetchTargetByMonth`), `lib/data/repositories/budget_repository.dart` (`fetchForecastValues`), `lib/core/utils/target_overlay.dart` (`resolveTarget`), `test/core/utils/target_overlay_test.dart` (new cases), `docs/schema/010_wyzesales_seed_test_data.sql` (current-month proration), `docs/schema/026_wyzesales_sales_document_facts_rls_indexes.sql` (new).

**Verification**: every touched Dart file parses clean with the tree-sitter check; the seed script and the new index migration were both run end-to-end against a scratch Postgres loaded with the complete schema/001-026 chain (not just checked for syntax) — the seed script's current-month proration, the actual-vs-RLS-driven target gap (point 3), and the index migration's effect on the query plan were all directly observed there, not assumed. Still open at the time this section was first written: Craig's answer on whether the whole-company target should be visible to every access level, and whatever the Dashboard timeout looks like after the new indexes are applied (worth watching Supabase's own Logs/Query Performance if it recurs).

**Craig's first answer, same day: yes** — the whole-company target should be visible to every access level. `027_wyzesales_company_target_visible_to_all_levels.sql` added one unconditional `dimension = 'company'` branch to both `budget_figures_select` and `sales_forecast_select`, ahead of the existing level checks, so any profile on the same client would see a `dimension = 'company'` row regardless of level.

**Superseded the same day, before Craig had run it.** Walking through what "specific" should actually mean for the Dashboard surfaced a cleaner fix that makes 027 unnecessary — Craig: "The dashboard must be specific. i.e. User sees only their info. RegUsers sees their branch and Admin sees everything." Rather than widen RLS so a non-admin login can see the literal whole-company figure, the Dashboard's Revenue Target Attainment tile and Sales Analysis' default (0-filter) view now each ask for the VIEWER'S OWN scope — new `defaultTargetScope` (`core/utils/target_overlay.dart`) resolves an adminuser to company/ALL (unchanged), a reguser to branch/their own `branch_code`, and a user to sales_person/their own `rep_code`. Every one of those was already correctly permitted under schema/018's ORIGINAL policies with no widening needed at all — a reguser's own branch and a user's own rep code are exactly what those policies already grant, so this needed zero RLS changes once framed this way. `028_wyzesales_revert_company_target_carveout.sql` reverts 027 back to schema/018's exact original text — written as safe to run whether or not 027 was ever actually applied (`drop policy if exists` + `create policy` is idempotent either way), so it's the one migration that guarantees the correct end state regardless of which of 026/027 already ran. Craig can skip 027 entirely if he hasn't run it yet and go straight from 026 to 028.

The Dashboard fix needed no change to the ACTUAL side at all — `fetchConsolidatedSales` already reads an RLS-scoped view, so it was always naturally "my own visible revenue" for a User and "my branch's" for a RegUser; only the TARGET side was ever hardcoded to `dimension='company'` regardless of who was asking. Sales Analysis' single-dimension-filter case (`dimCount == 1`) needed no change either — the filter pickers themselves already only ever offer entities schema/018's RLS lets that login see. The one remaining gap, flagged rather than silently invented around: Sales Analysis' 2+-filter proportional-share estimate is inherently a whole-company calculation (this combination's share OF the whole company) with no "my own" analogue to substitute, so for a non-admin login it now resolves to null — `deriveProportionalTarget` already degrades from that gracefully (no estimated bar), same behavior as before 027 ever existed.

**Files changed**: `lib/core/utils/target_overlay.dart` (new `defaultTargetScope`), `lib/features/sales_analysis/screens/sales_analysis_screen.dart` (`_loadTargetBars`'s 0-filter case), `lib/features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart` (`_fetchWholeCompanyTarget`), `test/core/utils/target_overlay_test.dart` (new cases), `docs/schema/028_wyzesales_revert_company_target_carveout.sql` (new).

**Verification**: every touched Dart file parses clean with the tree-sitter check. `028` was run against the same scratch Postgres two ways — once layered on top of 027 (confirming it correctly reverts: a `'user'`-level profile's SELECT for `dimension='company'` goes back to zero rows) and once with 027 skipped entirely (confirming `028` applies cleanly on its own) — both landed in the same end state, and the `'user'`-level profile's own `sales_person` row (their own rep code) stayed fully visible throughout, confirming the revert only removed the unconditional company carve-out and touched nothing else.

## 63. Retested as Johan: target still R0, and a second, unrelated Dashboard bug — "where does five customers come from???" (2026-09-03)

Craig retested the Dashboard as Johan straight after the Section 62 fixes and sent two screenshots: Revenue Target Attainment still read "R 8,780 of R 0 target (MTD)" — the exact same symptom as before `defaultTargetScope` existed — and the Customer breakdown's MTD donut listed 5 customers (Free State Grain Co-op, Tshwane Water Services, Walk-in/Counter Sales, Garden Route Municipality, Karoo Bottling Co) when, in his words, "there are only two transactions in September so where does five customers come from???"

**Target still R0 — most likely an undeployed-fix problem, not a new code bug.** `_fetchWholeCompanyTarget` now calls `defaultTargetScope(ref.read(sessionProvider).value)`, which for a `'user'`-level profile resolves to `dimension: sales_person, entityCode: <their own rep_code>` — a combination schema/018's ORIGINAL policy already permits (RLS-wise this needed no change at all; see Section 62). Craig's own report already established that filtering explicitly to "Sales Person: Johan" DOES show a real target, which is the identical query this fix now runs by default — so the underlying data and RLS are known-good. The one thing this screenshot can't rule out from here: whether the app Craig was looking at had actually picked up the Section 62 commit yet. Flagged back to Craig rather than guessing further: confirm the Flutter app was rebuilt (not just `git pull`'d) and redeployed/reloaded after that commit, and that Johan's `profiles.rep_code` is actually populated and matches the `entity_code` on his `budget_figures`/`sales_forecast` row for September — if both check out and it's still R0, that's a real bug needing a fresh look, not this one.

**The 5-customer figure was a real, separate, pre-existing bug — found and fixed.** `_pickSlices` (the shared ranking logic behind every Dashboard dimension-breakdown pie, not just Customer) built its candidate list as the UNION of current-period and previous-period entity codes for every rank mode, then sorted and took the top 5. That union is deliberately correct for Diminishing 5/Growth 5 (an entity that dropped to zero this period is exactly what those modes exist to surface — see the code's own long-standing comment on this). But for Top 5/Bottom 5 it meant: whenever fewer than 5 entities were active in the CURRENT period, the remaining slots got padded with whichever PREVIOUS-period entities happened to sort first out of the union — entities with a real August number and literally zero September activity, shown in September's own breakdown at R0. This is the exact same bug class as the `current.isEmpty` fix already shipped 2026-09-01 (Craig, then: "we have no MTD data for September yet," 5 names each at R0) — just one notch less extreme: not "zero data for the whole period," but "some data, just fewer than 5 distinct entities," which that earlier fix didn't cover. It also explains why the pie's visible arcs plainly didn't match its own legend: only the entities with real current-period revenue drew a visible slice; the padded ones sat in the legend at 0% width.

**The fix**: pulled the ranking decision out of `_pickSlices` into a new, independently-testable `rankEntityCodes` (`core/utils/dimension_ranking.dart`, `DimensionRankMode` enum) — same reasoning `target_overlay.dart`/`sales_coverage.dart`/`performance_rollup.dart` are standalone utility files rather than private State methods. Top 5/Bottom 5 now only ever draw from the current period's own keys (so 2 real entities show exactly 2 slices, never padded to 5); Diminishing 5/Growth 5 keep the union, unchanged. `_pickSlices` itself now just resolves the active measure (R Value/Gross Profit) into plain `Map<String, num>`s and hands off to `rankEntityCodes`.

**Files changed**: `lib/core/utils/dimension_ranking.dart` (new — `DimensionRankMode`, `rankEntityCodes`), `lib/features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart` (`_pickSlices` rewritten to delegate; new `_toDimensionRankMode` mapping helper). New test: `test/core/utils/dimension_ranking_test.dart`, including Craig's own worked example (2 real current entities, 5 stale previous ones, confirming the result is exactly the 2 real ones) and one case per rank mode confirming Diminishing 5/Growth 5 still see dropped-to-zero/brand-new entities correctly.

**Verification**: every touched Dart file parses clean with the tree-sitter check (no local Flutter SDK in this sandbox to run the analyzer/test suite directly — same limitation noted throughout this engagement). Worth confirming on Craig's side: `flutter test --platform chrome` (covering the new `dimension_ranking_test.dart` alongside everything else), and reloading the Dashboard as Johan to confirm the Customer — MTD pie now shows only the customers with real September activity, with no R0 legend entries left over from August.

**Craig confirmed both on his own machine**: `flutter test --platform chrome` — "00:10 +95: All tests passed!" — and `flutter analyze` came back with only 6 pre-existing `info`-level style lints in older test files (`prefer_const_constructors`/`prefer_const_declarations`), nothing touched today. That ruled out a stale build as the explanation for the Customer breakdown fix (confirmed fixed on retest — see Section 64) and made the still-R0 target a real bug rather than an undeployed one.

## 64. Rep/User-level targets still read R0 after a confirmed fresh build — Dashboard never fell back to sales_forecast (2026-09-03)

Retesting as Johan with a confirmed-current build: the Customer — MTD pie now correctly showed only his 2 real September customers (Free State Grain Co-op, Tshwane Water Services) — Section 63's fix confirmed working. But Revenue Target Attainment still read "R 8,780 of R 0 target (MTD)," and Craig was clear the target itself is real: "Johan Botha does have a sales target not 0." With a fresh build ruled out, this was now a genuine bug to trace, not a deploy question.

**Root cause: `_fetchWholeCompanyTarget` and Rep Target Attainment never read `sales_forecast` at all — only `budget_figures`.** Section 62's `defaultTargetScope` fix correctly pointed the Dashboard's target lookup at Johan's own `sales_person`/his own `rep_code` — a combination schema/018's RLS already permitted, so that part was right. But the method that turns that scope into an actual number only ever called `BudgetRepository.fetchBudget` — it never called `fetchForecastValues`/ran `resolveTarget`, the budget-or-forecast fallback Section 62 already built and shipped for Sales Analysis's own Target overlay. So if Johan's September figure exists as a `sales_forecast` row rather than a manually-entered `budget_figures` one — schema/021's own definition of "target" is `coalesce(nullif(budget_value, 0), forecast_value)`, i.e. either should count — the Dashboard showed 0 while Sales Analysis, which already had the fallback, would show the real number for the exact same rep and month. The same gap existed in the KPI row's Rep Target Attainment tile (`repBudgetRows`, used the same budget-only way).

This explains the whole sequence without needing a data-setup theory: Johan's original "if I select Sales Person Johan then it works" (Section 62) was via `v_dimension_performance`/`fn_dimension_performance_filtered`, which already does the budget-or-forecast coalesce server-side (schema/021) — so it was never really about the `'company'` RLS carve-out proving the number was a real *budget* row, just that the FUNCTION doing the lookup already had the fallback the Dashboard's own hardcoded-to-`budget_figures` calls never gained.

**The fix**: `BudgetRepository` gained `fetchForecast({dimension, entityCode?})` — `fetchBudget`'s own optional-`entityCode` shape, returning full rows (new `SalesForecastFigure` model) rather than the flattened month-only map `fetchForecastValues` already returns for the single-entity case (kept as-is, now implemented on top of `fetchForecast`, so `sales_analysis_screen.dart` needed no changes at all). `_fetchWholeCompanyTarget` now fetches both `budget_figures` and `sales_forecast` for the resolved scope and merges them per fiscal month via `resolveTarget` — same as Sales Analysis's overlay. Rep Target Attainment does the same across every rep at once: budget and forecast collected into separate per-rep-per-month maps, then merged one (rep, month) pair at a time.

**Files changed**: `lib/data/models/sales_forecast_figure.dart` (new), `lib/data/repositories/budget_repository.dart` (new `fetchForecast`; `fetchForecastValues` now built on it, same signature/behavior), `lib/features/dashboard/screens/dashboard_screen.dart` (`_fetchWholeCompanyTarget` and Rep Target Attainment's map-building both gain the forecast merge).

**Verification**: every touched Dart file parses clean with the tree-sitter check. This one is more code-review-and-reasoning than independently unit-tested — the merge itself is a straightforward application of `resolveTarget`, which already has full unit coverage (`target_overlay_test.dart`), and it's inline in two private methods rather than pulled out to its own testable function (unlike Section 63's `rankEntityCodes`) since the scope was already large for one round. Worth Craig re-confirming on his side: `flutter test --platform chrome` (should still show 95+ passing, nothing in this change touches existing test-covered logic), and reloading the Dashboard as Johan to check whether the Revenue Target Attainment and Rep Target Attainment tiles now show his real target. If it's STILL R0 after this, the forecast-fallback theory is wrong and the next step is checking Johan's `profiles.rep_code` directly against the `entity_code` his real target is stored under in Supabase's Table Editor, since a rep-code mismatch is the other explanation that produces this exact symptom.
